# INV-03-01 — Combined architecture and implementation direction

## Current direction — build-first, October 25, 2026

The owner requests Rust-first incremental construction, with host-side FEX first, rather than waiting
for complete research or a final generator comparison. Develop the provisional
ARM64 execution ABI, FEX adapter and resident runtime together. Generated
computation may be opaque, but state roots, access intent, effect phases, recovery
and lifetime metadata must be explicit. Bring actual FEX output into the first
runnable increments alongside hand-written controls. Design configured N-vCPU
ownership throughout, add three/four-vCPU cases soon after two-vCPU bring-up,
and measure supported paths before full boot/devices. These October25 decisions
are consolidated in the implementation plan.

The prior hybrid preference and Track03 closure remain withdrawn. Existing
memory, event, code-lifetime, device and containment findings supply the runtime
requirements; their scope and platform premises remain unchanged. The bounded
extension-versus-hybrid comparison ended with comparative insufficiency. No new
codegen campaign or hybrid selection follows from this sequencing decision.

**Current deliverable:** I1–I4 and I4-V are complete within their declared finite
profiles on M4/macOS26.6.2, SDK26.4, Rust1.97.0. Final independent rebuilds pass
760 distinct healthy native cases, 25 intentional failures with separate-process
fresh controls, and 87 legacy jobs. A separate six-profile pinned-QEMU comparison
passes primary and fresh independent execution with exact full-RFLAGS checks.
Both independent reviews accept the finite integrated scope with no unresolved
substantive blocker. The [contract-to-test matrix](../reviews/INV-03-01-i4v-coverage.md)
and [findings thread](../reviews/INV-03-01-findings.md) record the source contracts,
producing artifacts, exact coverage and exclusions. The ABI remains provisional
outside tested profiles; Track03 remains open. Arbitrary guest programs, broad
ISA/FP support, architectural IRQ/exception delivery, boot, native-macOS FEX
compilation and workload performance remain unestablished.

**Setup fulfilled, October25:** `aarch64-unknown-none` is installed in the existing
stable ARM64 Rust toolchain alongside the native target. `tools/with-env` supplies
the durable sibling `/Users/liaozping/orca/natemu-raw` unless explicitly overridden;
the parent/global shell is unchanged. Five diagnostic/launcher tests pass, and
the full local prerequisite check exits0 with external-directory access. The
workspace sandbox alone reports the sibling root unwritable, so retained runs
need that access rather than switching to temporary storage. Pinned FEX inputs have now been reproduced durably and signing/VM admission
pass in I1. I2 reuses the pinned producer/toolchain through a bounded Linux
worker; native-macOS compiler integration remains unimplemented.

**Next action: native macOS compilation and VM refactoring (October 26 owner direction).**
The finite profiles do not establish a general compiler/runtime interface.
Follow the [restructure plan](../../vm-restructure.md): build the FEX compiler
subset natively while refactoring useful VM mechanisms and separating test-driven
orchestration. Connect small runnable slices early, developing their ABI and
recovery/system semantics together. The orchestrator owns overall quality and
coherence, rejects special-case fixes and reports justified incomplete capability.
Reassess affected old tests for correctness and applicability during this work;
there is no prior whole-suite audit or complete-ABI gate.

**I5 (as its execution dependencies become ready), boot and small workloads.** Select an exact stock kernel and
external initramfs with `/init` and small test programs; define one bounded
direct-boot slice using the accepted startup/machine contracts. Add the system
semantics reached by that path, progress from uniprocessor boot and userspace
outputs to SMP, and keep applicable I1–I4-V checks as regressions. Full ISA/device
coverage and a final generator comparison are not prerequisites to starting.
Kernel boot alone cannot establish application coverage or workload performance.

### Restructure progress (orchestrator log)

The main session orchestrates; agents implement; separate agents review. Each
row is one bounded assignment. Update the row on return; keep findings short and
link to retained artifacts or files rather than restating them.

Runs fixtures 64/0/0, seed 4242 44/0/0, reviewer 22/0/0, faults 434/0/0 at two block sizes. | Boot-ready | S4 (debug): TF single-step with the SDM's POPF/IRET/MOV SS/INT n/SYSRET rules; DR0–3 instruction, data and I/O breakpoints with DR6/DR7 and RF; data/I-O breakpoints on Arm EL0 watchpoints (a hit reruns the x86 instruction alone, its access under hardware single-step with watching off) plus software checks of every explicit resident access, BT* cut while armed; exception-frame stores count (Linux's cpu_entry_area exclusion as evidence), implicit descriptor/TSS/IDT accesses do not (accepted choice); non-canonical DRn never programmed; all debug state in one per-vCPU word. Reviews (two rounds): blocking fix — no instruction breakpoint inside a REP string; then an external-interrupt frame trap one instruction late and a non-canonical DRn halt, both fixed with regressions. run_s4 56/56 vs SDM, 14 cases, 13 negative controls red; no measurable cost on the common path. QEMU departures recorded. A tar slip by the author briefly reverted the working tree; restored and verified against the patch. | — | Oct 30. |
| Boot-ready | G1 audit and fix: the guest-state page sat at an x86-non-canonical address with bit 63 clear, which Linux's user-pointer check does not refuse, so an unprivileged process could make the kernel write there (prctl(PR_GET_TID_ADDRESS), time()) and corrupt the kernel's GS base, DF and FPU state. Moved to 0xffff_4000_0000_1000 in the Arm upper table (entry 128, vCPU-private tables, one EL0/EL1 RW no-execute leaf); the old alias removed. x87 slot index check tightened (debug code-shape check; hardware containment is the one-page slot). Shadow targets exclude the frame page and alias tables. Constraint recorded and tested: never advertise LA57 or LAM. Booted-guest probe: HEAD lets the kernel write (0 / the time), fixed gives -EFAULT as QEMU. Review: no blocking; no boot regression (release HEAD and fix both 1.34 s guest, 12.6 s wall). Remaining deviation: an access inside the page reaches the vCPU's own state; user writes are rewritten at the next resident entry. | — | Oct 30. |
| Boot-ready | I10: the 0x67 address-size prefix in 64-bit mode (FEX already truncated effective addresses; patch 0025 fixes REP strings on ESI/EDI/ECX, LOOP on ECX and XLAT's segment-base order; host-decoded forms and INVLPG truncate; `addr32 call` admitted); XLAT; exact #UD for encodings the profile does not advertise or the SDM reserves (XOP, EVEX, RTM, PadLock, 3DNow!, reserved VEX/MMX encodings, AMX/VNNI/GFNI, WAITPKG, INCSSP/SAVEPREVSSP/RSTORSSP), fetched first and length-limited like any instruction; the CET hint space runs as NOPs. INCSSP is #UD per its SDM page, correcting the plan's draft (QEMU and probably pre-CET hardware run it as LFENCE; unreachable without shadow stacks). Gathers and masked stores stay refused (owner). Review: no blocking; fetch-before-#UD, an INVLPG test that could not fail, the 15-byte limit and a stale comment fixed. 88/88 QEMU corpus, 296 SDM cases (11 red on the previous build), FEX ASM 1,864 pass. fexc series36. | — | Oct 30. |
| Boot-ready | Compile-cache eviction fix: the shared cache sat 290 bytes under its cap, so an open (which evicted only over the cap) never freed room and every new entry was refused; all 889 k entries were from older fexc builds and could never hit, and pack names carried no build identity. Pack names now carry 8 bytes of the identity; an open over 3/4 of the cap evicts to 3/4, other builds' packs first, then user, then kernel. Two unit tests red on HEAD. Boot on a copy of the full cache: 3.04 s → 1.17 s guest at the second boot (fresh private cache 1.17 s). Review: no blocking. | — | Nov 2. |

Owner decision (Nov 2): uncached RAM keeps x86 strong ordering — UC/UC- pages are never accessed natively; each access runs alone in a one-instruction step with full barriers. WC/WT/WP/WB native. Independent oracle (coherence_model) written without reading the code: 159,548 defined cases agree, 0 disagree. Reviews: no blocking; the store-buffering litmus cannot show the new barriers (the abort path's full-barrier swap and host step order UC accesses today), recorded in the plan as resting on those, barriers load-bearing if step blocks move into the resident. Shadow misses hackbench N=4 2.3 M → 0.25 M per vCPU. hackbench N=1/4 30.3/9.4 → 28.3/8.5 s; installer cache off N=1/4 8.65/6.67 → 8.05/6.44 s; build N=4 9.61 → 9.23 s; locks N=4 5.00 → 4.71 s. QEMU control flags +smep,+umip applied. Review: no blocking. | — | Nov 4. |
| Performance | Compile-ahead gate (natemu-raw/ahead-20261104-01/): 82-88% of compiles are direct successors of an earlier compiled block on its fetched pages; median lead 4 µs at depth 1, 67-85 µs up to depth 3; fexc 16 µs/compile on idle performance cores, 32 µs at utility QoS under load, 77 µs at background. Simulated 2-worker pool at depth 3: installer N=1 45% served (0.92 s of 2.06 s fexc), build N=1 43% (1.6 s); N=4 32-34%; fails at depth 1 or background QoS; 58-61% of ahead compiles unused. Gate (≥25%) passes at depth 2-3 with utility-QoS workers. Log fields added (off by default). | — | Nov 4. |
| Performance | Chunk 16 (compile-ahead pool): built (2 utility-QoS fexc workers, depth 3, newest-first stack, in-flight waits, every answer still matched, relocated and admitted at use; check-mode boots 0 mismatches), but not shipped: installer N=1/4 8.10/6.54 → 8.06/6.34 s, build N=1/4 20.01/9.28 → 20.24/9.34 s, +3-6 s CPU and +45-70 MB per run. Ahead answers served 31% and in-flight waits another 24% of installer compiles, but a wait costs about a compile and the vCPUs' own compiles slowed (17 → 21 µs) under the extra load; tuning made no difference. Patch kept in natemu-raw/ahead-20261104-02/ for a later ahead-of-use placement step with its own gate. | — | Nov 4. |
| Performance | Chunk 10 (in-VM reuse by physical page): the host marks templates; the resident keeps a per-vCPU hint table by frame, page offset/half and context, and on a miss copies a live template from the same physical page, relocating it with the encoder shared with the host relocator and re-checking every RIP-dependent admission outcome (canonical values, move re-encoding at the same word count, exits, link points; relocation tables must be ordered and non-overlapping); refusals fall back to the host. Check mode compares every copy with the host's admitted answer: 850 k copies per boot, 0 mismatches. Host block requests 1.16 M → 0.30 M. Installer cache off: N=1 10.71 → 8.57 s, N=4 10.98 → 9.37 s (QEMU MTTCG 6.3/4.9 s). Reviews (author's and orchestrator's containment review): no blocking; duplicate-relocation refusal added. | — | Nov 3. |
| Performance | Chunk 15 (CLI, STI and RDTSC inline in kernel-mode blocks; FEX patches 0031-0032, series54-inline-tsc): an IF byte and an event-pending byte in the guest-state page (IF read back only after kernel-mode blocks, so user code cannot change it), the TSC offset there, the counter readable at EL0; admission refuses STI right before a REP string or LOCK NEG. hackbench N=1/4 28.6/8.4 → 16.9/4.8 s, locks N=1 11.0 → 6.2 s, installer N=1 cache off 8.25 → 7.90 s. locks N=4 4.84 → 7.17 s: diagnosed and independently replicated as more mutex contention, as natively (futex calls per 1,000 rounds: native 146, natemu 18 → 100; a 1.5 µs wait per system call restores 21), no interrupt or TSC defect; the earlier native figure (3-6) counted only sleeping waits and is replaced. Remaining cost there is the system-call path. Review: one blocking defect (user-mode IF forgery) fixed with a test and negative control. | — | Nov 4. |
| Performance | Chunk 17 (system-call path): a Linux-shaped SYSCALL→SYSRET round trip measured at 0.69 µs, two resident entries carrying six system instructions; SWAPGS now runs inline at CPL 0 (FEX patch 0033, series55-swapgs; KERNEL_GS_BASE in an unused FEX frame field, written only at CPL 0 and read back only after CPL-0 blocks; admission allows the form and field accesses only in CPL-0 blocks). SYSCALL and SYSRET stay resident trips (they switch page tables and code context). hackbench N=1/4 16.3/5.0 → 13.8/4.5 s (QEMU 15.7/5.3), locks N=1/4 6.1/7.3 → 5.6/6.5 s, build and installer flat. Review: no blocking; compiler-free admission tests and saved negative-control outputs added; a pre-existing linker halt on a CPL-changing delivery at the link target recorded for the correctness pass. | — | Nov 4. |
| Performance | Chunk 18 (fresh breakdown, nothing built; natemu-raw/breakdown-20261104-01/): installer N=1 7.73 s = requests 2.80 s (fexc 2.04 s for 126.5 k mostly new compiles, 16.1 µs each, 41-48% of fexc busy time is per-request setup), resident 2.70 s, guest code 1.48 s, HVF exits 0.69 s. `build` N=1 19.88 s = guest code 7.08 s, resident 7.69 s led by dispatcher misses 2.54 s (15.8 M, 97.6% L1 conflicts in the 4,096-entry table, 95% separable by RIP bits 12-15) and page-fault region scans 1.53 s (18 µs per fault: a hashed 2 MiB-region bit sends almost every fault through 512 page chains; 2 of 5,840 scans found a 2 MiB-page block), plus 5 whole-cache flush refills. Estimated savings ranked: L1 enlargement ≤3.2 s build N=1, exact region test ~1.5 s, flush refills ~1.5 s, fexc setup + in-process FEX ~1.2-1.3 s on the installer; ahead-of-use placement, disk key and huge pages small or nil. Next: exact region test and a larger L1, measured first. | — | Nov 4. |
| Performance | Chunk 19 (resident lookups): (1) exact page-fault/INVLPG region test — up to 64 whole region numbers beside the 1,024-bit hash, so a fault scans a region only if it really holds large-page blocks (`build` N=1 fault time 1.60 → 0.13 s; cut set unchanged from chunk 8); (2) dispatcher L1 4,096 → 65,536 entries (RIP bits 0-15, `adrp` dispatcher), CR3 loads walk per-table lists of non-global entries. `build` N=1/4 19.7/9.1 → 14.4/7.0 s, hackbench N=4 4.4 → 3.9 s, installer N=1 7.7 → 7.1 s. `locks` N=4 6.53 → 7.00 s shipped as a measured exception (SYSCALL/SYSRET +9%, halts 6 k → 35 k; trigger not identified). Review: no blocking; listed-bit and 64/65 boundary mutations added; the 1 GiB-leaf cut gap is unreachable (no 1 GiB pages in the profile; revisit if added). | — | Nov 4. |
| Performance | Chunk 20 (fexc per-request setup; series56-setup-r1, fexc.cpp only, no FEX patch): the compile server keeps one parsed and configured setup per argument list less the RIP (6 distinct lists per boot), rebuilds Config only when values change, builds the feature table once, replies with one `writev`. Server CPU per request 12.4 → 8.1 µs; every reply byte-identical on recorded installer (125,799) and `build` (229,676) streams, also after review fixes. Installer N=1/4 7.16/6.00 → 6.53/5.51 s, `build` N=1/4 14.37/6.91 → 13.63/6.71 s, others flat. Review: no blocking; window set before context creation, reply fields cleared per request. In-process FEX estimated 0.6-0.7 s more; owner put it in the backlog (only when necessary or asked); a shared-memory channel keeping the process boundary is next. | — | Nov 4. |
| Performance | FEX patch series moved to the owner's fork (https://github.com/ArArgon/natemu-fex, branch `natemu` at 455698b5: upstream 48d71752 plus the 33 patches as commits authored by the owner); the submodule pins it and build.sh no longer applies patches. Same source tree (de278977), same compiler bytes (series57-fork `obj/fexc` SHA-256 a81a2f24…1fd8 = series56-setup-r1), a freshly recorded installer stream replays with 0 differences, compiler and cargo tests pass. | — | Nov 4. |
| Performance | Chunk 21 (shared-memory compile channel; fexc series58-channel-r1, fexc.cpp only): per compile server a 16 MiB `shm_open` mapping with one sequence word per direction; both sides spin briefly (host 30 µs, server 20 µs) then sleep with an `os_sync` wake; the host bounds-checks and copies every reply out, polls the server's stdout for death, keeps the deadline; the fd is inherited through `posix_spawn` (a `pre_exec` version forked the running VM and broke multi-vCPU stress cases; fixed). Round trip per compile 13.1-13.5 → 5.2-5.5 µs; replies byte-identical on recorded installer and `build` streams. Installer N=1/4 7.05/5.84 → 5.85/4.78 s (QEMU 6.2/4.9: natemu now ahead), `build` N=1/4 14.47/6.64 → 12.81/5.57 s, zstd N=4 −10%; hackbench/locks unchanged; CPU +1-3%. Review: no blocking; retryable wait errors, death check after every sleep, volatile copies, a layout magic word, and tests for both wake directions added. | — | Nov 4. |
| Performance | Chunk 22 (SYSCALL/SYSRET fast path in the resident): at a literal exit to a current SYSCALL/SYSRET decode with nothing pending (no debug state, TF, DR7, shadow, posted work, due timer or takeable event), the same transaction runs in place on the state record and goes straight to the block at IA32_LSTAR or the return RIP; any other exit declines after one probe. Round trip 0.67 → 0.57 µs; 99.9% of hackbench's 32 M SYSCALL/SYSRET take it. hackbench N=1/4 13.57/4.02 → 11.56/3.62 s, locks N=1 5.63 → 5.20 s, every other cell within 0.5%. 16 `sysfast` conformance cases and six variants (each red only on its expected cases); full suite green. Reviews: one blocking defect (a pre-commit page-table walk could kill the exiting block and halt) fixed with a non-walking probe; the decode-first reorder reviewed sound. | — | Nov 4. |
| Performance | Chunk 23 (code-cache capacity): at HEAD every whole-cache flush was the 64 MiB metadata arena filling, and after the first none found dead blocks (about nine in ten were other processes' code that the next process asks for again), so reclaiming would free nothing. The arena is doubled to 128 MiB and the block index to 2 Mi entries (512 Ki blocks); link records stay 256 Ki; identity map 872/1,024 MiB. `build` N=1 5 → 1 flush, 12.63 → 11.39 s; N=4 5.62 → 5.43 s; installer within spread; nothing slower. RSS +110-160 MiB where the cache fills. Review: no blocking; capacity tests reach both bounds. | — | Nov 5. |
| Performance | Chunk 24 (fresh breakdown at 4afd86a, nothing built; natemu-raw/breakdown-20261105-01/): new resident costs found: STI at a block's start still run by the resident (`locks` 6.2 M, `mem` 4.5 M), VSTMXCSR trips in `luajit` (4.0 M, ~1.1 s), page-fault shadow installs in `mem` (2.0 s). Gates: PCID with mitigations off ≤0.19 s anywhere (fails); with PTI the installer is 14.4 s against 5.5 s and per-root copies are estimated at up to 5.9 s (passes; belongs to the later PCID phase); bulk region compiles 0.1-0.65 s (marginal); idle-core compile-ahead ≤0.15 s (fails). Ranked next: inline STI at block start and STMXCSR/VSTMXCSR at CPL 3 (~1 s each on locks, mem, luajit), then bulk REP STOS/MOVS within a page. | — | Nov 5. |
| Performance | Chunk 25 (inline STI at a block's start; inline STMXCSR/VSTMXCSR; FEX ab62c7e06 on the fork's `natemu`, compiler series61-chunk25-r1): an STI that starts its block is admitted and marked; `find` runs it in the resident only when IF=0 with an event held, under a MOV SS/STI shadow or with RF set, and such a block is never linked to itself; STMXCSR stores the bank's MXCSR (written to FEX's state at every entry) ORed with the flags accumulated since. Generic: Linux's `local_irq_enable()` after a call (561 of 569 kernel STI sites); glibc libm's rounding-mode guard (36 VSTMXCSR sites in libm.so.6). `locks` N=1 5.25 → 3.81 s, `mem` N=1/4 9.65/3.10 → 8.49/2.77 s, `luajit` N=1/4 5.39/1.68 → 4.09/1.46 s; `locks` N=4 7.12 → 7.51 s accepted as the contention cell (guest SYSCALLs 30 → 34.6 M); others within 2.5%. Review: no blocking; hardening (same-RIP self-link rule, PC table must start at offset 0, explicit STMXCSR prefix guard) and tests for the untested paths added. | — | Nov 5. |
| Performance | Chunk 26 (REP STOS in bulk; MOV from CR2 inline; FEX 0430dc8a6 on the fork's `natemu`, compiler series64-chunk26-r1): a REP STOS in 64-bit addressing with DF=0 stores 16-byte pairs up to RDI's page end (≤256 per pass) when fast-string operation is enabled (IA32_MISC_ENABLE bit 0) and the memory is WB/WC (WT/WP/UC stay element by element), per SDM Vol.1 §7.3.9.3 and Vol.3A §11.2.4.1; MOV r64, CR2 at CPL 0 reads a guest-state field the resident writes at every entry. Generic: memset/`clear_page`-style fills; every page-fault handler's CR2 read. `mem` N=1/4 8.32/2.74 → 7.76/2.55 s; others within about 1%. Bulk MOVS/CMPS/SCAS and an IRET fast path measured under 3% and dropped. Review: no blocking; fixed two SDM gaps it found (fast-string enable and memory type not checked; FEX decoding 0f 20 with a memory operand) and added hardening and tests; full suite via run_suite.py 113/113. | — | Nov 5. |
| Integrity | An installer boot that missed the dialog (guest `main-menu` segfault at address 8) is the Debian installer's own race between its two `main-menu` copies on `/var/lib/dpkg/status`, not natemu: 0/75 boots with the default resident, 4/50 with the perf log on; widening the guest's rename gap makes QEMU fail identically (6/6, control 0/6). Recorded in the handoff's known issues. | — | Nov 5. |
| Integrity | The suite's `privilege` group (U1) had failed 250 cases since 1d2719c (perf chunk 14) unnoticed, because the per-chunk suite lists left it out; found by the new parallel runner (625df3c), which runs every group. Cause: a stale expectation, not a defect — the runner's fixed model expected one shadow reset per MOV to CR, but a CR3 load now keeps the global upper-half shadows (SDM Vol.3 4.10.4.1) and counts in `k1.kept_resets`; no fault, register, stop or retry check failed. The model now expects the kept resets exactly (red against the pre-1d2719c host). Independently reviewed. | — | Nov 5. |

**Phase scope (owner, Oct 27): this pass stops before kernel boot** — see the plan's
"Phase boundary". Progress within this phase (supersedes the whole-project table for
reporting; **corrected late Oct 27 to ~75%: slice 2 done (25%), slice 3 design + references ≈ 10% of its 15%; earlier 77–81% figures overstated**): compiler 10% (done), VM restructure 15% (~90%), slice 1 25% (done), slice 2
25% (0%), multi-vCPU on the general path 15% (0%), general path covers the old
specialized paths and they are retired 10% (0%) → **~48%**.

**QEMU baseline (S2p, Oct 27; `tools/bench`, results `natemu-raw/bench-qemu-20261027-01/main2`):**
qemu-system-x86_64 11.1.2 TCG native on the M4, bare-metal Multiboot stub → 64-bit CPL3,
4 KiB paging, steady state from N/2N/4N, 11 reps, every run validated against a C model.
ns per guest instruction (CV 1.3–2.3%): int 0.200, mem64 0.346, mem4096 0.398, call 0.744,
branchy 0.597, mixed 0.283. Targets in absolute terms: int ≤ 0.200, mem64 ≤ 0.242,
mem4096 ≤ 0.278 ns/insn. Linux-user reference (container, Debian QEMU 7.2): int 0.242,
mem64 0.253, call 0.438. Fairness notes: host not idle in one run (medians agree ±4%),
guest tables warm (best case for QEMU's TLB). Install: Homebrew qemu (~700 MB) also
upgraded six shared Homebrew packages.

**natemu run-time numbers (S2c, reviewed VALID; ns per guest instruction, natemu vs QEMU system TCG):** int 0.189 vs 0.200 (0.94×), mem64 0.102 vs 0.346 (0.30×), mem4096 0.99 vs 0.398 (2.5× slower; access pattern + VM TLB reach), call 0.156 vs 0.744 (0.21×), branchy 0.410 vs 0.597 (0.69×), mixed 0.080 vs 0.283 (0.28×). Both owner targets met as measured; no tuning done.

**S2g measurement and attribution (Oct 27, no tuning; tools/bench/README.md "Measurements and attribution"):** two back-to-back natemu/QEMU pairs: int 0.97×, mem64 0.29×, mem4096 2.59× slower, call 0.21×, branchy 0.70×, mixed 0.29× of QEMU system-TCG time. Executed words per guest insn in linked steady state: int 2.2, mem 3.5, mixed 3.1, branchy 2.8, call 6.0 (15 words of dispatcher per `ret`). Native class-removed runs: guards+splits ≈ 6% on mem64, unmeasurable on int/mixed; dispatcher ≈ 20% of call; VM adds nothing measurable once linked except mem4096. mem4096: slowdown follows the power-of-two stride (cache-set conflicts, also native), not page count — TLB-reach hypothesis not supported; a VM-only extra of ~2–4 ns/iter remains unexplained (PMU needs root).

Performance (Oct 27, static only): ~5.8 native words per guest instruction vs ~3.1
unpatched FEX (precision +0.7, guards +0.9, splits +0.5, never-executed stubs +0.5);
compile ~7 ms/block (process per block); admission ~2 µs/block. First run-time number
vs QEMU TCG comes from slice 2's first chunks.

and consolidation proposal (no code) → V1b host lifecycle/CPU-state/memory

### I4-V accepted coverage and boundaries

The final campaign preserves all 688 previously accepted native case definitions
and adds 72 seeded recovery/progress cases. Five serialized independent native
bundles contain 760 distinct healthy cases and 25 intentional failures. Their 986
executions include 176 repeated baselines and 25 fresh controls; repetitions do
not increase distinct coverage. A sixth fresh build preserves the exact 87-job
legacy matrix. All 1,308 supervised jobs pass and are reaped with quiescent process
groups. The independent audit verifies 203,545 native and 3,428 legacy checksum
entries, all five 811-input source snapshots and the 132 legacy source inputs,
producer pins and the common runtime ELF. Earlier diagnostic failures remain
retained and excluded from accepted coverage.

Strict QEMU primary `i4v-qemu-dev-20261026-03` and independent
`i4v-qemu-independent-20261026-02` bind the final native reports and current
comparator sources. Each passes six sequential scalar profiles with exact full
RFLAGS, 134 artifact checksums and exact container/volume cleanup. Independent
emitted-code/artifact audits reject 420 corrupted outputs per bundle. The earlier
masked-checker bundles remain historical; the final checker masks no result.
Ordering profiles expose native NZCV preservation, so their additional QEMU
canonical flags remain QEMU-only evidence. The comparator does not qualify
paging, fault retry, concurrent ordering, atomic indivisibility or native
publication; those rely on the separate native evidence.

| Required area | Accepted evidence | Explicit boundary / later work |
| --- | --- | --- |
| V1 kernel addresses and precise boundaries | Low/high fetch, load and stack; shared/mixed-permission aliases; precise stack prefixes and split-store profiles at N1/2/3/4, preserved in final-source rebuilds. | General high-address ISA/helper forms and arbitrary split/unaligned accesses remain outside these finite programs. |
| V2 paging-off and address formation | Segmented/A20/service fixtures, mixed paged/off owners and 60 concurrent protected32 address cases; no implicit walks/A/D on the off path. | General segmentation, dynamic A20 and production device backends remain later work; checked service does not establish unconstrained native ordering. |
| V3 mode/context transitions | Actual fetched legacy32, hidden-PAE and IA-32e transactions, rejected transitions, exact next fetch, ABA and retained peers; all declared family/N/actor cases reproduce. | Additional control/descriptor combinations and arbitrary asynchronous guest control programs remain unsupported or untested. |
| Memory ordering and atomicity | 174 litmus/XCHG/fence cases, live-dependency LFENCE capture and seeded post-exchange recovery; independent outcome model and emitted-code checks. | Finite observations do not prove universal ordering. Other widths, alignments, routes and broad FP/dependency programs remain unqualified. |
| Translation coherence | 60 parent/A/D/page-size cases plus earlier profiles, independent aliases, retained old routes and delayed all-owner acknowledgements. | Table/data backing recycling is exclusion-only; actual code clearing after final release is tested. |
| Recovery and lifetime | 72 seeded cases across stack, control, coherence, atomic and partial-helper families; exact effects, retained routes/samples, stale work, event eligibility and publication/reclamation argument. | Partial-helper evidence is not generated REP. Exact offline semantic/schedule replay is implemented; saved-ledger native playback is not. |
| Progress and containment | Healthy N1/2/3/4 scheduling, real FULL/readmission and bounded fairness; 25 declared failures, acquired-gate deadlines, owner teardown, owned-worker/container cleanup and separate-process fresh controls. | Containment N3 is untested. Failed held authority/references are preserved as failed VM state rather than fabricated repaired state. |
| Independent evidence and reuse | Pinned FEX/kvm-unit-tests adaptations, independent models, corrupted-output controls, six fresh native/legacy bundles and the strict QEMU primary/replica. | No whole upstream suite, native-x86 comparison, M2 parity, stock boot or performance result is claimed. |

Independent final requirement review is retained in
`i4v-final-scope-review-20261026-01`; the producing runs and earlier failures stay
immutable under the durable sibling raw root. The
[accepted matrix](../reviews/INV-03-01-i4v-coverage.md) provides the detailed
contract and synchronization/lifetime argument. I4-V completion qualifies its
tested profile for dependent I5 construction; it does not close Track03 or the
whole emulator project.

### I4-V active development — first V1 slice, October 25, 2026

The owner requests implementation of the full I4-V plan, continuous testing,
upstream reuse where appropriate and independent review before completion.
The goal includes V1/V2/V3 and the broader integrated validation matrix; the
first high-address result alone cannot complete it. Root owns runtime/host
integration, `/root/i4v_producer` adapts the pinned FEX exporter,
`/root/i4v_upstream` selects and adapts upstream cases with independent expected
semantics, and `/root/i4v_contract_review` independently reviews contracts/code.

First V1 slice begins October26 02:10UTC (October25 local): bound preparation,
implementation, retained execution, review and corrections to240 working wall
minutes /480 aggregate agent minutes. Use2 build jobs,8GiB build memory,16GiB new
retained artifacts, at most16MiB native backing,512MiB worker RSS,120-second
compiler-backed native cells and20-minute execution bundles; each exporter build
has a30-minute bound. Subsequent V2/V3/campaign slices declare their own bounds
when started. Stop this slice at its declared acceptance or bound, retaining any
partial result and remaining full-I4-V work. Do not call I4-V complete at a bound.

Initial runnable target: distinguish upper-canonical guest RIP/data/stack from
host input pointers and private native code/state addresses, publish and execute
actual FEX output, and take a real high-address shadow miss/retry using an owned
upper native translation root. Follow with permission/fetch/fault vectors,
low/high aliases and N=2/3/4 concurrent remap/source/retained-event/fairness cases.
The producer and oracle must bind original guest RIP, input bytes and output
metadata; a fixed low-RIP compilation or host-only walker result cannot satisfy
V1. Preserve full I1–I4 regressions and their admission limits.

At V1 start, the code had only TTBR0 bootstrap, a low-address fixed shadow slot,
fixed source-RIP/recovery fields and exact finite FEX exports. These were known
implementation gaps, not unanswered architecture questions. Extend explicit
address/context records and bounded native mapping lookup with their consumers;
never pass an unchecked upper address into the legacy fixed-slot arithmetic.
V2 paging-off32, V3 legal mode/control transitions, broader memory-ordering,
parent-table/alias/page-size, recovery and adversarial schedule coverage remain
required, unimplemented or untested until their own evidence is recorded.

**First V1 load checkpoint (Observed, October26 UTC):**
`i4v-address-dev-20261026-01` passes14 native load cells with fresh FEX compilation
at low and upper-canonical RIP. `i4v-live-dev-20261026-01` extends this to44 cells:
14 eager controls,14 resident-requested cells and16 malformed-reply controls.
The same adapted FEX `MemoryData.asm` load executes with high/low operands;
successful cases take a real cold native translation fault and retry through
separate lower/upper shadow leaves. Missing data, CPL3 permission denial,
NX/SMEP fetch denial and noncanonical/private operand rejection produce exact
guest state and faults. Full guest/native frames, memory, page-table A/no-D
effects and private backing exclusion are checked independently.

The live path begins with an empty code slot. Its resident request binds VM,
vCPU/incarnation, source/context generations, full guest RIP, mode, source bytes
and serial. A fresh pinned FEX context compiles the requested source; the resident
checks every reply word before publishing the body. Corrupting each of16 reply
words independently leaves code unpublished and guest effects uncommitted.
This tests immutable N=1 context identity, not concurrent generation changes.
The producer input buffer stays at host0x10000; logical guest RIP and native
code-cache addresses are distinct. Its final executable is pinned in
`tools/i4v_producer/producer.json`; exact upstream cases and license are retained
under `fixtures/i4v/`.

jobs after the shared bootstrap, bounded shadow-slot and host-exit changes.

N=1 load checkpoint. The regression and replica builds briefly overlapped;
each used two jobs, so an aggregate two-job limit was not maintained during that
interval. Serialize subsequent builds. These are correctness runs, not timing
or speedup evidence.

The new harness is `tools/run_i4v.py`; it snapshots producing sources, builds,
signs, compiles, supervises native cells and retains request/artifact associations.
All these are bounded development results. V1 stack/write/permission breadth,
cross-page effects and concurrent aliases remain incomplete; V2/V3 and the
broader I4-V campaign remain required. Four upstream cases are adapted, but only
the load has native runtime coverage at this checkpoint.

**V1 stack slice contract:** reuse the adapted FEX five-instruction immediate-push
case. Preserve its generated instructions in five native slots, with resident
returns only at guest-instruction boundaries. Guard the due access before each
slot, so a later #SS/#GP or paging fault preserves earlier pushes and their
updated guest RSP. Native exception retry must leave a failed preindexed store's
RSP unchanged and resume exactly that store. This is a semantic validation
profile, not a claim about unsegmented-block cost or coverage.

Within the existing V1 bound, bring up N=1 at high/low source RIP with high/low
stack addresses, two stack pages, RO/nonpresent denial after one committed push,
supervisor CR0.WP on/off, CPL3 permissions, parent permissions, NX/SMEP fetch,
first and later noncanonical stack accesses, and a noncanonical/missing DS load
after all three pushes. Retain both complete data pages and all native prefix
frames. The independent fixture model supplies committed-prefix expectations;
separate checks cover exact faults, table A/D effects, writeback and native
mapping state. Stream the fresh producer's52-byte body in three identified
chunks and publish no slot until all chunks validate. Corrupt each word of each
chunk, including late rejection, and require all slots to remain blank. Preserve
the44 load cells and I1–I4 regressions. Serialize build/reproduction jobs. Concurrent
stack/source/mapping interactions and the rest of I4-V remain required later.

Actual faults confirm that preindexed writes leave incoming RSP unchanged until
successful retry. Late stack #SS preserves the first push and RSP=HIGH_BASE;
late DS #GP preserves all three pushes and leaves CR2 unchanged. Late missing-page
and write-permission faults preserve the correct prefix and report exact RIP,
CR2 and error. Supervisor WP=0 allows the admitted RO writes, while CPL3 WP=0
still faults. Both complete physical data pages, all five native prefix frames,
full canonical guest state, page-table A/D and shadow mappings match independent
expectations. Corruption in any word of any reply chunk leaves all native slots
blank, including rejection after earlier chunks were accepted.

This validates the generated stack operations with resident returns inserted at

**Next V1 alias composition:** extend the existing I4 pressure coordinator at

prewarms its inactive data alias with the old route while the active alias stays
cold. Old execution, replacement execution and ordinary maintenance intervals
alternate low/high aliases. Every composed remap and subsequent maintenance
head must repair and locally invalidate both aliases before that executor
acknowledges; snapshots check the expected physical route and permissions,
not just equality of the two descriptors. Retain the existing delayed peer,
old route/code references, source generations, pending events, capacity and

Compile actual XOR/OR fragments at the selected full RIP with the pinned
I4-V producer. An explicit address-bound reply profile carries that RIP back
to the resident runtime; a same-identity reply naming the other RIP must reject
without consuming the pending request or publishing replacement code. Mutate
source through the opposite guest alias under exclusive coordinator ownership,
checking physical source identity, guest A/D and before/after bytes. This is a
protected runtime-fixture write through guest translation, not a claim about a
translated guest self-modifying instruction. The architectural source RIP stays
unchanged across physical-source invalidation.

Use the existing V1 budget and serialize retained runs/builds. Independently

**Mixed-mode extension (began05:12UTC):** within the existing V2 cumulative

retained store faults after a committed register prefix, paged remapping that

Use the existing resource caps and serialize retained build/native campaigns.
The mixed native cell permits one active live compiler per owner (at most four),

completed replies retain their slot until consumed and rejected.
Independent review and fresh replication precede acceptance; compile-only or

Keep EIP offset, CS-derived linear fetch, FEX operand offset, DS-derived linear
data and A20-masked GPA separate. Check each due instruction's complete fetch

These rules follow [Intel SDM Vol3A §§3.4/6.3/10.7](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/850978/253668-087-sdm-vol-3a.pdf); independent
review confirmed their applicability before native execution.

CS limit before/inside a later instruction and before first fetch, linear-base
wrap, and bounded device/hole service. Distinct owned physical pages back source
GPAs302000/202000 and data GPAs300000/200000/0; none aliases the poisoned table
fixture at GPAs1000–4fff. Data pages begin with a5/5a/3c; only the A20-selected source page contains the
valid program, while the inactive source page remains poisoned. Independent
review required this control so a wrong source backing pointer cannot pass merely
because both pages contain identical instruction bytes. Device GPA500000 retains a32-bit value;
hole GPA600000 discards writes and reads all ones. Each due memory instruction
starts with its native leaf absent. RAM faults repair and retry; serviced faults
perform just the registered memory subaccess and resume the remaining generated
instructions at native PC+4. Check all pages, every instruction prefix, exact
service counts/results and unchanged tables/CR2. Other GPAs reject unsupported,
never fabricating #PF.

Each request binds actual EIP, source bytes, context/profile generation, linear
fetch, source GPA, serial and instruction index. Publish only that instruction's
slot after validating its reply. Corrupt every reply word at the final instruction
and word0 at each of nine boundaries (25 native rejection controls); previous
committed code/state/memory remain intact while the rejected slot stays blank.
Independent oracles and a full retained primary/replica plus legacy regressions
remain required. Directly copy the stable captured native frame into its owner
record to remove a full-frame temporary from the common dispatcher; emitted
stack and gateway lifetimes must be independently rechecked with this change.
Mixed paged/off concurrency, wider address forms, V3 and remaining V1/broader
campaign obligations remain required after this finite slice.

### I4-V active development — control transitions, October 26, 2026 UTC

V3 begins05:37UTC with a240-working-wall-minute/480-aggregate-agent-minute

First execute actual fetched MOV CR0/CR3/CR4 instructions through the resident
semantic owner, between freshly compiled FEX MOV-immediate/store/load instructions.
Use flat protected32 with A20 enabled, first paging-off then legacy32 paging.
Check PG off/on/off, CR3 A/B/A and same-value reloads, WP changes, illegal control
values, encoding/privilege failures and first-fetch failure after a legal commit.
Earlier generated stores must survive a rejected control instruction; successful
controls and new context generations must survive a later fetch fault. Compare
canonical control state, exact fault location, table bytes/A-D effects, physical
memory, native state and compiler identities. Every context-changing operation
invalidates incompatible code/shadow admission before the next instruction.
MOV-CR arithmetic flags are architecturally undefined on success; this slice may
preserve them as an explicit deterministic policy, while faults preserve prior
flags. Include ignored ModR/M.mod and operand-size behavior in decoder checks.

The later required V3 paths remain hidden-PAE loads/reloads/failure lifetime,
IA-32e entry/exit and far transfers, and N=1/2/3/4 stale-work/ABA concurrency with
the existing retained-access/event/capacity/fairness rules. This initial executable
transaction is not a substitute for them. Independent review and fresh replication
are required before accepting the finite result; host-only state models supplement
actual guest instruction execution.

#### Implementation preparation

This is a concrete implementation dependency, not a new architecture unknown.
Pinned FEX `48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825`
[`OpcodeDispatcher/SecondaryTables.h::OpDispatch_TwoByteOpTable`](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher/SecondaryTables.h)
maps MOV CR*/WRMSR to
[`OpDispatchBuilder::PermissionRestrictedOp`](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher.cpp),
which emits an unconditional #GP break for its userspace runtime. Its
[`BaseTables.cpp::ENTRY_EA`](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/X86Tables/BaseTables.cpp)
has no immediate far-JMP handler; an indirect far-JMP handler exists, so this is
not a blanket absence of far transfers. These pinned source paths were inspected
locally by `/root/i4v_producer`; no new native behavior is claimed.

Implement admitted actual guest control instructions in the resident semantic
adapter, ending ordinary FEX blocks at those boundaries. Fetch/decode their bytes
at architectural RIP, take operands from canonical registers populated by real
generated prefixes, validate before control commit, invalidate the affected
context and redispatch through the next fetch. Reuse the paging-off and four-level
paths; add separate legacy32 and legacy-PAE walkers rather than applying four-level
rules to those modes. Claim12 and INV03-04 claim41 already own hidden-PDPTE
load/reload/failure state, including same-value CR3 reload and no implicit reload
on INVLPG/shadow eviction. Add legal/illegal control sequences, generated effects
before rejection and after successful commit, then N-vCPU stale-work/ABA schedules.
The first executable transaction has reached the reviewed checkpoint below;
V3 remains incomplete and the full I4-V requirements remain intact.

### Next I4-V slice — concurrent guest control transitions

Implementation begins October 26 at 07:39 UTC after the accepted long-mode checkpoint.
Bound preparation,
implementation, tests, review and corrections to 240 working wall minutes / 480
aggregate agent minutes. Retain two build jobs, 8 GiB build RSS, 512 MiB compiler
RSS, 16 MiB native backing, 16 GiB new retained artifacts, 120-second native cells
and 20-minute bundles. Serialize builds/native campaigns and preserve all 294
I4-V native cells plus 87 legacy jobs. Reaching the bound reports partial coverage;
it cannot complete this slice or I4-V without the required evidence.

Extend the control dispatcher under the existing coordinator, keeping the actual
guest program and state throughout. Primary families are legacy ABA (profile 0),
hidden-PAE ABA (21), IA-32e ABA (24) and direct IA-32e entry/exit (22), each at
N=1/2/3/4. PAE-to-IA-32e profile 23 remains a separate composition. Use one family
per VM: existing legacy and PAE fixtures assign different backing to overlapping
guest table addresses and cannot be combined unchanged. Stagger actual progress
within the family instead. Rotate the actor and delayed-owner roles at N>=2.
Add the bounded rejected-CR0 program (profile 3) at N=1/2/3/4: its earlier store
survives #GP, the rejected transaction changes no control/context state, and a
retained peer still completes correctly. This short rejection case supplements
the longer programs' maintenance coverage rather than claiming it by itself.

Before the actor commits a fetched control instruction, retain a peer's actual
native fault, prior committed prefix, admitted physical route and code reference.
At N>=3 also delay an admitted owner; at N=4 queue two faults to check FIFO service.
The actor's local commit changes only its controls/hidden roots/CS/context. Peer
state stays unchanged and its retained operation completes exactly once using
the admitted old route. Release those peers before the actor's next native fault
requires FIFO service, then check its first fetch/access under the new context.
N=1 supplies self-ABA/compiler controls without claiming a peer interleaving.

In the ABA families, reject the actor's old entry and actual delayed reply,
including a reply with only its serial rebound. A peer reply whose own source
and context remain current must remain admissible and execute its still-due
instruction exactly once. Use a separate cut from the retained native fault if
needed; never replay a committed store to demonstrate admission. Separately hold
a fresh final-instruction compile across protected shared-source mutation, reject
its old-source reply, compile the new bytes and execute an observable replacement.
Shared source/table mutation
closes admission and drains routes before writing, advances the relevant shared
generation and waits for required acknowledgements. Retain old code/map references
past invalidation and prove reclamation waits for release. This shared-mutation
cut is distinct from retaining an active peer access across a local control commit.

Keep canonical/control/hidden/segment state, frames, Visit pages and code private
per owner, with shared guest source/data/table backing. Bind owner, incarnation,
source generation, local context, full RIP and dynamic decode mode in each
request and entry guard. Reject malformed bindings without publication. Replace
CPU0-only code publication and fixed source/data addresses with owner-specific
backing/windows; route descriptor and table accesses through the same authority.
Derive each expected physical route from that owner's mode, CR3 and hidden roots;
shared PDPT writes do not reload peer hidden state. If descriptor/far-pointer
metadata occupies a remappable data page, initialize and check both physical
copies and record which GPA supplies every read.
These are implementation gaps, not unanswered architecture questions.

Compose bounded maintenance requests, duplicate/full queues, delayed acknowledgements,
pending events during compiler waits and bounded cancellation with the next real
instructions of these programs. Preserve the at-most-eight-head maintenance
batch and round-robin ordinary-work guarantee. Generated MOV progress can supply
an ordinary interval without claiming an extra memory access; no reset into a
disconnected post-transition fixture is allowed. Record ordered cuts, every
owner's state, physical effects, compiler artifacts, source/table bytes, event
packets and queue progress. Check trace capacity and emitted native stack bounds.
Independent semantic review and fresh replication remain required for acceptance.
Independent `/root/i4v_upstream` reviewed the proposed cuts and identified the
rejected-control supplement, separate peer-reply execution cut and metadata-route

### I4-V completed checkpoint — concurrent terminal stack faults and split stores

Start October 26 at 09:37 UTC after the accepted concurrent-control checkpoint.
Bound implementation, tests, review and corrections to 240 working wall minutes /
480 aggregate agent minutes, retaining the two-job/8-GiB build, 512-MiB compiler,

remaining V2 address forms or the broader memory-model/coherence/lifetime campaign.

Reuse the retained FEX `Primary_68.asm` adaptation and its five-instruction push
program; no new generated bytes are needed. Existing N1 fixtures already define
late #SS after one push and #GP after three pushes. Concurrent fixtures currently
keep individual accesses within one page; their terminal probes cover #PF, not
these late stack/address faults. Extend the existing post-pressure probe wave,
retaining prior memory effects and clearly reporting fixture RSP/RDX bindings.
Peers retain real second-push faults after their first push commits. The actor's
terminal packet and committed prefix must survive peer drain, protected mapping
changes, acknowledgements and reference release, without executing a fabricated
guest continuation after its fault. Require #SS(0) after one push and #GP(0) after
three pushes, preserving CR2 in both cases. A terminal actor services maintenance
using saved routing metadata without changing its faulted canonical state.
Keep the actor's first-write backing disjoint
from peer stacks, including CPU0's span when the actor uses the canonical boundary.

Add an eight-byte push spanning two pages, with success, second-page-not-present
and second-page-read-only outcomes. Claim33 of INV-03-04 already selects checks
of both fragments before the scalar store; claim30 separates access readiness
from dirty maintenance. Pin both physical fragments under one admitted context
and ownership lifetime, preserve preexisting A/D and lawful new A effects, and
defer new D effects until the whole access is ready. On the selected fault path, preserve
RSP and operand bytes, report the first-push RIP and CR2 at the second page, and
check #PF error2/3 for supervisor WP=1. This is our checked-service outcome, not
a universal claim about hardware decomposition of unaligned x86 stores. Report
the explicit service and completion continuation; do not claim execution of a
native cross-page store whose partial-fault effects have not been established.
Derive success values from the independently replayed prior memory state rather
than resetting the pressure fixture or assuming its original fill pattern.

The five outcomes at N=1/2/3/4 with every actor position give 50 cells. Include
both low/high source-address forms across that matrix and record each tested
combination explicitly: number #SS, #GP, split success, split-not-present and
split-read-only as profiles 1–5; use a high source RIP when `(profile + actor)`
is odd and a low source RIP otherwise. N1 is the no-peer control. Report the
captured before-wave prefix separately from actual final state; its unfinished
VM status must not be replaced with a fabricated successful termination.

`/root/i4v_producer` and `/root/i4v_upstream` identifies implementation gaps, not

### I4-V completed checkpoint — concurrent protected32 address formation

After the boundary checkpoint, extend V2's existing mixed-mode pressure/source
harness with instruction-boundary execution. This is implementation of answered
address/ownership contracts, not a prerequisite research phase. Bound the next
slice's implementation, tests, corrections and review to 240 working wall minutes
/480 aggregate agent minutes, retaining the current build/native/artifact limits.
Start October 26 at 10:38 UTC after committed checkpoint `1d74544`. Preserve all 394 current
native cells and the 87 legacy checks; split regression bundles if necessary to
retain the 20-minute per-bundle bound.

Reuse the adapted three-instruction source `89c1890b8b02` and replacement
`89c189138b02`, with actual FEX output at individual instruction boundaries.
Do not invent missing recovery offsets for the existing block export. The actor
runs protected32 with paging off; peers use admitted legacy32 paging and distinct
linear aliases to the same physical source/data. Rotate the actor at N1/2/3/4.
The current mixed harness's peers use IA-32e compatibility mode; the new peers
must use the existing legacy32 walker/control contract instead of relabeling
`mixed32::resolve` as legacy32.
Six profiles produce 60 cells:

| Profile | Required observable behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Subpage segment bases | CS base `0x300123`, EIP `0x2000`, DS base `0x200123`, and EBX `0x100040 + id*256` select source `0x302123` and data `0x300163 + id*256`. Separate offset, linear and physical addresses; service accesses when native/physical page offsets differ. |
| Fixed A20-off | The same bindings select source `0x202123` and data `0x200163 + id*256`. Only the selected source backing contains the program; the other is poisoned. A20 policy is fixed for this VM profile, not changed per CPU during execution. |
| Linear-base wrap | DS base `0xfff00080` plus EBX `0x100040 + id*256` produces linear address `0xc0 + id*256`; preserve the original offset and wrapped linear/GPA separately. |
| Address-size16 wrap | Adapt source to `89c1678908678b00`, with `[BX+SI]`, BX `0xfff0`, SI `0x30 + id*256`, and nonzero upper register halves. The address offset is `0x20 + id*256`; replacement `89c1678910678b00` stores EDX. Both accesses use `[BX+SI]`, so the load reads the preceding store's location, unlike the original `[EDX]` load. Retain fresh FEX emission/disassembly and recovery bindings. |
| Terminal DS boundary | After the ECX-copy instruction, a four-byte store at offset `0xfffffffe` produces selected #GP(0), preserving CR2 and memory. This maximum-limit policy is distinguished from mandatory ordinary finite-limit faults. |
| Terminal CS boundary | CS limit `EIP+2` permits the first two-byte instruction; fetching the following two-byte instruction produces #GP(0) at `EIP+2`, preserves CR2, and precedes any operand access. |

For the ordinary three-instruction profiles, set EDX to EBX+128 and retain
separate initialized load bytes. For address-size16, bind EBX to `0x1234fff0`,
ESI to `0x56780030 + id*256`, DS base to `0x200123`, and EDX to the distinct
replacement value `0x33440000 + id`. Explicitly admit the resulting
`0x200000` data backing; do not force it through another profile's GPA range.
For subpage/A20 peers, CS=DS=`0x400123` gives source linear `0x402123` and data
linear `0x500163 + id*256`. A legacy PD[1]/PT below 1 MiB can map these to raw
GPAs `0x302123`/`0x300163 + id*256`, making subsequent A20 masking observable.
The wrap profile may reuse the existing GPA-zero fixture; its alternate remap
backing must remain disjoint from page tables at GPAs `0x1000..0x5000`.

For the maximum effective segment limit `0xffffffff`, Intel permits
implementation-dependent fault behavior. Select deterministic #GP(0) for this
profile without claiming it is a universal hardware outcome. Ordinary finite
limit violations retain their required #GP/#SS behavior. This distinction follows
[Intel SDM087 Vol3A §6.3, pp.6-4–6-5](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/850978/253668-087-sdm-vol-3a.pdf)
and does not reopen the existing segment-before-translation contract.
Fixed A20-off applies to paged peers' resolved physical addresses as well as
the paging-off actor. Keep page-table backing below the affected address bit;
make the peer's final A20 masking observable through the selected source/data
backing instead of silently seeding an already-masked PTE target.

Retain real memory faults after a committed ECX-copy prefix and an actual old
compiler reply. Close admission, complete saved physical routes, then mutate
shared source and paged mappings. Paging-off accesses retain identity routing;
only paged activity may contribute walks or implicit A/D effects. Bind each
owner's compilation to EIP, segment-derived source, mode/context and source
generation; reject stale replies/entries and execute the replacement. Keep
pending events, delayed acknowledgements, retained code/maps, nine-head pressure
and ordinary-work fairness. Run terminal probes after a valid pressure prefix
with explicitly captured fixture bindings; preserve their packets while peers
drain. Compare full physical memory, tables, registers, prefix effects and native
service continuations with independent models, including corrupted-report tests.

Retain existing FEX `Primary_A0.asm`/`Primary_A2.asm` and mixed-fixture provenance;
the address-size override needs fresh producer inspection, not a speculative
architecture decision. General dynamic A20 changes, A20-boundary split accesses,
expand-down segments and concurrent device effects are outside this selected
profile. The broader required ordering, coherence, recovery/lifetime and progress
campaign remains unfinished; this slice cannot complete I4-V by itself.
Independent `/root/i4v_contract_review` accepted this bounded direction with
the explicit terminal-CS packet and VM-wide A20 requirements above; the result
below supplies the subsequently implemented producer and executable evidence.

### I4-V active development — native ordering and atomic recovery

Next implement the integrated pass's
memory-ordering/atomicity requirement. This is implementation and conformance
work against the existing [memory contracts](INV-03-04.md), not a new global
research gate or a demand for complete ISA coverage before boot. Start October26
11:56 UTC after committed address checkpoint `174e76e`; bound this slice to
240 working wall minutes/480 aggregate
agent minutes with the existing build, memory, artifact and execution limits.
Preserve all 454 native and 87 legacy checks, splitting bundles as needed.

Run complete actual FEX participant blocks on warmed ordinary-RAM mappings,
with no resident lock, HVC or added runtime barrier between ordinary guest accesses.
Trial setup/reset and result collection occur outside the participant block.
This distinction is necessary: the segmented-address service path deliberately
serializes memory and cannot test the native ordering contract. Use N1
instruction controls and rotate participant roles for the following profiles:

| Profile | Participants | Required outcome check |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Store buffering | 2 | Both reads observing zero is permitted, not required. |
| Message passing | 2 | A read of the published flag cannot be followed by stale data. |
| Load buffering | 2 | Both reads cannot observe the respective later stores. |
| Write/read causality | 3 | An observed publication chain cannot end with stale initial data. |
| Independent reads of independent writes | 4 | Readers cannot observe contradictory write orders. |
| Store buffering with MFENCE | 2 | Both reads observing zero is forbidden. |
| Store buffering with XCHG on separate per-participant guest-RAM dummy words | 2 | Each participant stores its shared location, exchanges its own dummy word, then reads the peer's location; both reads observing zero is forbidden. |

Retain fixed trial counts, seeds, outcome histograms, precise participant inputs
and failing records. Independently enumerate outcomes with a pinned architectural
x86 model or an explicitly bounded reference model checked against the retained
Intel ordering contract; LKMM is not a substitute. Absence of a forbidden outcome
in finite trials does not prove universal correctness. Inspect actual generated
memory order and the runtime's publication/TSO setup separately.

Start locked-operation integration with implicit XCHG32 on aligned ordinary
RAM. Unique per-owner tokens and returned values must form one serialization
chain through the final memory value. Exercise a native permission fault before
the exchange commits, then a separate later result-store fault after it commits;
the latter must resume the store without repeating the exchange. Reuse the
pinned FEX `Primary/Primary_87_Atomic32.asm` inputs where applicable and retain
the adaptation and actual opcode/continuation evidence. The pinned exporter
enables LSE, while claim33 selected exclusive loops: explicitly review the
emitted primitive and required ordering/admission before qualifying it, rather
than inheriting an exclusive-loop proof or changing the generator silently.
The unique-token chain establishes same-location atomicity and recovery, not
full ordering around XCHG. The separate-dummy litmus above and inspection of the
actual surrounding ordering sequence qualify that additional obligation; sharing
one dummy would introduce extra coherence ordering and weaken the test.

Validate explicit fences against claim40's local completion construction.
Pinned FEX's DMB-only fence bodies do not by themselves establish that whole
contract. Preserve actual generated prefixes/suffixes while adding the admitted
local completion boundary: prior architectural service effects complete, DSB SY
and ISB execute, and LFENCE consumes all live admitted result dependencies in
non-elidable private stores. Check GPR/vector/flag preservation and inspect the
dependency coverage; do not globally park all vCPUs for each fence.

This next slice leaves translation-coherence expansion, broader supported
recovery/lifetime interactions and seeded adversarial progress coverage required
by the matrix above. Keep unsupported locked families, widths, alignment/routes
and device/vector semantics explicit; do not silently infer their correctness
from an aligned XCHG32 result. Guest litmus outcomes also do not qualify native
host/compiler publication, cache maintenance or reclamation by themselves.
Independent `/root/i4v_upstream` reviewed this next-slice plan and found no
remaining substantive blocker after adding the separate-dummy XCHG ordering
case. Trial reset must await every participant; live LFENCE dependencies include
deferred flags and FP/status, not only preserved register values. The aligned
adaptation of the upstream XCHG tests does not establish their unaligned cases.

#### Ordering implementation and exploratory bring-up

The initial ABI/runtime/host path now compiles complete pinned FEX participant
blocks, warms three guest walks per owner, and runs 32 trials with reset/start/
completion gates outside every participant block. Full native state, two bounded
fault records, whole 8-KiB physical trial images, immutable source/table images,
actual compiler requests/replies and private published code are retained. Trial
and owner tokens distinguish prior work from the current exchange chain. The
new host/compiler paths preserve the earlier runners and their configured-owner
checks. Local validation passes 55 ABI tests, 30 host tests, 24 producer tests,
three new worker tests, 14 independent TSO-model tests and eight independent
report-checker methods covering all 77 configuration/rotation combinations.
These counts describe local preparation, not native acceptance.

Independent `/root/i4v_contract_review` found and closed overbroad LSE feature
admission: the finite path now accepts exactly Atomic encoding 2 and tests all
16 encodings. Its preliminary review accepts exploratory execution only; the
producing native binary and results still need final review and replication.
The raw fence controls do not qualify the full live-dependency LFENCE contract.

Exploratory `i4v-ordering-dev-20261026-01` completes the N1 XCHG native command.
The retained bundle correctly remains `oracle_failed` because its strict checker
was deliberately disabled pending actual-report inspection. Root and independent
`/root/i4v_upstream` separately audit the 32 exchanges, complete state/RAM images,
32 serialization chains and actual compiler/delivery association successfully.

### I4-V accepted checkpoint — translation coherence

Preparation starts October26 at13:25UTC while the ordering reviewer finishes
serialized regressions. Preserve that review's executable inputs; development
uses an isolated source copy until those runs are terminal. Raw run evidence
continues to use the durable raw root. Bound this slice's implementation,
execution, corrections and independent review to240 working wall minutes /
480 aggregate agent minutes. Retain two build jobs,8GiB build memory,512MiB
compiler workers,16MiB native backing,120-second cells,1,200-second bundles and
16GiB new artifacts. All builds, native runs and compiler campaigns remain
serialized across the project, including the preceding checkpoint's review.

**Behavior contract:** execute actual pinned FEX loads and stores while changing
the guest translation structures they depend on. A guest table write alone
does not require immediate replacement of a valid cached translation. After
the declared guest invalidation and every affected owner's acknowledgement,
new accesses must use the current mapping and permissions. An already admitted
operation may finish exactly once through its retained old physical route;
it must drain before mutation/reclamation. Clearing A/D bits without invalidation
may leave them clear on a cached access. After explicit invalidation, a fresh
read sets A without inventing D, and a subsequent first write establishes D.

Run six profiles at N=1/2/3/4, rotating the mutation actor through every owner
position:60 native cells. Reuse the existing pinned FEX protected32 load/store
instruction exports and actual host compiler, in legacy32, legacy PAE or
IA-32e64 as applicable. Four-level cases compile DWORD load/store instructions
with64-bit addressing, so high aliases really use full-width RBX;32-bit producer
artifacts cannot be relabeled as64-bit. New exact64-bit exports require fresh
producer checks before admission. This extends translation tests without
requiring new guest ISA coverage.

| Profile | Required mutations and observations |
| --- | --- |
| Four-level shared parent | Replace PT-A with PT-B, return to PT-A, then restrict parent write permission. Both tables retain source mapping but swap two data pages, so stale routes change observable values. |
| Legacy32 A/D clearing | Warm native writable mappings; clear leaf A/D without invalidation and execute cached writes; clear/invalidate, then establish fresh read A and first-write D. |
| Legacy PAE A/D clearing | Same history checks, preserving the loaded hidden PDPTE snapshot throughout lower-table mutation and invalidation. |
| Four-level A/D clearing | Same history checks, including distinct low/high native aliases of the same guest table entries. |
| Legacy PAE page sizes | Change4KiB mappings to a2MiB leaf, demote to4KiB, then install a read-only2MiB leaf. Warm and check at least two native4KiB subpages. |
| Four-level page sizes | Same promotion/demotion/permission sequence, covering affected low/high aliases as well as both subpages. |

Each profile must compose actual faulted retained work with admission closing,
delayed peer acknowledgements, source/code references and exact commit boundaries.
Capture the old physical route and permissions before parking a native retry.
Warm writable/dirty history for both subpages and every tested alias before
clearing A/D; a due cached store must not silently rewalk/repair that translation.
Promote an identity2MiB leaf to preserve source mapping. For PAE, change backing
PDPT bytes to a different valid root after loading the hidden snapshot, making an
accidental reload observable. Attempt and record rejected entry while the final
acknowledgement is withheld; N1 uses a finite self-delay control. Distinguish
checked reuse exclusion from any actual poisoning/reuse of retired backing.
An acknowledgement must follow invalidation of every affected native subpage and
alias. A large guest leaf does not become coherent by invalidating just one native
4KiB entry. New entry and reclamation stay forbidden until the declared drain and
acknowledgement conditions hold; retain trace evidence and corruption controls
for these conditions. Record guest-visible bytes, full native frames, table
reads/writes, native fault/resume sites, source/compiler identity and final
permissions separately from host-only reference expectations.

The new independent Python coherence model has14 passing preparation tests and
an independent read-only review. It models the admitted complete cached-route
or fresh-walk policy, not every architectural paging-structure-cache behavior.
Its eager-parent A/permission-before-leaf policy matches the selected walkers;
that exact fault-side-effect order is not a universal hardware requirement.
`invalidated=True` restricts an observation but does not erase stored history:
the native oracle must also invalidate its recorded cache before later replay.
The model alone proves neither native shootdown nor cross-owner lifetime safety.
Native integration, its oracle and independent reproduction are still required.
This slice does not remove the remaining broader recovery/lifetime, seeded
adversarial progress and final integrated review obligations.

Implementation now has a separate coherence ABI/runtime profile, existing host
compiler orchestration extended with exact owner/profile/mode binding, complete
resident table/fault/frame records and an independently authored replay oracle.

pages/links before native execution; the host now maps and tests all four pages

held-reference probe reaches the real reclamation guard with the program finished,
all acknowledgements complete and only the delayed actor's code pin retained;
it must reject and preserve code bytes before that final release.

### I4-V remaining pass — seeded schedules, recovery and containment

Read-only preparation begins October26 at14:05UTC while coherence reproduction
runs. This is the remaining integrated validation work, not an additional ISA
or whole-emulator conformance project. Bound preparation, implementation, tests,
corrections and independent review to360 working wall minutes /720 aggregate
agent minutes. Preserve the existing two-job/8GiB build,512MiB compiler worker,
16MiB native backing,120-second healthy cell,1,200-second bundle and16GiB new
artifact limits. Builds/native/compiler campaigns remain serialized; use an
isolated source copy while coherence inputs are frozen. Preserve all688 preceding
native cases and87 legacy jobs once the coherence checkpoint is accepted.

**Behavior contract:** vary actual scheduling at supported recovery, compilation,
mutation and event boundaries, then verify the same guest-visible effects and
progress guarantees. On a declared injected failure, contain the VM, preserve the
original cause and enough state to reproduce it, stop and join every owner,
reap owned compiler work and remove its containers before starting a fresh
control VM. A contained failure remains a failed native execution; only its
separate containment oracle passes. Healthy-phase errors are never relabeled as
intentional failures.
An aborted VM may retain a held coordinator lock or references: preserve that
failed state, stop/destroy every vCPU before freeing backing, and prove external
cleanup. Do not invent normal unlock/drain events or claim the abandoned
coordinator was repaired merely because a fresh VM succeeds.

| Campaign | Declared matrix | Required observation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Seeded healthy composition | Four families × N1/2/3/4 × four seeds =64 cells | Stack/source recovery; PAE/long-mode ABA; parent/page-size coherence; XCHG postcommit recovery composed with closing/remap. |
| Partial-service composition | N1/2/3/4 × two seeds =8 cells | Retain a changed-on-read helper value across a fault, competing maintenance and pending events; read/write exactly once. This is semantic-helper evidence, not generated REP coverage. |
| Intentional containment | Five failures × {N1 actor0; N2 actors0/1; N4 actors0/3} =25 cells | Worker exit, malformed reply, resident panic while holding authority, withheld acknowledgement deadline, explicit cancellation with deferred compilation and retained work. N3 containment remains untested by this matrix. |

Use seeds1,37,0x12345678 and0x9e3779b9. Rotate actors by seed ordinal/family,
not seed modulo N. Record the PRNG version, state and draw index, model-derived
eligible owner mask, chosen owner, phase and epoch for each scheduling decision.
The independent offline replay checks these exact decisions against the recorded
native actions and model-derived eligibility; timing delays alone do not supply
that evidence. Native reruns use the retained seed and producing inputs, but
cross-cut arrivals may differ. Feeding a saved decision ledger into native
execution is not implemented and is not claimed by this pass.
Inject waits outside coordinator locks. Do not add per-access serialization
inside ordinary ordering/atomic participant blocks. Preserve the existing
maintenance fairness bound and recheck event eligibility at actual handoff.

Separate readiness from capacity admission. At N≥2, fill a declared real
request pool with outstanding accepted identities, reject/backpressure a further
request without changing its identity/effects, drain and admit it unchanged.
An all-N admission barrier must not deadlock when the declared pool limit is
below N. Native maintenance-pool exhaustion is not applicable to the single
outstanding N1 request; existing separate compiler-slot capacity tests remain
distinct evidence. Do not fabricate capacity coverage from duplicate rejection.

The atomic case parks after the exchange committed but before its result store;
the token chain stays committed and only the retained store resumes before
mutation. Events cannot observe an intermediate canonical state. The partial
helper retains its sampled value, routes, completed count and next subaccess
through fault/cancellation, without rereading the device or duplicating a write.

Two implementation seams are already identified: host terminal handling must
cancel peers promptly for any unexpected non-success status, using explicit
per-profile allowed outcomes; and compiler/container ownership must be registered
before spawn/create with atomic metadata updates so interrupted logging cannot
orphan work. Killing the direct Python child alone is insufficient cleanup.
Every failure test proves its declared injection fired while the required peers,
references and deferred work existed. Preserve host worker/protocol causes as
host causes, not invented native faults. A3-second stall deadline and10-second
outer failure limit begin at the observed injection gate, excluding normal
compiler startup; cleanup itself remains bounded. The fresh control VM uses a
new nonce/state after all joins/reaping/owned cleanup; record process reuse too.

Independent plan review accepts this72+25 bounded campaign with the capacity,
actor-selection, exact-once and containment clarifications above. The final
I4-V exit still requires a contract-to-test matrix, explicit unimplemented/untested
cells, preserved regressions, independent reproduction and final findings review.

snapshots under the durable raw root, preserve failures and producing checksums,
and remain exploratory; architectural acceptance would need fresh reviewed
decision-evidence runs. Reuse existing retention/supervision helpers unchanged
where applicable. Stop at the demonstrated slice, a concrete blocking failure
or the total bound; do not substitute handwritten computation for FEX fit.
