### Adequacy and iteration

**Assessment (Inferred):** existing mechanism evidence and selected architectural
contracts are adequate to start incremental construction. They do not establish
full Linux correctness, complete FEX fit or end-to-end speed/economics. Independent
fresh review `/root/fresh_adequacy` checked the current twelve-question inventory
and supporting current summaries; `/root/abi_iteration_review` separately assessed
the ABI seam and iteration risks. The existing findings thread records their scope.

| Gap | Classification and next use |
| --- | --- |
| Native VM execution, paging, faults, publication and secondary startup | Accepted finite M4 mechanism foundation in Track01; implement the new runtime against its actual conditions rather than repeat generic feasibility research. |
| CPU profile, memory, event, code-lifetime, boot/device and containment protocols | Selected scoped contracts in the current inventory below; implementation/conformance work. A counterexample or changed platform premise reopens the affected choice. |
| Actual FEX recovery roots, native-site maps, helper/state transitions and system-semantic adaptation | I3 now checks actual35-root allocated maps, spills, aliased destinations and a live helper in finite profiles. Broader programs/system semantics remain integration uncertainty; expand producer and consumer together. |
| EL1t entry/capture profile on the actual host | I1–I4 demonstrate finite M4 entry, full capture/ERET, helper restoration, fault recovery and concurrent retained-state composition at N=1/2/3/4. Wider controls and arbitrary generated programs remain untested. |
| Queued faults, partial effects, remapping and reclamation in combined running code | I3 checks counted partial effects; I4 independently reproduces queued faults, remap/source retirement, pending events and bounded fairness under adverse N=1/2/3/4 schedules. I4-V now deepens interactions and conformance before dependent boot paths. |
| Pinned native fragments/toolchain inputs | Durably reproduced in I1; I2 independently rebuilds the runtime and actually invokes the retained compiler. Broader producer/exporter coverage remains integration work, not missing-input research. |
| Intel-specific MOV-SS/ICEBP, ENTER and legacy FOP details | Retained narrow unknowns in INV03-02; investigate when a supported behavior depends on them, not as a general build prerequisite. |
| Workloads, x87 tolerances, acceptable effort and material speedup threshold | Product choices, not discoveries a probe can make for the owner. Resolve before accepting the affected compatibility tradeoff or product-success claim. |
| Complete workload performance and maintenance economics | Genuinely unmeasured; integrate first, then measure representative work and actual transition/fallback frequencies. |

The [twelve-question inventory](#current-design-choices-and-evidence-ownership)
remains the detailed map. A missing implementation is not automatically a missing
answer. Consult an existing contract before opening a new research question.

**Implementation order:** follow the [implementation plan](../../implementation.md),
which owns the Rust/assembly/C++ split, pinned-fragment meaning, early live FEX
producer loop, recovery, N-vCPU checks, boot/initramfs and early measurements.
The ABI's layouts evolve with producer/runtime consumers while its behavioral
invariants remain explicit. Manual maps and a pair-only test cannot establish
an automated exporter or N-vCPU coverage.

## Fixed architecture and current conclusion

Host-side code generation plus a captive VM with an EL1 shim is the fixed
architecture. QEMU TCG, FEX, Box64 and other generators are component choices
inside it, not end-to-end alternatives. Native paging, exceptions, interrupts,
timers and TSO are the architectural mechanisms to exploit. Guest-visible x86
behavior must survive the adaptation; upstream runtime machinery is not assumed
portable. Semantic/helper reuse, the virtual CPU profile, devices and runtime
placement are separate choices.

- **Question:** Q-03-01 owns the combined answer to the twelve charter questions
  below, including their interactions. The four investigations are evidence work
  areas, not four substitutes for those twelve questions.
- **Current conclusion:** extended FEX with resident semantic helpers and the
  FEX+TCG hybrid are unresolved alternatives. Claim20's preference/closure is
  withdrawn after the bounded comparison ended with insufficiency. Selection is
  deferred while the provisional ABI and experimental runtime are established. Its hybrid
  construction and finite body measurements remain scoped candidate evidence.
- **Applicability:** modern unmodified x86-64 Linux, direct boot, SMP and the
  required headless devices on Apple Silicon/macOS 26. Inherited observations
  remain limited to their recorded M4 fixtures and conditions. Track 03 now has
  an independently replicated finite native containment-profile probe on that
  host, plus the scoped translated-body comparison in claim20. Neither is a
  deployed containment system, stock guest boot or end-to-end performance result.
- **Accepted question contracts:** questions1,3,4,6–12 retain their scoped
  contracts. Question2 selection and the final component binding of question5
  are reopened; accepted recovery constructions are not discarded.
- **Codegen and semantics:** [INV-03-02](INV-03-02.md) owns the matched candidate
  comparison. Claims43–48 supply scoped state-image and FP effect contracts
  and specific inherited corrections. Claims49/53 identify Bochs numerical APIs
  as candidate evidence; QEMU numerical paths remain alternatives under the
  owner's x87 tradeoff. Claims56–59 and claim20 retain candidate-specific bounded
  constructions; their final selection inference remains withdrawn.
  Claim50's event-delivery contract is accepted in root Return40. Earlier FEX diagnostic preparation failures are retained; later guarded primary/
  replica evidence and claim20 retain only their scoped comparative findings.
- **Machine, devices and containment:** [INV-03-03](INV-03-03.md) owns accepted
  clock/events/boot contracts and device subchoices. Claim70 and root Return49
  accept the role-specific containment/resource/teardown architecture, conditional
  on named OS/private-policy enforcement and trusted authority endpoints. This
  is not a deployed sandbox. Claims68/71 accept the Darwin/DNS binding and IPv6
  packet-layer gap; claims72–73 and root Returns51–52 accept the normalizer and complete
  required-device composition under the stated platform premises.
- **Memory and ordering:** [INV-03-04](INV-03-04.md) owns accepted memory/shadow
  and SMP ordering contracts (claims40–42), the named-M4 aligned128 operational
  premise (claim39), and scoped host-RAM/block completion/durability contracts
  (claims26–29). Finite native observations establish only their recorded scope,
  not private stage-2 attributes, universal hardware behavior or performance.
- **Combined interaction status:** claim20's hybrid composition remains a
  reviewed candidate construction. Final architecture selection/completion is
  unresolved after the comparison's insufficiency; the ABI-first milestone is next.
- **Inventory discipline:** accepted facts, conditional constructions and complete
  question decisions remain distinct. Historical pass-local next actions are
  superseded by each investigation's current summary. The dedicated
  [retrospective](../../retrospectives/track03-architecture-drift.md) records the
  systematic mistake; it is neither evidence nor a substitute for this inventory.

## Assumptions and breadth

The user requested completion of Track 03 and subsequently build-first iteration.
Research, planning and implementation now proceed per executable slice. Track completion means
independent acceptance of a coherent design, not producing enough documents or
claiming correctness of unwritten code. The frozen Track 01 program is not
modified. No archived history was searched.

**Inferred scope handling:** use the current charter and current Track 01
findings. Historical backlog rows referring to absent INV-00 records do not
establish current guest/workload or quantitative success definitions. Preserve
the broad product envelope; do not use a reduced fixture ISA, single-vCPU
execution, or one workload as a substitute. Concrete distribution/workload
selection and any compatibility tradeoff must be recorded before acceptance.
Q-00-02 owns compatibility changes; Q-04-01/05-01 own qualified costs and
measurement; Q-06-06 owns the full translator-adaptation budget before production
implementation. The bounded ABI experiment uses this investigation's own budget.

Breadth includes kernel and userspace, startup32, JIT and cross-modifying code,
FP/vector state, aliases and split accesses, MMIO/DMA, interrupt and wait
progress, resource pressure, reset/teardown and late replies. M2 discrepancies
are investigated if relied-on capabilities vary; M2 parity is not a gate.
Undocumented capabilities are admissible with evidence of relied-on behavior.
Licensing is a warning, not a rejection criterion. UEFI/Windows implications
remain analysis work in the machine assessment, not implementation scope.

### What the cleanup retains and what still needs work

The framing correction changes the **use of evidence**, not the fixed architecture.
The original 75-claim audit remains a historical snapshot:61 scoped entries were
retained, six inference/scope entries corrected and eight component-dependent
proof artifacts identified. Those counts are not today's completion percentage
and do not mean61 architectural decisions were accepted.

The matched codegen comparison and separate semantic-component boundary replace
the former QEMU-system-coverage preference. CPU, memory, recovery and lifetime
findings apply only under their recorded component premises; changing a generator
or walker reopens the affected proof, not automatically every investigation.
Device and machine findings retain their independent scope. The table above maps
these many-to-many contributions back to all twelve charter questions.

**Current next action:** the owner-agreed build-first progression at the top of this
record. The extension-versus-hybrid comparison is complete with insufficiency;
final codegen selection is deferred while FEX is integrated first, and claim20's closure remains historical. Preserve
cost/compatibility inputs for a later selection and synthesis decision.

## Owner correction — performance-led selection, September 20

The owner rejects the TCG/Bochs preference: performance decides component
selection, subject to the required guest behavior. Claims46/49/53 in INV-03-02
remain candidate-specific source and interface evidence; their component-selection
policy is superseded. The correction itself established no winner; the later
matched comparison and claim20 retain scoped evidence, with their selection
inference subsequently withdrawn as recorded below.
The owner additionally makes x87 numerical accuracy secondary when its cost is
too high. The comparison must include cheaper inherited numerical choices and
report their accuracy limits, workload effects and performance/adaptation costs;
full80 fidelity cannot by itself force Bochs selection or exclude a candidate.
Concrete tolerances and cost thresholds remain open under the
[scope clarification](../tracks/00-scope-and-success.md).
The prior ten-question contract inventory is not a readiness percentage; its
component-dependent premises and claim18 composition require reassessment when
candidate bindings change. Unaffected scoped findings remain usable.

The AMD/KVM run establishes its six recorded exploratory observations. It neither
establishes Intel behavior nor justifies requiring an Intel/Haswell machine for
project progress. Exact Intel fidelity has to earn its place through an explicit
product requirement or a consequential candidate distinction. Claims51/55 remain
unresolved within their Intel scope; they are not the project's next work gate.

### Goal continuation — one integrated closure package

The owner explicitly continued the full Track03 goal after the scoped
insufficiency was reported. Start00:22UTC September21 with a60-wall-minute /
150-aggregate-agent-minute ceiling, ending01:22UTC. This is an additional,
explicit allowance; prior122 minutes remain charged, not reset or relabelled.
Root owns integration and the numerical contract; one source author has20
agent-minutes for the actual recovery/cost construction and independent
method/findings/replication work shares at most60 additional agent-minutes.
All preparation, corrections and runs consume this package. No new downloads,
new hosts, full-ISA implementation or separate per-cell campaign is assigned.

Decision: whether a source-constructible FEX ordinary-code/queued-recovery design
with a concrete inherited FP fast/slow contract supports a conditional component
preference, rather than requiring unmodified upstream frontend semantics. Preserve
the full exposed guest contract by checked resident service for non-admitted native
lowerings. Native eligibility is not a guest-instruction exclusion. Quantify its
cost consequences; a merely simpler passing subset is not completion.

Use existing claim28's allocation-visible recovery uses and claim19's queue.
For FP, assess a conservative specialization of QEMU's inherited normal-number
native path, with all guest exceptions masked, nearest rounding and already sticky
inexact; retain checked status-capable service for other inputs/controls. This
must preserve guest sticky flags and destination commit, not waive SSE semantics.
A fixed control profile does not waive dynamic input/output checks. Compare against
QEMU both before and after sticky inexact enables its existing native path.
Any new probe reuses the retained fixtures, helper blob and supervision, with
explicit guard/cold-bridge additions and independent controls. At most one initial
exploration, one focused correction, and—only after reviewed method/binding
acceptance—one fresh primary and one independent replica. No repeated unchanged
fixture runs can establish the missing contract or speedup.

Stop when the package supports an independently accepted scoped component choice
and remaining architecture/breadth resolution, or at its total ceiling with the
specific unresolved requirement. Do not substitute a workload-wide performance
claim for a component inference, or promote TCG from greater source familiarity.

### Integrated package: reviewed finite comparison method

**Purpose:** settle the finite matched cost premise for a conditional component
choice after claim57's status-preserving native-path construction, not prove
whole-emulator speedup. The old exact/clear-status exploration is not promoted.
New guarded exploration `comparison-guarded-exploration-01` completed00:35UTC:
204 admission and294 timing outputs pass; longer clock sanity passes. This now
includes timed native control/input/result checks and FEX destination retention,
and gives actual QEMU helpers sticky-inexact status so their native path is
eligible. It motivates the following fresh decision-evidence run and replica.

**Frozen producing method:** `comparison.py --guarded-fp`, its captured dependent
sources, six retained x86 fixtures plus NOP, the previously sealed candidate
outputs and actual QEMU helper blob. No new emitter acquisition or opcode/input
expansion is part of the primary/replica. Retain complete code, inputs, build,
stdout/stderr, controls and failures under distinct identities. The replica
rebuilds exclusively from the primary's checksummed source files with
`--rebuild-from`; the driver must match the retained producer byte-for-byte.
The run manifest uses comparison schema v2 and purpose decision-evidence; outcome
still requires independent findings acceptance.

**Controls/oracle:**21 candidate/fixture combinations, two initial states. Each
positive FP state has resident full MXCSR0x1fa0; QEMU SSE status includes its
float_flag_inexact bit0x10 and the same MXCSR. Box64/FEX control and normal-input
checks occur before each body; normal-result checks and staged destination occur
for each arithmetic instruction. Thirty-six negative admission cases reject
clear-inexact control, NaN input, overflow trial results or negative SQRT, retaining
guest operands/state with no completed instruction and clearing speculative FPSR.
These reject the first arithmetic instruction only; they do not test a
later-instruction committed prefix, completed software fallback or guest exception
delivery. The independent oracle checks the full32KiB state and512-byte guest-data
observation window, owned/preserved native values, upper lanes, full status/control, actual counters,
branch outcomes/RIP, exact registered rejection PC and teardown accounting.
Root independently inspected the oracle extensions and compiled guard/rollback
instructions before exploration; the independent author checked the78 input images
and204/294 schedules. Its executable self-check includes guard-state/PC mutants.

**Sampling and execution:** admission runs1/2/3/64 positive iterations plus the
single-iteration negative controls. Only successful correctness admission permits
seven seeded randomized batches of65536/262144 iterations,294 timed samples total.
Retain every sample and use medians; exclude no outlier. Same M4/macOS26.6.2,
Clang/SDK,256KiB VM, hardware TSO and read-back FPCR6/AFP apply. No build worker
runs during timing. Native core placement is uncontrolled. Each phase has90 seconds,
180 total per invocation; the package allows exactly one fresh primary and one
independently rebuilt replica, with no retry until an ordering appears.

**Clock and inference:** compare median counter ticks per body for the same
candidate/fixture scope. CNTFRQ/host/guest frequency and long host-counter versus
mach_continuous_time checks must agree under the checker; otherwise retain
correctness and reject the timing inference. Nominal nanosecond conversion uses
the observed24MHz frequency, not a new platform-wide clock guarantee. Report both
iteration lengths, both runs and spread; a preference must retain its direction
across both lengths/runs and be larger than the observed instability. Do not call
near-equal results a winner. Short admission timing is not used. NOP measures a
different dependency/scheduling shape and is never subtracted. SQRT and the other
FP operations need not have the same ordering. This qualifies only these finite
relative costs under the recorded controls, not cold compilation, service frequency,
SMP, whole workload performance or every instruction's required adaptation.

**Decision use and stopping:** combine accepted finite costs with claim56's
recovery/native-eligibility construction, claim57's inherited FP contract and the
explicit unresolved cost/frequency terms. Do not count source familiarity as a
performance benefit. If that package cannot justify a conditional component choice,
name the remaining architectural/cost issue once rather than expand this method.
The source author cannot independently accept claim56; root owns that review.
The fresh method/producer reviewer is independent of root and the oracle author.
All preparation, reviews and replica consume the original150-agent/60-wall closure
package; no timer restarts for this method.

**Queued-fault decision,23:43UTC September20:** claim19 is independently accepted
as a conditional architectural replacement for universal instruction-commit polling.
The source/protocol pass closes within7 wall minutes and24 aggregate allowance:
charge10 author/correction minutes and8 independent review minutes,18 total.
It reuses PAUSED/DRAIN_SERVICE, preserves admitted-route obligations, and adds

### Why these results do not yet select a component

**Source-backed and independently reviewed after execution:** at the pinned QEMU
revision, `fpu/softfloat.c:233` `can_use_fpu` requires an already set
`float_flag_inexact` and round-to-nearest. The retained initial SSE status is zero,
and the exact FP inputs never set inexact. Thus this run measures QEMU's software
path throughout. A status-set case can take an existing native fast path; its cost
is not measured here. The table cannot characterize QEMU's general FP cost or
establish a same-contract native-FP comparison.

At the pinned FEX revision, `OpcodeDispatcher/Vector.cpp:3164` `GetMXCSR` and
`:3293` `RestoreMXCSRState` both mask with0xFFC0, discarding sticky exception
bits0–5. Exact numerical outputs do not establish SSE status/exception behavior.
The owner's x87 accuracy concession does not waive that SSE contract. A native
status/commit adaptation or inherited checked service is possible, but its
ordinary-path cost is not present in the measured leaf bodies.

The source comparison in
[consolidated-bindings.md](../../../experiments/track03/codegen-comparison/consolidated-bindings.md)
identifies the concrete recovery changes: FEX's current REP STOS/MOVS does not
support precise partial faults; Box64's batched postindexed REP needs subaccess
progress and bounded stops; TCG has reusable REP progress/exit machinery. A common
bounded resident REP service is a constructible alternative, not a TCG-generator
mandate. FEX's allocation last-use handling also requires recovery uses to keep
needed old values live through the fault/commit site. A saved frame cannot recover
an overwritten value. Additional liveness may add spills or constrain scheduling;
these costs are unbounded by the finite emitted examples, even though the
claim28 recovery interface and claim19 queue construction already exist.

Root inspected the cited FEX allocation/REP/status, Box64 REP/recovery and QEMU
REP/SoftFloat paths. `/root/direction_review` independently accepts this scoped
insufficiency and the stopping decision in the existing findings thread. These
are blockers to an unsupported selection inference, not design kills or demands
to implement the full ISA during research. The proposed fresh primary/replica of
the unchanged exact fixtures is withdrawn as unable to settle the decision.

### What can change the decision

| Uncertainty | Comparable observation | Decision consequence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Ordinary generated work | Full emitted blocks, entry/tail, memory ordering, live integer/flag/vector state, guards and helper transitions for identical inputs/native capabilities | Changes expected steady-state cost; a shorter printed instruction list alone cannot select a generator |
| Recovery and captive adaptation overhead | Actual output plus the minimum justified state/materialization, relocation and event-boundary changes; distinguish measured instructions from unimplemented changes | A candidate-specific unavoidable cost or inability to preserve the required interface can change selection; an absent diagnostic export is unknown, not incompatibility |
| Compilation and reuse | Repeated compilation time on the same worker, bytes generated and explicit cache/reset policy; execution-time crossover across reuse counts | Distinguishes cold/JIT-heavy and repeatedly executed code; Linux-worker timings do not become macOS host-compiler measurements |
| Numerical helpers | Same operations/inputs/control settings, runtime and reported numerical differences for cheaper inherited and higher-accuracy paths | Selects x87 cost/accuracy policy independently of the generator; no full80 gate or automatic Bochs preference |

Candidate scope is TCG, FEX and Box64 using their existing inspected revisions.
Keep frontend/helper provenance explicit. Use identical guest inputs and behavior
requirements, while allowing each candidate its own registers, flags and resident
ABI. Hold the native feature target constant for paired comparisons; an
Apple-specific optional variant is a separately labelled result. Upstream Linux
signal/softMMU/dispatch costs are not automatically captive-runtime costs.

upper bound over the entire interval, below120; actual effort was lower. This
includes preparation, builds, one focused correction, runs, reviews and analysis.
Three fresh sequential two-job/8GiB export builds used retained sources with no
new source downloads. Total accumulated raw storage is5.4GiB, below12GiB. Execution
phases finished within their90-second limits; every failure/clock rejection is

with the selected guarded-EL1 memory check and an explicit resident boundary.
Compare maintained register residency with forced state materialization/helper
round trips at K=1,4,16,64 bodies per boundary. Derive candidate-specific register,
deferred-flag/vector and helper bindings from their actual interfaces; handwritten

or an inability to construct a valid comparison within the allowance.

The measured FEX/TCG live-body gap is approximately0.84ns/body. An extra FEX cost
of0.84*K ns per boundary could erase that gap, so this next measurement can change

about1.31ns/body. These are fixture-based break-even calculations, not assumed

### Guard/resident-boundary campaign — completed execution

**Question and stop:** determine whether required per-access admission and a
periodic resident state consumer can reverse the cached-body ordering. Stop at
one validated sensitivity matrix or an insufficiency within the shared60-minute
allowance. Source/binding preparation uses the existing accepted claims15/18,
not another rare-instruction audit. Two preparatory reviewers share10 agent-minutes;
remaining producer/oracle/run/review work consumes the same allowance. Allocation
may move between preparation and analysis without increasing that total.

Reuse the exact nine hardware-profile live bodies. Insert the six-instruction
canonical-span predicate immediately before each8-byte scalar and16-byte vector
access; the predicate preserves NZCV and reserves two scratch registers. Rejection
reaches a typed probe stop without issuing that access, preserving earlier effects.
The ordinary canonical-unmapped control still reaches the native fault handler.
Test low noncanonical pointers, low-half span crossings and unsigned-wrap spans
at both scalar and vector sites. The existing fixture's low-address runtime
mappings are retained for this cost probe: **this is not a proof of the selected
private-pool layout or guest isolation**. Mapping/permission/MMIO semantics remain
separate; no hostile guest or unrestricted emitter executes.

Compare ten configurations for each body: unguarded/no boundary, guarded/no
boundary, and guarded boundaries at K=1,4,16,64 for each of two explicit synthetic
adapter contracts. A read-only resident view preserves the named guest roots and
decodes deferred flags while reading each candidate's actual state locations.
A canonical80-byte record publishes the same five GPRs, six arithmetic flags and
two vectors, then a declared volatile-clobbering helper consumes it and required
cached roots reload. The second is snapshot/ABI pressure, not a mandatory spill
at every block. Original deferred flag recipes are preserved because this helper
does not mutate guest state. Both helpers compute the same rotate/XOR semantic
digest and boundary count; individual live state and the last record are checked.
Handwritten guards/adapters are identified separately from unchanged emitter bytes.

The90-case matrix has612 correctness/rejection cases and1260 long samples (seven
randomized paired batches at65536/262144 bodies). A dedicated preserved x29 counts
K bodies without changing NZCV; native x30 remains the loop counter across helper
calls. The oracle independently checks floor(N/K), digest recurrence, live outputs,
partial fault/reject effects, actual access sites and source/byte admission before
execution. At most two fresh executions (initial and one focused correction),
90 seconds per phase/180 total per invocation. Snapshot RAM remains256KiB; code
limit expands to64KiB with page tables moved out of that code interval. No source
downloads, new runtime framework or change to frozen Track01 code is needed.

**Retained outcome:** `boundary-execution-01` under
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-comparison-raw` completed in12 seconds, with its
producing snapshot, schedules, disassembly, manifest, outputs and checksums.
One build-only preparation preceded it; no corrected VM rerun was needed. The
independent oracle caught a K-frequency defect during synthetic preparation;
a dedicated countdown corrected it before execution. All1872 output checks pass.
Long-sample host-counter/mach ratios are1.000000–1.001744; short admission timing
fails the overhead-sensitive sanity threshold and is not used for costs. Clock
sanity is not timing qualification. Host and guest counters report24MHz.

Exploratory median ns/body, including the common loop, at262144 iterations across
seven randomized batches; ranges below span the three live fixtures:

| Configuration | Box64 | FEX | TCG |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Unguarded, no boundary | 1.739–1.754 | 0.672–0.730 | 1.490–1.517 |
| Guarded, no boundary | 2.610–2.636 | 0.678–0.738 | 1.519–1.529 |
| Preserved-root helper, K=1 | 2.989–3.090 | 2.465–2.479 | 3.131–3.380 |

**Decision consequence:** boundary contract and frequency can reverse the body
ordering. This does not establish that full materialization is required every
block; claim15 permits resident state. FEX remains cheaper in the preserved-root
matrix and in the measured canonical boundary cases atK>=4. The synthetic dense
case disadvantages its volatile cached roots; Box64/TCG retain resident state.
That difference belongs to the declared ABI, not to an inevitable generator cost.
Nonmonotone Box64 medians and some paired duration ratios far from4 also prevent
interpreting individual differences as additive guard/helper costs. No warm-loop
result selects a whole generator or establishes actual workload frequencies.
The campaign's sensitivity question is answered; broader selection evidence is
insufficient, and repeated sampling of these same bodies would not repair it.

### Required-boundary comparison proposal — superseded by claim19

**Disposition:** the owner’s queued-fault challenge led to accepted claim19 below.
The proposed every-commit checkpoint experiment is not the current assignment;
its source/cut-point evidence remains useful where an actual cold path needs it.

**Preparation bound:**10 aggregate agent-minutes from23:33UTC, including root
construction and independent source/direction review. This is a proposal, not an
execution result or automatic extension of the closed boundary campaign.

**Decision-changing omission found:** claim14 requires a real stop checkpoint at
every guest-instruction commit and REP iteration, and claims14/02-37 require
protected admission and pin handover at every external object entry. The existing
body/guard/digest probes omit those actual normal paths. Claim02-37 explicitly
preserves qualified predecessor registers and says dispatch is not a universal
canonical-state boundary; claim15 likewise requires preservation only of affected
roots. Therefore forced dense publication at every body is not the selected
architecture's normal ABI. This follows from the accepted contracts, not a new
performance assumption. Measuring another arbitrary semantic digest would not
answer the omission.

**Concrete proposed comparison:** retain the nine emitted hardware-TSO live
bodies and candidate-native roots, with existing per-access guards. Add actual
instruction-commit pending checks and a permanent external-entry admission
service. The successful service preserves those roots, validates the declared
fixture's complete context/object/source identities under the coordinator, and
transfers its execution record/pin before entry. Its bounded uncontended mutex
path is included, not replaced by an unchecked pointer or synthetic state digest.
Use two retained immutable objects linked through runtime-owned data cells;
compare1/4/16/64 live bodies per object, always checking every guest commit.
Internal repetition does not count as a new external entry. Report these as
controlled object sizes, never measured workload frequencies or upstream guest
branch lowering. The external transfer, guards and service are explicit adapters.

Reject stale context, source or object incarnation before new guest work; preserve
the prior pin and guest state on rejected admission. Inject a pending stop at each
of the five guest commit sites and verify exact prefix effects, next guest RIP,
raw roots and recovered flags/vector state. The no-pending path must neither
canonicalize nor mutate guest roots. Capture/semantic event delivery beyond this
service stop remains with the accepted recovery owner. Native-PC cut points need
actual emitted-byte/IR support; if instruction scheduling prevents a valid cut,
report that candidate's required lowering change and stop its timing rather than
inserting five arbitrary checks at the tail. A known guest-order mapping is an
admission condition, not an assumption inferred from final-output PASS.

**Producing path and budget proposed:** extend the existing `boundary.py`/
`execution.c` retained snapshot and VM supervisor with one dedicated checkpoint/
admission mode; reuse input/output models and guard controls. A separate oracle
must derive guest prefix states and admission decisions without importing producer
cut-point tables. Cap the full follow-on exploration at60 wall and90 aggregate
agent-minutes:35 implementation/binding,20 independent oracle,15 review,
10 execution and10 corrections/analysis. At most one initial run plus one focused
correction,90 seconds per phase/180 total each. No downloads or changes to frozen
Track01; one VM/vCPU,256KiB RAM,64KiB emitted-code ceiling and existing12GiB raw
ceiling. Stop at a validated matrix, an unbindable candidate, or the first budget
limit; do not use unused runs to enlarge the matrix. This proposal is not yet a
reviewed decision-evidence method and cannot select a generator.

**Owner challenge — lazy/queued faults:** the proposal received independent
source/direction review, but that does not establish that its checkpoint-heavy
protocol is the best direction. Before execution compare the following candidate
against claim14. This is a hypothesis, not accepted replacement behavior:

- Hardware/semantic faults stop the issuing guest instruction. Retain its native
  frame, precise access/operation identity and partial-effect record. Queue service
  without executing later guest instructions or treating the access as successful.
  Canonical reconstruction and full event delivery can be cold-path work.
- Close conflicting admission before committing a queued mutation. Other vCPUs
  drain to bounded object exits or necessary internal REP/helper safepoints while
  the old view is still valid. Queue competing faults without holding a lock that
  those peers need. Inactive pinned frames are distinct from active RAM grants;
  retaining a frame is not permission to use its old route after invalidation.
- Commit only after all affected old-view accesses are drained. Revalidate queued
  operations after earlier commits. If an operation invalidates its own following
  code, finish its already-admitted instruction through a precise cold continuation
  or semantic path, then dispatch under the new identity; do not resume stale B
  merely because faulting A completed. Preserve permitted partial effects and never
  replay a completed MMIO transaction.
- Asynchronous events can remain pending until an eligible bounded boundary;
  interrupt-unmasking transitions and interruptible long operations retain their
  specific service obligations. Deferred delivery does not allow a synchronous
  fault to retire incorrectly. Ordinary x87 deferred exceptions retain their own
  ISA semantics and are a separate matter.

The decisive cases are two vCPUs faulting during one drain, self-modifying A with
stale following B, remap while a fault frame holds an old route, partially completed
compound/MMIO operations, and an internal loop that otherwise never reaches an
exit. If this construction handles them with cold exits and bounded progress,
remove the universal checkpoint premise and price that revised design. If it
fails, identify the particular unavoidable boundary rather than declaring that
all guest instructions require polling. The current INV03-04 revision2 already queues operations and permits memory-quiescent
PAUSED frames. The new issue is replacing its after-A checkpoint with cold
completion/current dispatch and letting peers finish bounded old-view execution
before mutation; native-frame retention itself is already part of the design.

QEMU's [translator internals](https://www.qemu.org/docs/master/devel/tcg.html)
describe host-PC-based exception recovery, deferred state reconstruction and
block-exit interrupt checks. This is direct support for the separation of fault
capture from state reconstruction, not proof of this captive coordinator design
or a reason to select TCG.

**Finite remaining selection scope:** after this discriminator, consolidate
branch/control-flow, scalar FP and separate cheaper/higher-accuracy x87 paths,
compilation/reuse and adaptation effort in one comparison plan with a single
remaining budget. Do not keep opening one microfixture campaign per omitted cost.
Actual workload priorities and numerical tolerances must be explicit; report
crossover regions and insufficiency where absent rather than inventing weights.
No full80 requirement or Intel-acquisition gate is introduced.

### Execution comparison and selection boundary

Before selecting a winner, use actual emitted code in a bounded captive execution
probe. Extracted snippets need explicit state/relocation/entry/return bindings;
whole upstream blocks cannot be copied blindly. Preserve the full entry/tail and
adaptation accounting. A hand-written equivalent is a control or estimate, never
labelled generator output. Reuse the existing Track03 VM probe foundation and
retention; leave the frozen Track01 program unchanged. A Linux-VM execution result
can guide exploration but is not measured captive-EL1 performance.

The execution method must cover integer/flags/branches, dependent and independent
RAM accesses, vector/integer and scalar FP work, and a resident helper boundary;
include pure x87 arithmetic and a transcendental-helper comparison separately.
Use independent expected results, live-state/fault checks for the measured paths,
and explicit numerical tolerances/error reports. Costs of untranslated system
instructions, exceptional coordination, code invalidation and SMP contention
remain separate sensitivity terms unless actually exercised. This finite matrix
is a research sample, not a reduced guest ISA or product workload qualification.

Use paired randomized batches, calibrated clock/overhead controls and uncertainty
intervals before decision-evidence timing. Report cold compilation, steady state,
code size and helper/boundary costs separately. Estimate total cost across stated
reuse and event/helper frequencies, including x87 frequency and accuracy policy;
replace baseline costs when adapting rather than adding overlapping components.
Do not invent a workload-weighted winner: show crossover regions and test whether
plausible missing adapter costs reverse the ranking. Any unresolved interval that
can reverse selection must get a discriminating measurement or an insufficiency
verdict within a separately fixed budget. No upstream application benchmark alone
can settle the captive architecture's choice.

At the180-minute limit the campaign ends with retained artifacts and either a
concrete execution proposal or an insufficiency report. A proposal names producing
code, inputs, correctness/clock controls and a total preparation/run/review budget;
missing preparation is explicit, not an implied ready-to-run experiment. No further
campaign starts automatically: an extension needs the owner's decision on the
additional budget and decision consequence. Decision-evidence execution uses the workflow's
independent review and replication requirements; bounded exploration remains
available under its lighter rules. This method introduces no additional approval
layer and does not authorize product implementation.

**Historical completion limit at that campaign close:** questions2/5, affected composition and final breadth
remain open. The method can advance those decisions; it cannot substitute for
comparative evidence or accept unwritten adapters.

### Common codegen contract: architectural dependencies

**Scoped integration accepted in independent findings review, September 20.** The bounded
interface pass in [INV-03-02, Supplement L](INV-03-02.md#supplement-l--common-codegen-contract-and-symmetric-adapter-map)
serves question 2 and the following coupled choices. This is a comparison
boundary, not an implementation ABI or selection of the proposals below.

| Input or obligation | Architectural owner and comparison rule |
| --- | --- |
| Exposed x86 behavior and decode state | Question 1 supplies the same profile/modes to every candidate. Selecting a generator does not adopt its upstream CPU masks. A comparison may use an explicitly stated common profile hypothesis without accepting it for the product. |
| Resident execution environment | Question 3 supplies privilege, address domains, available native capabilities and runtime entry points. Claim15 selects guarded EL1; physical state layout remains an implementation binding. All candidates must exclude guest-derived access to private state and guest-derived native control targets. |
| Memory effects and recovery | Questions 4–6 specify allowed accesses, effects, partial progress and recovery obligations. The selected walker and helper provenance remain independent. RETRY_ACCESS/ACCESS_COMPLETED/GUEST_FAULT and nonnesting repair are proposed realizations, not mandatory names or an upstream ABI imposed on every candidate. |
| Dispatch, events and lifetime | Questions 7/8 require valid entry assumptions, legal guest-event boundaries and live code/metadata references. Concrete link counts, checkpoint placement, frame bounds and reclamation recipes must be derived for each adaptation; TCG's candidate capacity constants do not transfer. |
| Translation replies and failures | Questions 3/7/12 require source/state identity, relocation into the resident domain, rejection of stale replies and bounded ownership through cancellation/teardown. A serialized artifact layout and transport can be chosen later; host pointers cannot serve as resident targets. |

**Performance constraint on the comparison:** the contract must permit native
shadow-mapped accesses, resident helpers and deferred state where correct. It
does not require a host request, shim call or full architectural spill for every
guest memory access or instruction. Each candidate instead identifies the checks,
materialization and service transitions required at its actual fault, commit,
event and coherence boundaries. Correctness of those boundaries remains required;
removing upstream machinery is an opportunity, not a measured saving.

An interface cell marked “replacement” identifies work, not its size. An unknown
cell identifies missing evidence, not a defect or higher cost. Selection needs
matched evidence for the consequential differences: emitted guards/spills,
resident versus host services, recovery representation and adaptation surface.
The shared contract alone closes none of the twelve decisions.

## How to use the supporting evidence

The sections below retain source findings and conditional candidate derivations.
Their historical pass descriptions and stopping points describe completed work;
**only the current summary and question inventory direct new work**. An older
“next action” or provisional preference is not an additional active assignment.

- **Shared requirements and interfaces:** address-domain separation, authoritative
  state, explicit access outcomes, exactly-once effects and resource lifetimes.
  Proposed implementations still need their stated composition arguments.
- **Candidate evidence:** QEMU CPU masks, TCG IR/spill/checkpoint/continuation
  details, and extracted QEMU walker branches. Retain their source facts, but
  remap and re-establish the affected proof when selecting different components.
- **Independent component choices:** machine/device reuse does not choose a
  generator. Likewise a code generator does not select the guest CPU profile.

The [retrospective](../../retrospectives/track03-architecture-drift.md) owns the
mistake and lessons. It is not required reading to identify the current choices
above and supplies no architectural acceptance.

## Supporting contracts and execution-boundary assessment

### Translation request ownership — scoped conditional decision accepted

September 20, root: one bounded 30-minute design pass for charter questions 3,
7, 8 and 12. This is a protocol argument over the existing requirements and
accepted finite mechanism evidence, not a new experiment or a generator choice.
Stop at an independently reviewable ownership rule and its remaining premises.

**CLM-03-01-11 — Inferred protocol construction, independently accepted. Selected
choice:** the resident runtime owns admission and publication. A
host compiler receives immutable source/decode inputs and produces an unpublished
object; it never receives authority to update live architectural state or an
executable destination. Use bounded request slots and explicit cancellation.
This refines the existing state/communication hypothesis below without selecting
a wire format, shared-memory transport or generator state layout.

The basis is [INV-01-33 claims 05 and 08–10](INV-01-33.md), which distinguish
complete code/metadata publication from transfer and demonstrate the finite
register-transfer path, and [INV-03-04 claim 12](INV-03-04.md#selected-construction-limits-and-inventory),
which already requires bounded job slots and non-reuse while callbacks survive.
Those records do not prove a production transport or generator adapter.

| Transition | Owner and required action |
| --- | --- |
| Free → admitted | Resident runtime captures checked source bytes and the decode/state, source and mapping dependencies coherently under the memory coordinator. Reserve one request slot and one bounded output entitlement before dispatch. Identity includes VM lifetime, slot incarnation and request identity; source version alone is not a request identity. |
| Admitted → producing/receiving | Host works only on the immutable copy. Transfer progress belongs to this request; partial bytes and metadata remain unreachable. Reply fields are bounded and interpreted as object offsets/relocation identities, never host-selected installation pointers. |
| Receiving → ready | Complete bytes, metadata, relocation records and declared lengths are validated before installation. Validation checks the adapter's structural contract; it does not prove arbitrary native code semantically safe. The compiler and resident helpers remain in the VM's trusted computing base. |
| Ready → published | Resident runtime rechecks request liveness and all entry dependencies under the same serialization authority used by invalidation. It registers the complete object's dependency identity before making it reachable, and completes the selected cache/entry synchronization protocol. A dependency change during preparation prevents publication or invalidates the registered object before affected execution is admitted. |
| Any unpublished state → cancelled | Revoke publication eligibility immediately. Retain the slot, input/output storage and transport references until the producer and every delivery path have relinquished them. Cancellation acknowledgement is a lifetime guarantee, not merely receipt of a cancel message. |
| Published → request retired | The executable object's lifetime transfers to the code-lifetime owner. Retire request storage only after all transport/producer references have ended; publication itself does not acknowledge those references. |
| Retired/cancelled → free | Reuse only after that lifetime condition. Advance incarnation before the next admission. Namespace wrap requires a complete drain, including queued deliveries; never rely on a timeout or numerical wrap alone. |

**Admission/publication invariant:** an entry can name only a complete,
registered object whose dependency identity is still admitted. Checking versions
and then publishing without shared serialization violates the invariant: a
source write could occur between them. Holding a global lock throughout host
compilation is unnecessary and would obstruct progress. Only capture and final
admission require coordination; ordinary compilation operates on copied inputs.
The concrete coordinator may use a lock or an equivalent versioned transaction,
but it must establish this ordering, including direct-link admission and saved
continuations. Existing invalidation rules still determine when an already
executing old instruction may finish; this request rule does not tighten or
relax x86 self-modification semantics.

**Cancellation invariant:** a stale/duplicate response cannot publish or write
into reused storage. An identity check at completion is insufficient if a host
callback has already written through a stale destination pointer. Accordingly,
producer-owned output and delivery storage stay retained until acknowledgement,
or delivery resolves the identity under lifetime authority before obtaining any
destination. The latter still needs retention across the actual copy. No device
side effect is rolled back by this rule: compilation has no guest effect, while
device cancellation remains a separate operation-specific contract.

**State and progress invariant:** waiting for compilation preserves the owner's
recoverable guest state and does not make the host its writer. Resident service
and coordination notifications must remain serviceable while waiting; a host
worker cannot wait for a rendezvous acknowledgement from a vCPU it keeps stopped.
The accepted register-transfer WAIT/poll pattern is one finite mechanism, not a
requirement for per-access host calls. If a guest event changes the continuation,
the old demand is cancelled; delivery uses the event/recovery owner's legal
boundary, then dispatches again. A late translation must not resume the old RIP.
Reserve pressure stops new admissions while already admitted operations drain;
it must not consume the permanent service storage required for that drain.

**Failure and teardown:** malformed output is rejected before reachability;
producer failure leaves the continuation recoverable or triggers contained VM
termination. Teardown first revokes admissions and publication, then stops vCPUs
and drains or terminates producers/delivery paths before releasing storage. A
new VM must not inherit old delivery authority merely because a PID, slot or
address is reused. Process exit may end in-process references, but outstanding
external I/O must separately be completed/cancelled under its backend contract.

**Alternatives and costs:** allowing direct host mutation of live state adds
cross-domain ownership and recovery obligations without being needed for
compilation, so it is not selected. Retaining every historical request avoids
reuse races but is unbounded. Cancelling and immediately reusing its slot is
incorrect. Bounded immutable jobs incur snapshot/output storage and final
validation; they do not require spilling registers on every guest instruction.
Transport throughput and the adapter's actual object bounds remain unmeasured.

**Integration with typed source capture (claim 16 in INV-03-04), independently accepted:**
the compilation job and its source-fetch operation have different effect
ownership. Calling compilation effect-free does not make a completed UC/device
instruction fetch cancellable without effects. The resident fetch record retains
completed fragments and their route/effect identity across host waits, cancellation
and decoder retries. An old host response cannot cause those fragments to be
fetched again. Guest fault/event abandonment follows the architectural fetch
contract rather than the compiler-job storage rule.

If host decoding needs more bytes, it returns a bounded continuation request
for the same current instruction, not permission to read guest memory. Resident
admission checks that the fetch operation and continuation are still live and
that the additional bytes belong to the demanded instruction, under the selected
mode/length and typed-fetch rules. Only then can it perform the fetch and extend
the immutable input supplied to host decoding. No output becomes publishable
until the complete captured instruction and its dependencies are bound to it.
Ordinary cacheable-RAM block formation retains its separately permitted lookahead;
this refinement does not impose single-instruction blocks on that fast path.

**Closure scope:** this can settle translation-job ownership independently of
generator choice. It does not settle all of question 3 or 7: coherent capture,
complete dependency enumeration, emitted entry guards, precise recovery,
publication ordering and concrete callback/reference bounds must satisfy their
separately owned contracts. Counterexamples include an untracked source alias,
an uncounted callback, a reply resuming an obsolete continuation, or a wait path
that blocks required coordination. Independent review has accepted the scoped
conditional choice in the existing
findings thread. Concrete adapter, coordinator and transport conformance remain
with their owners; no runtime execution is authorized by this acceptance.

**CLM-03-01-01 — Inferred, independently accepted in scope:** Track 01 supplies reusable
mechanisms only where this design preserves their assumptions. In particular,
[INV-01-31 claims 05–11](INV-01-31.md) require recovery recipes for live state
and commit points, and exclude arbitrary translator/FP correctness.
[INV-01-33 claims 01–10](INV-01-33.md) accepts register-transfer publication
and finite immutable links; shared staging and reclamation remain unestablished.
Changing to a shared host/VM queue or reclaiming code cannot inherit those
observations unchanged. The investigations link their primary contracts,
observations and independent reviews; no new observation is claimed here.

**CLM-03-01-02 — Source-backed, independently accepted in scope:** QEMU records CPU-state
assumptions in translation blocks and requires interrupt reconsideration after
changes that may unmask interrupts. Its recovery support reconstructs deferred
state per guest instruction. See [QEMU translator internals, CPU state
optimisations, Direct block chaining and Exception support](https://www.qemu.org/docs/master/devel/tcg.html),
documentation reporting version 11.1.50, inspected September 19. This is an
upstream design contract, not evidence that relocated blocks and helpers retain
those properties inside our VM.

**CLM-03-01-03 — Inferred, independently accepted in scope:** a split translator needs three
distinct address domains: host pointers used while compiling, native VM
addresses embedded in emitted instructions, and x86 guest addresses. A helper
call target, CPU-state pointer, literal pool or exception lookup key cannot be
copied between domains merely because both hosts use AArch64. Each relocation
and helper must name its execution domain. The required recovery contract in
INV-01-31 and QEMU's block-state/exception contract above motivate this audit;
the concrete selected generator must settle it in Q-03-02.

**Hypothesis — private-address protection:** execute translated guest code at
EL0 with the shim at EL1, or use guarded generated accesses at EL1. Neither is
accepted. EL0 permissions alone do not protect private CPU state or code
metadata if that state must also be accessible to generated EL0 instructions.
Likewise, placing private mappings in an x86 noncanonical address range does
not itself establish that translated guest operands cannot access them.
The selected design must distinguish guest-derived accesses from trusted
runtime accesses, check full operand ranges and overflow, prevent raw guest
control targets from becoming native code targets, and honor the correct x86
fault precedence. Address checks may be omitted only with an explicit proof
that the access form and chosen native mapping enforce the same restriction.
This is an outstanding proof obligation, not a measured escape or a claim
that one privilege level is intrinsically sufficient.

**Hypothesis — authoritative state and communication:** each vCPU owns its
architectural state while running. A translation request contains source bytes,
decode state and dependency generations captured coherently; it grants no
host permission to mutate live guest architectural state. The shim accepts a
reply only for the still-live request, VM lifetime and dependencies, installs
code through the selected publication protocol, then resumes. Failure or
cancellation leaves a recoverable continuation or terminates the VM. Service
and coordination notifications must remain consumable while the owner waits
for translation. Full register transfer is the existing evidence-backed
transport fallback, not an asserted economical bulk-code transport.

## Concrete execution candidate — guarded EL1, provisional

**Hypothesis:** assess translated code and native runtime helpers at EL1 first,
with x86 CPL represented explicitly in emulated state and shadow permissions.
This preserves the established Track 01 native execution level and avoids
assuming that an EL0 helper-state mapping protects itself. It does not inherit
an isolation proof from Track 01. EL0 remains an alternative if its protection
benefit exceeds the transition/state-sharing cost under the same guest contract.

The candidate uses four-level x86 virtual addressing. Arm stage 1 uses 48-bit
lower/upper regions and 4-KiB translation granules, with top-byte ignoring off.
[Arm Memory Management, version 1.0, §§5.1 and 7](https://developer.arm.com/-/media/Arm%20Developer%20Community/PDF/Learn%20the%20Architecture/LearnTheArchitecture-MemoryManagement-101811_0100_00_en.pdf)
describes region sizing by TCR and separate translation bases. The concrete
configuration and its native faults remain subject to review; this older guide
is not evidence for all newer extensions. Proposed virtual allocation:

| Native virtual range | Intended contents | Admission rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `0000000000000000`–`00007fffffffffff` | Lower x86 canonical mappings | Software x86 walk and current access/CPL policy |
| `0000800000000000`–`0000ffffffffffff` | Private native code, shim, stacks, CPU state and metadata; allocate only bounded needed subranges | Never a valid guest-derived operand or guest-PC destination |
| `ffff000000000000`–`ffff7fffffffffff` | Unmapped private reserve | No normal access |
| `ffff800000000000`–`ffffffffffffffff` | Upper x86 canonical mappings | Same guest walk and current access/CPL policy, not native privilege alone |

Guest physical addresses, IPAs and native VAs are separate namespaces. A guest
PTE's physical target passes through the machine's RAM/MMIO resolver; it cannot
select private VM backing just by using its numeric IPA. Only shim-owned Arm
tables become native translation roots. Guest RAM is native non-executable;
execution comes only from the runtime or admitted translated allocations.
Private data is non-executable. The initial candidate uses runtime-only RWX
code allocations with immutable admitted bytes: only the trusted publication/
retirement protocol may write them, never guest-derived accesses. Native W^X is
optional hardening, not a baseline requirement: EL1 controls its page tables, so
W^X alone would not contain a compromised EL1 runtime. Guest exclusion, correct
publication, complete continuation lifetimes and no live byte rewriting remain
mandatory. If W^X is later selected, its permission/alias maintenance needs its
own assessment; Track 01 established neither W^X nor guest-access guards. The
private pool's concrete capacity and branch veneers are construction choices
within a bounded layout, not permission to emit unrelocated host pointers.

Every native access belongs to one of these classes:

1. **Guest operand or guest stack:** preserve x86 effective-address calculation,
   address-size/segment rules and exception priority. Validate canonicality and
   the span of each architecturally indivisible access before directly accessing
   the corresponding shadow. Decompose instructions with permitted partial
   completion according to their semantic contract; do not prevalidate a whole
   REP/vector instruction in a way that suppresses required partial effects.
   Canonicality/span checks exclude the private range even when native Arm
   translation would accept it. Shadow policy independently enforces x86 CPL,
   CR0.WP and any selected permission extensions. PG-off startup uses the
   guest-physical resolver, not unchecked IPA dereferences.
2. **Trusted runtime state:** use a compiler/runtime-owned base plus validated
   fixed field offsets, or an allocation handle whose owner and bounds have been
   checked. Guest values are field contents, never native state pointers. A
   helper taking a guest memory operand must use class 1, not a raw C pointer.
3. **Guest control flow:** guest RIP/RET/indirect target goes to guest dispatch,
   validating mode/state/mapping identity and instruction fetch permissions.
   It is never branched to as a native numeric address. Trusted native links
   resolve only to admitted block entries and retain lifetime dependencies.
4. **Runtime control flow and metadata:** helper targets, literals, returns and
   recovery PCs use relocation records and runtime-owned identities. Host replies
   cannot choose arbitrary installation addresses. Unknown fault PCs or invalid
   metadata cause contained VM termination, not speculative recovery.

**Inferred conditional isolation argument, not acceptance:** if the emitter and
every helper respect this complete partition, class 1 cannot select the private
VA interval or private backing, class 3 cannot execute guest bytes or arbitrary
runtime offsets, and class 2/4 permissions derive only from runtime-owned state.
Thus guest-controlled values alone cannot acquire native EL1 authority. The
load-bearing premise is still unproved for the chosen generator; its source
audit must enumerate all memory/control operations, including implicit stack,
vector, helper and exception-delivery accesses. A missing access form or an
incorrect fault-precedence check invalidates this candidate. Compiler/runtime
correctness belongs to the trusted computing base; compromised native code still
requires the separate host/VM containment boundary in INV-03-03.

This narrows a design alternative, not the product envelope. It adds guard,
dispatch and shadow-switch costs that Track 04 must retain in the comparison;
the hardware-MMU hypothesis is not a claim of zero per-access work. No bounds
check may be removed for performance without an equivalent checked invariant.

configurations. Root freshly downloaded the official
[Ubuntu 6.8.0-139.139 amd64 headers](https://archive.ubuntu.com/ubuntu/pool/main/l/linux/linux-headers-6.8.0-139-generic_6.8.0-139.139_amd64.deb)
and [Debian 6.12.107-1 amd64 headers](https://security.debian.org/debian-security/pool/updates/main/l/linux/linux-headers-6.12.107+deb13-amd64_6.12.107-1_amd64.deb)
on September 19 and read `usr/src/linux-headers-<release>/.config` from each

`15bacf92d51a0ea971d9c1369c6385e8867cd4973eede8f8931fbaf99f3dd173`
and `0ab746a3d854ad0e666923147842d1e629cdb3e29a97b76ae25b4911285174e4`.
This is inspection of primary build configuration, not a new runtime experiment.

| Config symbol | Ubuntu GA 6.8.0-139 | Debian 6.12.107+deb13 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `VIRTIO_MMIO` | built in (`y`) | module (`m`) |
| `VIRTIO_MMIO_CMDLINE_DEVICES` | `y` | disabled |

**Inferred, independently accepted in scope:** virtio-mmio root-device support on the Debian
working target depends on the unmodified initrd loading the transport and block
modules before root mount. Ubuntu's built-in transport/block configuration
removes that particular module prerequisite, but does not establish ACPI device
discovery or successful boot. Module availability and device discovery remain
required for entropy on both, and network on Debian. A disabled command-line
device option does not reject ACPI enumeration; it prevents treating that
command-line route as an unconditional fallback. Neither table establishes that
all enabled clocks or CPU features must be exposed by the emulator.

These are working target kernels, not an exclusive compatibility boundary or a
claim about every distribution update. Exact bootable image/initrd identities
and representative workloads still require selection and inspection. Continue
with the inherited Ubuntu/Debian targets; do not silently drop Debian to preserve
the smaller machine. A stock virtio-PCI path remains the material alternative.

Source-audit method: a 30-minute follow-up integration pass, no VM execution;
bounded downloads (45 seconds per package), archive-only extraction, checksum
comparison and exact config-symbol inspection. Reading copies are in
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-stock-audit`. The exact packages/configs and inspected
CPU/Linux source copies are also retained in the checksummed external

artifacts: 20 files, 66,182,640 bytes, no binary execution or experiment claim.
The direct official package identities above remain their provenance.
Initial sandbox DNS denial was resolved by approved
network access; no downloaded package code ran. Stop this pass at the concrete
transport prerequisite and hand initrd inspection to the machine investigation.

## CPU profile source pass — provisional candidate

**CLM-03-01-05 — Source-backed, independently accepted in scope:** the pinned QEMU
[`target/i386/cpu.c`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/cpu.c)
provides a useful concrete starting point in `builtin_x86_defs`: Haswell version
2 (alias `Haswell-noTSX`) uses Intel vendor/family 6/model 60, basic maximum
leaf `0xD`, AVX2/FMA/F16C, XSAVE/XSAVEOPT, and disables HLE/RTM relative to
version 1. Its model definition still requests PCID and TSC-deadline, but the
system-mode `TCG_EXT_FEATURES` mask excludes both. A named hardware-like model
therefore does not by itself define the realized TCG guest profile.

The same source distinguishes model loading, feature expansion and accelerator
filtering. `query-cpu-model-expansion` intentionally does not perform accelerator
filtering; using that result alone as the exposed profile would be wrong.
`cpu_x86_cpuid` derives OSXSAVE from CR4, leaf `0xD` sizes from enabled extended
state, and topology from the configured CPU map. Preserve those dependencies.
The inspected `cpu_x86_cpuid` has no `0x15` frequency case, and this base model
has maximum basic leaf `0xD`; the proposed clock contract cannot assume inherited
leaf-`0x15` calibration. Source retrieval was read-only with a 45-second transfer
bound; no CPU object was realized and no guest execution occurred.

**Historical candidate, not a selection:** a versioned virtual CPU derived from
that system-TCG Haswell-v2 definition was used to make source inspection concrete.
Its preference over other profiles is withdrawn pending the independent CPU
contract in question 1; neither TCG masks nor a generator choice sets that contract.
**For that historical candidate only**, the following exposure and topology
choices bounded the audit; they are not requirements for every candidate:
retain AVX2 and its full state/atomicity burden while assessing the
design; do not remove it merely to pass scalar fixtures. Retain baseline x87,
MMX/SSE families, integer/control/segmentation semantics, CX8/CX16, long mode,
required startup32, NX, APIC/x2APIC and XSAVE state dependencies. Final exposure
must be explicitly enumerated after feature filtering, and agree with every
modified helper and memory path. QEMU's availability mask describes upstream
TCG, not proof of our modified runtime's support.

Use four-level virtual addressing, no PCID/INVPCID, no LA57 or 1-GiB-page promise
in this initial candidate; 4-KiB/2-MiB guest pages remain. Features outside the
chosen profile need defined discovery and instruction/MSR rejection, not arbitrary
success. Physical width and the guest RAM/MMIO map must be chosen together,
without exposing private IPA backing. SMP begins with one socket, one thread per
core and distinct APIC IDs, using one topology description for all CPUID/firmware/
mailbox/device routes. CPU hotplug is not implicitly supplied by having APIC IDs.

This is **not yet a complete feature contract**. In particular, PAT/MTRR and
cache-control behavior, privileged debug/error facilities, optional permission
features, full MSR inventory and rejected-instruction behavior remain to audit.
The named model does not waive those obligations. The exact Ubuntu/Debian
`verify_cpu`, Linux feature initialization and workload requirements must be
checked against the resulting profile. Initial nonexclusive workload classes
are software compilation/test workloads, database/file I/O, JVM and JavaScript
JIT workloads, and numeric/vector workloads; concrete versions/inputs and
performance success thresholds remain with current scope and Tracks 04/05.

**Inferred alternatives and decision consequence:** a smaller older CPU could
reduce adaptation but would not answer AVX/vector workload questions; the
inherited `qemu64` definition, for example, exposes a smaller application ISA
and an AMD identity, changing initialization paths. A custom higher CPUID leaf
could advertise a clock ratio but adds a new contract; prefer auditing the
inherited platform calibration path first. A newer/more extensive CPU model
adds feature dependencies without established workload need. These are reasons
for a bounded Haswell-v2-derived audit, not accepted kills of alternatives or
a compatibility narrowing. The immediate consequence is to retain 16-byte
atomicity, full FP/vector recovery and a real reference-clock device in the
coupled investigation instead of hiding those costs.

This 45-minute source pass stops at the first concrete clock/profile mismatch:
machine calibration cannot rely on an absent frequency leaf. Q-03-03 owns the
reference-clock/calibration trace; Q-03-02 owns helper reachability/FP/state;
Q-03-04 owns native atomicity and memory-type behavior. The [independent findings return](../reviews/INV-03-01-findings.md) accepts
claim 05 and the conditional audit priority only; the realized CPU profile remains
unaccepted.

**CLM-03-01-06 — Source-backed, independently accepted in scope: absence of CPUID frequency
discovery does not by itself require a legacy PIT.** In both upstream
[Linux v6.8 `tsc.c`](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.8/arch/x86/kernel/tsc.c)
and [v6.12 `tsc.c`](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/tsc.c),
`native_calibrate_tsc` rejects a maximum CPUID leaf below `0x15`.
`quick_pit_calibrate` returns zero when `has_legacy_pic()` is false;
`pit_calibrate_tsc` then uses an early-delay interval instead of accessing PIT.
`pit_hpet_ptimer_calibrate_cpu` can use the HPET/PM timer samples around that
interval even when PIT calibration fails. The v6.12
[`tsc_msr.c`](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/tsc_msr.c)
model table contains Atom entries, not this Haswell model. The v6.12
[`acpi_pm.c`](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/drivers/clocksource/acpi_pm.c)
PIT-based PM-rate verification is excluded by `CONFIG_X86_64`.

**Inferred, independently accepted in scope:** an explicitly no-PIC machine with a correctly
discovered HPET or ACPI PM reference is a material alternative to adding PIT or
inventing a CPUID frequency leaf. This is a conditional source path, not a
complete clock selection. Firmware must actually establish the no-PIC branch;
early-delay assumptions, timer frequency/read behavior, LAPIC calibration,
interrupt delivery and wallclock discovery remain to trace. These upstream
versions do not establish the complete vendor patch paths or an actual boot.
The source pass stops here and hands the timer/firmware choice to Q-03-03;
the profile cannot claim any particular reference until that choice is reviewed.

**Subsequent provisional choice:** [INV-03-03 claims 17–20](INV-03-03.md)
trace the early pre-ACPI PIT accesses and compare the no-PIT route with existing
QEMU device reuse. Their independent source review supports prioritizing the
host PIC/PIT/port-0x61/RTC models: fewer custom discovery/calibration behaviors
outweigh an unproved benefit from fewer device objects. This supersedes the
no-PIT route as the first machine direction, without rejecting its conditional
source validity. Conventional ACPI/PM/shutdown construction, direct-boot register
initialization, PIC/IOAPIC acknowledgement routing and one shared clock contract
remain to establish; microvm's hardware-reduced FADT cannot simply be copied.
The preference is an adaptation argument, not a measured cost or accepted machine.

The subsequent Q-03-03 provider pass makes that candidate more concrete:
existing PC/PIIX chipset and conventional ACPI construction, SMM off, explicit
PM decode/SCI configuration after reset, and loader-applied table relocations
and checksums before RSDP publication. It moves IOAPIC state back to the host
models while retaining LAPIC/event execution in the shim. ExtINT PIC
acknowledgement is side-effecting and the inspected upstream call order is not
an admission guarantee; the bridge must own acknowledgement and EOI phases
explicitly. Device source was audited at QEMU v10 while the CPU/frontend source
is a later pinned revision. The single-worker candidate now selects the frontend's
pin for device/QOM/ACPI code as well, after Q-03-03 claims 25–26 rechecked the
selected seams. The recheck found concrete changes, notably a PM-timer read path
without inherited BQL serialization, so one pin does not eliminate the
host-access authority obligation. It avoids tacitly combining two QOM runtimes.
The owning record retains the review status and remaining
controller/firmware contracts; none of these choices establishes a boot result.

## CPU feature inventory — source pass

September 19, `/root`, within the recorded 60-minute source-only budget. Inspected
the pinned QEMU model, expansion/filtering, TCG defaults, CPUID implementation and
system MSR helpers. No CPU object was realized, guest executed or performance
measured. The web reader failed to fetch the immutable source; the previously
retrieved local pinned files supplied the inspected text.

**CLM-03-01-08 — Source-backed, independently accepted in scope:** the following is
the core feature-word intersection of Haswell version 2 with system-mode TCG at
`c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294`, before unspecified machine compatibility
overrides. It is an explicit source inventory, not an observed final CPUID dump.
Version 2 disables HLE/RTM; TCG's instance defaults disable VME. Model loading
adds the hypervisor feature, and expansion adds HT when the configured package
has more than one logical processor, including multiple single-threaded cores.

| Feature word | Surviving named features in this source intersection |
| --- | --- |
| CPUID.1 EDX | FPU, DE, PSE, TSC, MSR, PAE, MCE, CX8, APIC, SEP, MTRR, PGE, MCA, CMOV, PAT, PSE36, CLFLUSH, MMX, FXSR, SSE, SSE2; HT added for a multi-processor package |
| CPUID.1 ECX | SSE3, PCLMULQDQ, SSSE3, FMA, CX16, SSE4.1, SSE4.2, X2APIC, MOVBE, POPCNT, AES, XSAVE, AVX, F16C, RDRAND, HYPERVISOR; OSXSAVE is dynamic |
| CPUID.7.0 EBX | FSGSBASE, BMI1, AVX2, SMEP, BMI2, ERMS |
| CPUID.80000001 EDX | SYSCALL, NX, RDTSCP, LM |
| CPUID.80000001 ECX | LAHF_LM, ABM |
| CPUID.D.1 EAX | XSAVEOPT |
| CPUID.6 EAX | ARAT |

Source: pinned [`cpu.c`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/cpu.c),
`builtin_x86_defs`, `TCG_*_FEATURES`, model loading,
`x86_cpu_expand_features`, `x86_cpu_filter_features`, `cpu_x86_cpuid`;
[`tcg-cpu.c`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/tcg-cpu.c),
`x86_tcg_default_props`. Absence from the table is not a claim about every
auxiliary CPUID/MSR word. In particular the model initializes VMX capability
words, but VMX is not requested in CPUID.1 and expansion removes dependent
capabilities; copying those raw initializers as exposed MSRs would be wrong.

PCID, TSC-deadline and INVPCID do not survive the system-TCG masks. HLE/RTM are
already off in version 2. LA57, SMAP, PKU, 1-GiB pages, AVX-512, RDSEED and
MONITOR are not in this model's requested feature set. Therefore the current
no-PCID/no-LA57/no-1-GiB-page candidate does not need additional feature removals
from this intersection. SMEP **is** present: supervisor instruction-fetch
permission and CR4.SMEP transitions must participate in dispatch/shadow policy.
Do not remove SMEP or RDRAND simply because their adapters have not yet been
audited. The RDRAND helper calls `qemu_guest_getrandom`, so its in-VM execution
also needs the selected service/entropy contract, not a raw host-library call.
[`int_helper.c::helper_rdrand`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/int_helper.c).

The source adds these realization dependencies beyond feature masks:

- PMU defaults off; CPUID.A is then zero. Topology, APIC IDs, enabled topology
  leaves and possible level adjustment still depend on instance/machine settings.
- OSXSAVE follows CR4; leaf D's current save size follows XCR0 in TCG. The final
  manifest must include the enabled save components and their exact offsets,
  rather than assume the AVX bit alone supplies a complete state contract.
- Physical width defaults through `TCG_PHYS_ADDR_BITS` when not explicitly set;
  extended leaf 80000008 also derives virtual width from enabled features. Set
  the eventual width together with RAM/MMIO resolution and reserved-bit checks.
- HYPERVISOR does not imply an implemented paravirtual ABI. The TCG signature
  leaf additionally checks `tcg_enabled()` and `expose_tcg`; the new accelerator
  must choose that discovery behavior explicitly. System realization also sets
  `CF_PCREL`; a TCG adapter using that realization must retain the anchor in its
  recovery/continuation contract. This does not require other generators to use
  TCG's PC representation.
- MCE/MCA cause `mce_init` to initialize banks/capabilities. PAT/MTRR, debug,
  machine-check, APIC and time MSRs need behavior inventories; they cannot be
  represented by just their discoverability bits.

**Concrete stop / design consequence:** the pinned system
[`misc_helper.c`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/system/misc_helper.c)
does not provide a ready-made strict unsupported-MSR policy. After machine-check
bank handling, the default `helper_rdmsr` branch returns zero and default
`helper_wrmsr` ignores the write, both with a source comment questioning an
exception. Other branches do raise #GP for selected invalid cases. Therefore
“inherit the filtered Haswell model” does not settle the charter's unsupported
discovery/rejection requirement. This is a source observation, not a blanket
claim that every unlisted MSR must fault or that QEMU's compatibility policy is
a defect. The next bounded CPU pass must inventory admitted MSR families,
reserved/unsupported behavior and guest initialization requirements against
