primary specifications, with an explicit dispatch/rejection policy. Source
intersection alone cannot accept the complete CPU profile. No compatibility
waiver or lower ISA ceiling is introduced by this finding.

The same inspection exposes a concrete clock seam: `MSR_IA32_TSC` is defined as
0x10 in pinned `cpu.h`, but these system RDMSR/WRMSR switches have no case for
it. `emit.c.inc::gen_RDMSR/gen_WRMSR` call those helpers, while
[`misc_helper.c::helper_rdtsc`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/misc_helper.c)
uses `cpu_get_tsc(env) + env->tsc_offset`. Thus the current source paths cannot
be inherited unchanged as the candidate's coherent RDTSC/RDMSR/WRMSR clock
contract. The selected runtime needs explicit TSC-MSR routing to the same
per-vCPU clock/offset authority. This is a source-path discrepancy and a required
adaptation, not an executed test or a claim about all QEMU accelerators.
The independent CPU inventory return in the [findings thread](../reviews/INV-03-01-findings.md)
accepts this scoped inventory and both MSR seams; the complete CPU contract
remains open.

## MSR policy pass — validation before mutation

September 19, `/root`: execute the recorded 60-minute source pass, with at most
32 MiB of new primary specification/Linux-source downloads, 60 seconds per
download, archive/text inspection only. Compare Intel SDM revision 092 RDMSR/
WRMSR and relevant register definitions with the pinned helper switches and
Linux v6.8/v6.12 initialization. Stop at a reviewable policy or the first
incompatible guest requirement and its smallest correction. No code execution,
CPU realization or runtime experiment is authorized by this method.

**CLM-03-01-09 — Source-backed/Inferred, independently accepted in scope:** Intel
SDM revision 092 [Vol. 2B, RDMSR](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922481/253667-092-sdm-vol-2b.pdf),
pp. 4-544–545, and [Vol. 2D, WRMSR](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922485/334569-092-sdm-vol-2d.pdf),
pp. 6-8–9, require #GP(0) for reserved/unimplemented MSR indices and disallowed
privilege. WRMSR additionally checks specified address-bearing registers for
canonicality and specifies reserved-bit failures. MTRR writes invalidate TLBs,
including global entries. Most WRMSRs serialize; the listed x2APIC and
TSC-deadline exceptions must not be confused with a generic memory barrier.

**Concrete source counterexample:** for the candidate's 48-bit linear-address
profile, WRMSR to IA32_FS_BASE with value `0x0000800000000000` must fault. The
pinned `helper_wrmsr` case instead assigns `env->segs[R_FS].base = val` directly;
`gen_WRMSR` updates PC/CC, calls that helper and ends the block without validating
the value. Rejecting the address only when a later load uses FS would report
the exception at the wrong instruction and leave invalid state committed. This
is a source-derived counterexample to unchanged helper reuse, not a runtime
observation. Sources: pinned
[`system/misc_helper.c`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/system/misc_helper.c)
and [`emit.c.inc`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/emit.c.inc).

### Proposed MSR dispatch contract

Use one profile-owned admission/validation layer before inherited or adapted
MSR handlers. An entry names index/range, availability predicate, readable and
writable fields, reset state, value checks, commit owner and post-write effects.
An absent entry raises #GP(0); it does not invoke the permissive upstream default.
The register-specific contract decides reserved and read-only bit handling;
there is no universal “mask unknown bits and succeed” rule. Preserve the
frontend's instruction/privilege/prefix checks and precise origin. Validate
before mutating CPU state or sending a side-effecting host request. Failed
validation leaves the pre-instruction architectural state, apart from the
required exception, intact. This is a proposed adaptation, not new runtime code.

The admitted-family inventory to complete is finite and tied to the selected
feature words, not every physical Haswell facility:

| Family / proposed route | Required contract and identified source mismatch |
| --- | --- |
| TSC 0x10 and TSC_AUX 0xC0000103 | RDTSC/RDTSCP and MSR access share the same clock/offset and auxiliary state; add the missing TSC-MSR handlers. TSC_ADJUST and deadline do not become available merely because TSC exists. |
| APIC_BASE 0x1B; x2APIC indices 0x800–0x8FF | Route to authoritative LAPIC state with mode, index-hole, read/write and reserved-field checks; completion/EOI owns its side effects. |
| SYSENTER CS/ESP/EIP 0x174–0x176; EFER/STAR/LSTAR/CSTAR/FMASK 0xC0000080–84; FS/GS/KERNEL_GS bases 0xC0000100–102 | Reuse state and transition handlers behind validation; enforce canonicality only for the registers whose specification requires it, and EFER/control dependencies before commit. Intel CSTAR is defined but unused by compatibility SYSCALL, not automatically an unknown register. |
| PAT 0x277 and the enumerated MTRR family | Validate memory types, physical width, ranges and writable fields; use coordinated memory-policy transition and required invalidation. Plain field assignment is insufficient. |
| MCG capability/status/control and advertised machine-check banks | Match the exposed capability/bank count, access permissions and reset values; no pass-through of Apple hardware state. |
| MISC_ENABLE, microcode identity and model/platform compatibility registers | Explicitly define retained discovery/control behavior and distinguish optional safe probes from mandatory accesses. No physical firmware update or host power control is implied. |
| Debug and optional feature-owned registers | Admit only with their complete exposed debug/feature behavior. PKRS, BNDCFGS, VMX/SVM or other upstream switch cases are not admitted solely because a helper implements storage for them. |

[Intel Vol. 4 revision 092, Table 2-2 and the Haswell tables](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922493/335592-092-sdm-vol-4.pdf)
provide the architectural index/availability definitions; the table above is
the candidate's work partition, not a complete bit-mask manifest. In particular
IA32_TSC_ADJUST depends on CPUID.7.0.EBX[1], absent from claim 08; TSC_AUX's upper
32 bits are reserved. Retaining RDTSCP therefore does not justify accepting an
arbitrary 64-bit auxiliary value. QEMU's register switch alone cannot define
feature availability or architectural validation.

### Linux compatibility check and stopping point

Both upstream [v6.8 Intel CPU initialization](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.8/arch/x86/kernel/cpu/intel.c)
and [v6.12](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/cpu/intel.c)
read IA32_MISC_ENABLE without a safe-MSR wrapper for this family/model and use
its fast-string bit to retain or clear ERMS-related capabilities. That register
cannot simply be omitted when tightening default behavior. By contrast,
`init_intel_misc_features` safely probes MSR_MISC_FEATURES_ENABLES and returns
on failure; `init_cpuid_fault` safely probes MSR_PLATFORM_INFO. For those paths,
a specified #GP absence is handled; a fabricated zero is not needed to avoid a
boot fault. This checks the named paths, not every driver or exact vendor patch.

[Linux v6.12 `common.c::syscall_init/idt_syscall_init`](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/cpu/common.c)
sets STAR/LSTAR/FMASK and the compatibility SYSENTER state; `wrmsrl_cstar` skips
Intel CSTAR writes. Together with the architectural address checks, these paths
support validation at the MSR operation itself, with coherent state for later
kernel/userspace transitions. They do not establish the whole startup32 or
debug/FP contract.

**Stop and smallest correction:** the first concrete incompatible semantics is
the missing precommit address validation, with TSC routing and default-index
rejection already identified in claim 08. Adopt the profile-owned validation
layer as the provisional direction; preserve inherited state helpers after
checks rather than replacing the entire semantics implementation. Complete
register field/reset/side-effect rows and the remaining Linux call-site audit
before declaring the CPU profile accepted. No feature was hidden, workload
dropped or full implementation demanded. Reading copies are under
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-msr-audit`; three official revision-092 PDFs and
three upstream source files total less than 9 MiB, within the declared bound.
All transfers completed; no guest code, downloaded source or binary executed.

**Sibling-path check within this pass, independently accepted in scope:** the same
precommit address rule must cover the exposed FSGSBASE instructions. SDM Vol. 2D
pp. 6-6–7 requires #GP(0) for a noncanonical WRFSBASE/WRGSBASE source. The pinned
`decode-new.c.inc` entries select FSGSBASE/64-bit-mode/prefix checks and operand
zero extension; `gen_WRxxBASE` tests CR4.FSGSBASE and then directly moves the
operand into the FS/GS base global. An MSR-only validator therefore leaves a
sibling write path unaddressed. Reuse the canonicality predicate across these
state-mutation paths while preserving their different privilege, mode, feature
and operand-width rules. Do not make unprivileged WRFSBASE depend on CPL0 just
because WRMSR does. This is source-based adaptation scope, not a new execution
result or a claim of complete address-state mutation coverage.

## Control-state pass — EFER validation precedes state loading

September 20, `/root`: execute the recorded 60-minute source-only control-state
pass using already retrieved revision-092 Intel manuals, pinned QEMU source and
Linux v6.8/v6.12 source. No downloads, guest execution or implementation. The
declared stopping condition permits a concrete incompatible dependency with its
smallest correction; the EFER mode-transition case below reaches that condition.

**CLM-03-01-10 — Source-backed/Inferred, independently accepted in scope:** Intel
[SDM Vol. 3A revision 092](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922487/253668-092-sdm-vol-3a.pdf)
§5.1.2 requires #GP(0) when WRMSR changes EFER.LME while CR0.PG is set.
§12.8.5 specifies reset EFER=0 and the coordinated CR0/CR4/EFER entry sequence;
LMA becomes set through successful activation, rather than a guest-writable
mode selector. [Vol. 4 Table 2-2](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922493/335592-092-sdm-vol-4.pdf)
defines SCE, LME and NXE as writable, LMA as read-only, and the other Intel EFER
bits as reserved. Reset state is distinct from a direct loader's prepared
64-bit entry state; the latter must establish a consistent active-mode tuple.

At the pinned revision, [`gen_WRMSR`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/emit.c.inc)
records PC/CC and calls `helper_wrmsr`. Its
[`MSR_EFER` case](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/system/misc_helper.c)
builds a feature-dependent update mask and invokes
[`cpu_load_efer`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/helper.c).
That loader assigns EFER and derives LMA/SVME hidden flags; it does not validate
the CR0.PG/LME transition. The frontend's end-of-block action cannot repair a
missing exception or undo already committed invalid state.

**Concrete counterexample:** start at CPL0 with CR0.PG=1, CR4.PAE=1 and
EFER.LME=LMA=1. Execute WRMSR(0xC0000080) with the old value except LME cleared.
The selected Intel contract requires #GP(0), leaving EFER unchanged. The source
path instead clears LME while preserving LMA. This is a source-derived mismatch
of the inspected TCG path, not a runtime result or a claim about other QEMU
accelerators.

**Smallest correction and composition:** the proposed guest MSR validator must
reject `CR0.PG && ((old_efer ^ requested_efer) & LME)` before calling the state
loader. Preserve an identical LME write while paging is enabled; rejecting all
EFER writes in that mode would incorrectly reject legitimate SCE/NXE changes.
Keep guest-instruction validation separate from trusted reset/direct-loader
state construction: `cpu_load_efer` also has SMM, SVM, debugger and loader
callers, whose existence does not authorize the same guest WRMSR transition.
Successful SCE/NXE changes must reselect compatible translated execution and
the applicable shadow policy before the next guest instruction. In particular,
NXE changes both execute permission and the interpretation of XD bits as
reserved; merely checking execute permission on an already admitted shadow is
insufficient. The exact invalidation implementation remains part of the complete
control-state contract, not established by this one predicate. Vol. 3A §5.1.1,
footnote 2, specifies that WRMSR ignores source bit 10 (LMA); preserve the
derived LMA rather than faulting solely because that source bit differs. This
closes that read-only field's write disposition without a generic rule for all
read-only MSR fields.

**MISC_ENABLE scope checked in this pass:** Vol. 4 Table 2-2 identifies fast
strings (bit 0), MONITOR enable (18), CPUID maximum limiting (22), and conditional
or read-only fields. Pinned `helper_wrmsr` merely stores the full value; reset
uses `MSR_IA32_MISC_ENABLE_DEFAULT` (1), optionally adding MWAIT (18). This is
not a complete virtual-register definition. Linux
[v6.12 `intel_unlock_cpuid_leafs`](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/cpu/intel.c)
clears bit 22 and re-reads CPUID when changed; its fast-string check reads bit 0.
The BTS/PEBS availability reads are guarded by `X86_FEATURE_DS`, absent from
the current candidate feature inventory, so that Linux path does not by itself
require exposing those facilities. No claim is made that raw storage alone
implements the CPUID-limit or feature-control effects. Complete model-specific
field availability, reset/readback and write rules remain open.

**Disposition:** submit claim 10 for independent findings review. The pass stops
at the concrete EFER incompatibility as budgeted; it does not claim completed
EFER/MISC_ENABLE rows or acceptance of the CPU profile. The next source work
must finish the coupled control-state transition table, rather than treating
each missing check as a separate architecture question.

## Integration pass — access completion must select an explicit continuation

September 19, `/root`, source/protocol work within the 90-minute next action
above; no generated-code execution. This pass composes INV-03-02's proposed
access ABI with INV-03-04 rendezvous revision 2. The following is a **proposed
interface and conditional argument**, submitted for independent review, not
acceptance of an emitter or the whole architecture.

The bounded examples use ordinary aligned scalar RAM accesses with a single
native load/store, and one MOVS iteration whose source and destination are RAM.
They do not stand in for split accesses, MMIO, atomic lowering or every helper.
Those remain required parts of the full architecture. The pinned frontend
ordering is recorded in [INV-03-02 claims 08–10](INV-03-02.md); the requirement
for a real generated checkpoint is established by
[INV-03-04 claims 08–09](INV-03-04.md).

**Missing interface distinction:** a service result cannot simply mean “resume.”
If the coordinator has already performed a trapped store, retrying its original
native instruction duplicates the architectural write. Conversely, advancing
past an unperformed load after installing its shadow leaves the destination
undefined. The continuation must distinguish these outcomes:

| Outcome | Effect owner and permitted resume |
| --- | --- |
| `RETRY_ACCESS` | Service changed only the internal mapping. Restore the complete native frame and retry the same unperformed native access; retain earlier instruction effects and temporaries. |
| `ACCESS_COMPLETED` | Service performed the recorded access exactly once. Restore the frame with the declared result/status updates and resume at that access expansion's explicit success continuation. Do not execute the access again. |
| `GUEST_FAULT` | Preserve required committed/partial state, reconstruct the originating guest PC/CC and fault information, and abandon the native continuation through the VM-local exceptional exit. No success continuation executes. |

These outcomes belong to an immutable operation descriptor keyed by VM lifetime,
vCPU, continuation identity and access phase. The descriptor identifies x86
access intent, checked RAM routing and its lifetime, width, source/result
locations, and the emitter-owned native retry/success locations. Neither a host
reply nor a guest value supplies an arbitrary native PC. A replayed or stale
service result cannot advance the phase or perform the effect again. General
success is **not** `native_pc + 4`: writeback addressing, exclusive loops,
multi-instruction expansions and helper calls have different contracts. Even
the scalar case below requires proof of its chosen expansion and frame layout.
Native syndrome alone cannot decide x86 read/write intent, as INV-03-04 claim 07's
atomic-load writeback counterexample demonstrates.

**CLM-03-01-07 — Source-backed, independently accepted in scope:** the pinned
[QEMU AArch64 backend](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/tcg/aarch64/tcg-target.c.inc)
already records an access-success location. `tgen_qemu_ld` and `tgen_qemu_st`
assign `TCGLabelQemuLdst::raddr` after emitting the direct access when a slow-path
label exists. `tcg_out_qemu_st_slow_path` calls the store helper and jumps to
that location; the load path additionally uses `tcg_out_ld_helper_ret` to restore
the result before that jump. Thus a successful service can continue after an
access without replaying it in the existing generator design. This is a concrete
reuse point, not evidence that its host helper ABI or pointers can run unchanged
inside the VM. The adapted fault metadata must make the corresponding success
location and result contract available for every admitted direct-shadow access;
the upstream conditional creation of a slow-path label does not already do that.
This pass inspected the retained local pinned source; a web-cache fetch failed
and supplied no additional evidence. No runtime result is claimed.

### Three conditional traces

**TCG adapter scope:** these traces depend on the inspected IR synchronization,
live-state and continuation conventions. The shared access-result contract is
not a proof that another generator supplies those conventions. Re-establish
the traces against any selected replacement; they do not rank generators.

1. **Ordinary load A, then instruction B.** The guest-memory IR synchronizes
   required architectural globals before A's access. The guarded backend checks
   the guest address before issuing the native load. A shadow miss captures a
   frame before the destination load has completed. Admission closes and every
   other observer pauses/drains; the requester retains its native frame and
   pinned metadata rather than falsely acknowledging a canonical park. A drain
   service establishes the permitted shadow and returns `RETRY_ACCESS`. Restoring
   the frame and retrying that load preserves its result destination and earlier
   state. A completes, then an actual emitted checkpoint materializes next-PC/CC
   and leaves for permanent dispatch before B. A true x86 permission fault instead
   returns `GUEST_FAULT`, leaving A's architectural destination unchanged. Native
   EL1 privilege never substitutes for the software x86 permission decision.
2. **Tracked scalar store A modifies B's source page.** The trapped access has
   not written RAM. Its descriptor captures the already computed address and
   value from the retained native frame; decoding A again from mutable guest
   bytes is forbidden. Under exclusion, the service checks the captured access
   route/permission, invalidates future eligibility of source-dependent blocks,
   performs the write and advances source generations. It returns
   `ACCESS_COMPLETED`, so A's original store is not retried. A's native code and
   metadata remain pinned while its success continuation finishes the instruction
   and reaches the forced checkpoint. Dispatch sees B's new source identity;
   reclamation waits until the old frame and all other references are gone.
   Pending guest interrupts cannot interrupt this completion between its write
   and architectural boundary. An instruction with another required effect
   follows its recorded phase instead of pretending this scalar trace covers it.
3. **REP MOVS has loaded a RAM element; its destination store traps.** The
   destination in this trace does not overlap the REP instruction's own source
   bytes. The source value is already a live temporary. Save it in the native continuation;
   service the destination as in trace 2, without rereading the source or
   restarting the whole REP instruction. The unchanged semantic order must then
   update the applicable RSI/RDI and count for exactly that completed iteration.
   An architectural event exit at a legal iteration boundary uses the REP guest
   PC if work remains, or the following guest PC if complete, with matching
   CC/RF/interrupt-shadow state. An internal coordination stop instead retains
   the native loop under revision 3 below; it does not execute event-exit RF
   changes. Previously completed iterations survive a subsequent true
   guest fault. INV-03-04's ongoing frontend audit owns the exact checkpoint
   placement; this trace does not yet prove its RF/shadow handling or implementation.

In all three traces the coordinator grants only the retained operation's finite
drain segment while other observers remain excluded. Another fault yields its
updated phase to the same coordinator, not a recursive coordinator acquisition.
A guest fault may park as pending exception state; exception delivery is a
separate checked operation. Mapping/backing dependencies survive until covered
accesses finish; a completed guest shootdown cannot precede that drain. Backend
RAM commits follow the same admission authority, so “all paused” includes them.

**Decision consequence:** retain the two-state pause/park protocol and add the
explicit access outcome to the proposed generator/service ABI. Pinning alone
does not settle effect ownership or retry behavior. The pass stops at concrete
remaining premises: generated success locations/result restoration, the actual
normal/REP checkpoint, and the VM-local exceptional exit. INV-03-02 owns helper
and exceptional exits; INV-03-04 owns checkpoint state and coordinated access
phases. These three traces are reviewable conditional compositions, not a claim
of complete recovery, isolation or all-instruction coverage. The
[independent review, Return 7](../reviews/INV-03-01-findings.md), accepts source
claim 07 and these conditional interface traces, retaining their implementation
and coverage premises. INV-03-04's subsequent checkpoint pass exposes the
same-instruction REP source-mutation case: an internal pause with the same RIP
does not authorize fetching different instruction bytes. That case is addressed
by the subsequent revision below, not by trace 3 alone. Guarding, frame
capture, checkpoint spills and exclusive-service costs remain in the cost case.

### Reviewed provisional composition after the REP and repair passes

[INV-03-04 revision 3](INV-03-04.md#rep-continuation-and-batched-reclamation--revision-3),
independently reviewed in its findings Return 5, now selects retaining the
original decoded REP/native loop across internal coordination. Its explicit
operation handle cannot admit unrelated execution of invalidated code. A real
eligible guest interrupt/fault instead follows the architectural exit and
abandons that native operation; subsequent guest return fetches normally.
The saved native PC-relative anchor and RF context survive internal resume.
Inspectable iteration registers do not imply that native references are gone.

The corresponding reclamation choice changes: batch-reclaim eligible allocations
while retaining active-operation dependencies, rather than requiring every long
REP to finish before any cache space can be freed. This requires per-allocation
ownership and a bounded retained set, not a global arena reset. Q-03-04 claim 12
now supplies an independently reviewed conditional construction: bounded whole
objects, a fixed current/handover/origin allowance per vCPU, bounded publishers
and callbacks, and reserved drain/successor capacity. It excludes transitive
link-graph pinning and accumulated completion history. Actual object/helper and
service limits must satisfy that contract; numeric sizing and allocator
conformance belong to construction and cost validation.

[INV-03-02 Supplement G](INV-03-02.md)
supplies the separately reviewed resident repair direction: one retained guest
continuation and separate fixed IRQ scratch storage, checked stable RAM aliases,
explicit service results and a permanent exceptional landing. Its walker cannot
inherit QEMU's recursive `start_exclusive` slow path unchanged. The extracted
direct walker/accessor inventory is now recorded in reviewed claim 16. It exposes
side effects in upstream nonfault probes and distinguishes bus transaction
responses from architectural page faults. Exact non-RAM PTE result/update
semantics, A/D writer preparation and cleanup remain concrete design gaps;
the next work freezes these interfaces rather than sampling more helpers.
A checkpoint pause and a repair must share the
same operation ownership; neither may overwrite the other's live native frame.

Subsequent reviewed Q-03-02 claim 17 and Q-03-04 claim 13 define typed PTE
results and the A/D transaction boundary. They preserve an exclusive-entry
dependency baseline and any mismatch already present before an owned A/D update;
advancing an owned version cannot erase an earlier race. Only the verified
owned delta with unchanged address/permission/routing fields may be folded into
the walk's dependencies. The unresolved case is now narrower: semantic
revalidation after an earlier side-effecting PTE read must neither replay that
transaction nor admit a stale reusable shadow. Q-03-02 claim 18 separately
identifies guest-debug delivery after MOV SS as an event-boundary contract that
unchanged immediate debug delivery does not supply. These remain semantic
design obligations rather than reasons to demand a complete implementation.

These are reviewed provisional protocol choices with remaining semantic
premises, not proof of generated code. Track 03 must close those premises and
the relevant composition argument; later implementation validation checks that
code conforms to the accepted design. Requiring a full implementation before
an architecture can be chosen would contradict this track's charter.

## Combined interactions and falsifiers

These are the original **Hypothesis** obligations and retained adversarial cases.
Claim18 below now supplies an independently accepted conditional composition
argument. Questions2/5 and final architecture acceptance remain open; listing
these cases alone never established them.

| Interaction | Required argument and adversarial counterexample |
| --- | --- |
| CPU state / dispatch / privilege | A mode or feature change must exclude incompatible cached/direct-linked blocks; try a kernel-to-user transition with the old link retained |
| Recovery / temporary state | A helper or fault cannot overwrite unrecovered flags/vector/FP state; interrupt at every noncommitted substep |
| Every writer / aliases | A backend write through a physical alias must invalidate affected code and participate in paging policy before required visibility |
| Remapping / split accesses | Revoke or reroute the second page between validation and commit; preserve legal partial effects and fault ordering |
| Cross-domain ordering / MMIO | Completion notification cannot precede data visibility; retry cannot duplicate an irreversible device read or write |
| Compilation / mutation | Change code bytes or mappings after request capture and before publication; stale result must be rejected |
| Retirement / references | Evict a block while another CPU has a helper return, fault frame, direct link or compilation reference to it; no reclaimed object may be used |
| Events / waits / locks | Request shootdown while target waits for a translation whose service needs the initiator; protocol must avoid that circular wait |
| Boot / topology / time | Mailbox IDs, firmware CPU IDs, interrupt destinations and clock frequency must agree with the CPU model |
| Failure / reuse | Complete old I/O or compilation after teardown/reset; identity checking and resource ownership must prevent effects in a new lifetime |

## Method, limits and review

### Architecture-framing audit — September 20

The dedicated [retrospective](../../retrospectives/track03-architecture-drift.md)
retains the audit, claim dispositions and systematic lessons. Operationally,
claims 05/08 here are upstream CPU inventories only, and claim 07's associated
traces are TCG-specific. The question inventory above governs their current use.
Cleanup does not withdraw valid source facts or accept the rebuilt comparison.

Source-only integration assessment. Seek conflicting contracts and missing
dependencies, not just examples of successful component use. The supporting
investigations compare material alternatives and adaptation implications.
Independent agents author those investigations; a different context must review
each author's load-bearing claims and this combined record before acceptance.
Pending review is work status, not itself a correctness defect.

No VM, synthetic model or performance experiment has run under this record.
Experiments will receive a concrete question, controls, budget, retained-input
method and stopping condition before execution; decision evidence additionally
requires independent pre-execution review and independent replication. Later
implementation testing does not excuse an unresolved architecture-level
correctness uncertainty.

The [independent review](../reviews/INV-03-01-findings.md) accepts claims
01–11 in their stated scope and specifies closure conditions for protected layout, generated recovery,
service progress, product compatibility and all-writer coherence. Completion
breadth and independent architecture acceptance remain outstanding.

## Coupled control-state transition closure — source pass in progress

September20, root: a 30-minute source/design pass for charter1/3/4/5/7 using
retained Intel092 manuals and pinned component sources. Complete one coupled
CR0/CR3/CR4/EFER/XCR0 admission-and-commit construction, including alternate
mutation paths, rather than treating each absent helper check as a new question.
The profile remains independently owned; this pass cannot adopt TCG feature masks
or waive startup32. Stop with a reviewable transition table and exact residual
profile obligations. No native experiment or implementation is authorized.

### CLM-03-01-12 — One resident control-state transaction, with instruction-specific validation

**Root-authored source construction, independently accepted in memory’s
findings review of claim12.** Select a
resident semantic owner for guest CR0/CR2/CR3/CR4, EFER and XCR0. Guest writes
never directly write native EL1 control registers. The owner validates a proposed
x86 transition, prepares its derived mode/permission/FP state, then commits it
with the applicable shadow invalidation and dispatch change before admitting the
next guest instruction. This is a concrete adaptation boundary common to codegens;
it selects neither their CPU masks nor their upstream execution loops.

Sources are retained Intel SDM **Vol.3A092**, §§2.5–2.6, 5.1.1–2, 5.4.1 and
5.10.4.1; **Vol.2B092**, MOV to/from Control Registers, pp.4-32–34;
**Vol.2D092**, XSETBV pp.6-70–71; and retained **Vol.2A082**, CLTS/LMSW.
The primary PDF links are [Vol.3A](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922487/253668-092-sdm-vol-3a.pdf),
[Vol.2B](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922481/253667-092-sdm-vol-2b.pdf),
[Vol.2D](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922485/334569-092-sdm-vol-2d.pdf).
Reading copies are in the existing `natemu-track03-msr-audit` and
`natemu-track03-split-cmpxchg-01` directories under `/private/tmp`; no newly
executed code or observation supports this claim. The web reader could not
retrieve Vol.3A; its already retained primary text supplied the inspected content.

#### Validation and state effects

Instruction decoding, prefix legality and exception priority remain with the
instruction owner; this table specifies the control-state portion after the
applicable instruction admission. Unsupported control-register numbers and
invalid encodings are not converted into generic MSR-style #GP responses.
MOV control-register accesses require guest CPL0; non-64-bit MOV operands are
32 bits and zero the stored upper half. MOV CR8 routes to the LAPIC/TPR owner,
with its own reserved-field rules; unlike other MOV CR accesses, it is not
architecturally serializing. Guest CPL is independent of native execution EL.

| Operation | Validation before architectural mutation | Committed state and next-admission effect |
| --- | --- | --- |
| MOV CR0 | Reject PG=1/PE=0 and NW=1/CD=0. Reject reserved high32 bits; reserved low32 bits are ignored, not blanket #GP. Keep ET set for the selected Intel-style contract. Activating IA-32e requires PAE=1, current CS.L=0 and a non-16-bit TSS. Clearing PG while in64-bit mode is #GP; a legal compatibility-mode exit is distinct. Apply feature-dependent constraints, such as CET/WP, if that feature is exposed. | Compute CR0 plus derived EFER.LMA and execution-mode/FP-admission state together. PG, WP and cache-policy changes update the selected memory policy; MP/EM/TS changes update instruction admission. No stale block or shadow may execute using the previous assumptions. |
| MOV CR3 | Validate the value using the destination paging mode and physical-width contract. In4-level mode with PCIDE=0, high bits beyond MAXPHYADDR fault; ignored low bits do not. Do not apply this same mask blindly to legacy PAE CR3, whose directory-pointer base is32-byte aligned. When legacy PAE is active, read and validate all four required PDPTEs before committing CR3 or their hidden register set. | Install the new root/hidden PAE state, then complete required translation invalidation even for a same-value MOV CR3. The PCIDE=0 contract invalidates non-global translations and paging-structure caches; this design may discard globals too. Root equality is not permission to omit the operation. |
| MOV CR4 | Reject bits reserved by the exposed profile. In active IA-32e mode, changing PAE or LA57 is #GP, including compatibility execution. Apply any exposed feature's enable dependencies. Under the current no-PCID/no-LA57 candidate, those enable bits remain reserved rather than ignored. Perform any required legacy-PAE PDPTE loading before commit. | Commit permissions, mode and instruction-admission derivatives together. PGE changes, PAE changes and SMEP enable have specified invalidation effects. OSFXSR/OSXMMEXCPT/OSXSAVE and other enabled control bits must feed their actual FP/instruction contracts; OSXSAVE discovery follows the committed CR4 bit. |
| WRMSR EFER | Admit only the profile's fields; ignore source LMA as specified. Reject LME changes while PG=1. Other reserved bits follow the register's #GP rules. Validate before the inherited state loader. | Keep LMA derived from the valid PG/LME tuple. SCE changes syscall admission; NXE changes both permission and reserved-bit interpretation in paging. End incompatible execution and shadow admission before continuing. |
| XSETBV XCR0 | Preserve #UD for absent XSAVE or OSXSAVE=0 and illegal prefixes. Preserve CPL0/index/value #GP checks. For the x87/SSE/AVX state subset, allow only CPUID.D.0-supported bits, require x87=1, and require SSE when AVX is set. Further exposed components bring their specified joint-enable dependencies. | Update XCR0 and instruction/save-area admission coherently. CPUID.D sizes and AVX instruction eligibility use current XCR0. Changing enablement does not itself erase register values or create valid recovery backing; the resident FP-state owner retains them. |
| LMSW and CLTS | Retain their own privilege/prefix and operand-fault rules. LMSW loads only low4 CR0 bits and cannot clear an already-set PE; its memory operand must complete before control-state commit. CLTS only clears TS. | Use the same derived-state/dispatch reconciliation as the corresponding CR0 changes. Do not route LMSW through an unchecked full-register overwrite or impose MOV's undefined arithmetic-flags behavior on it. |
| MOV CR2 / fault update | A guest MOV follows its control-register instruction rules. A delivered page fault writes its actual fault address through the exception owner. | CR2 is authoritative guest state, not the native FAR or an automatically canonicalized address. Internal shadow repair must not overwrite it as if it were a guest #PF. |

For legacy PAE, Intel§5.4.1 also requires PDPTE reload when a MOV CR0/CR4 leaves
PAE paging active and modifies CD, NW, PG, PAE, PGE, PSE or SMEP. The four entries
are hidden **guest semantic state**, distinct from disposable native shadows.
A present entry with reserved bits causes #GP at the control instruction and
does not load the candidate PDPTE set. This does not assert that a previously
loaded hidden snapshot is cleared. Select the documented present-entry validation
variant; the manual separately permits some processors to check nonpresent
entries too. Do not delay this #GP until the next ordinary memory access or
misreport it as #PF. Exact physical routing/memory-type behavior for those reads
continues to use the memory owner's typed-access contract.

The current4-level fast-path choice is not a proof that protected-mode paging
can be removed. CR0.PG=0 is identity linear-to-physical translation; legacy
32-bit paging and PAE are distinct architectural modes. Required startup32
remains supported. This transaction accepts legal control transitions into their
semantic owners; it cannot make missing legacy walker behavior disappear through
an invented #GP. Complete walker coverage remains question4. Real-mode execution
remains the owner's explicit exclusion; this record adds no new compatibility
restriction or promise to execute real-mode code.

#### Commit, invalidation and recovery composition

Use the current all-observer coordinator only when the transition changes shared
mapping/policy resources; state-only changes stay with the single vCPU owner.
Before a control commit, reserve needed descriptors and retain the originating
instruction/recovery state. Stage any required PAE loads and all validation
without publishing a partially updated control tuple. Faults retain the old
register tuple and report the original instruction, while independently allowed
operand/walk effects remain governed by their own ledger. A control operation is
not a rollback transaction for previously committed guest memory.

For a successful transition, serialize the affected semantic state, derived
execution key and mapping generation with publication/invalidation. Complete
native shadow/TLB synchronization required by the selected memory mechanism
before allowing any continuation to use that generation. Returning from an
upstream `tlb_flush` does not establish this for captive native mappings. Select
full eviction of the affected vCPU's cached guest translations on accepted
paging-control writes as the simple initial policy: Intel§5.10.4.1 expressly
permits additional invalidation. Hidden PAE PDPTE registers are not disposable
TLB entries and must not be silently reloaded by that eviction.

End or guard incompatible translated execution, including direct links; retained
code may be reused under an exactly matching entry key. Do not revoke another
vCPU's control registers because a shared shadow object is invalidated. A normal
native pause/reentry still uses its own current controls. State-only XCR0 or TS
updates require no invented global memory flush, although they reselect affected
instruction/FP assumptions. Whole-register spilling at every memory access and
host-side emulation of every control instruction are unnecessary.

A success continuation carries the committed new control state and next guest
instruction phase. An old precommit fault map cannot undo it. If a host failure
prevents required postcommit native synchronization, contain the VM before
reentry; do not deliver a fabricated guest fault after reporting the control
write as complete. Unrelated pending events retain current ownership.

#### Reuse seam, alternatives and limits

The pinned QEMU [`helper.c::cpu_x86_update_cr0/cr3/cr4`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/helper.c)
provides useful state-derivation code, but includes its own TLB runtime and a
legacy-PAE validation TODO. [`misc_helper.c::helper_write_crN`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/system/misc_helper.c)
is not the whole instruction validator. [`fpu_helper.c::helper_xsetbv`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/fpu_helper.c)
checks supported XCR0 bits/dependencies and refreshes derived flags; frontend
checks also matter. [`translate.c`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/translate.c)
implements distinct LMSW/XSETBV admission and block ending, while
`cc_helper.c::helper_clts` directly changes TS. These are concrete alternate
mutation seams to reconcile, not reasons to adopt QEMU's whole system runtime.
Trusted loader/reset construction and any exposed task-switch state mutation
must invoke the same derivation/publication responsibilities with their own
architectural validation and partial-progress rules. A guest MOV validator must
not be blindly imposed on those different entry paths.

**Selected scoped choice:** one resident admission/commit owner, reusable pure
state derivation, explicit native-shadow and code-entry reconciliation, and
initial conservative translation eviction. Independent validators in every
emitter duplicate the coupled rules; unvalidated upstream helper calls leave
runtime-dependent effects unaccounted for. Host round trips for all control
operations add ownership and latency without a demonstrated need. The selected
owner allows native fast paths and resident semantic service without claiming a
measured saving. Full-eviction cost remains an explicit optimization opportunity,
not a correctness blocker where the specification permits it.

**Limits and review:** this settles a control-state integration construction,
not the final CPUID/MSR profile, full legacy walkers, segmentation/task-switch
semantics, FP save/restore or instruction-exception priority catalog. These remain
named charter obligations rather than alleged completed behavior. Independent
review must challenge the commit/hidden-state distinction and alternate mutation
coverage; no prototype is needed to restate this source-level transaction.

**Claim12 closure:** memory independently accepted the resident control-state
transaction in the existing findings thread, with no substantive return. Final
profile and the separately identified semantic coverage remain open.


## Clock discovery and LAPIC frequency — bounded source choice

**Method:** root, September20; a30-minute source pass on the frequency dependency
left by claim06 and INV-03-03 claim29. Inspect Intel CPUID leaf15 and Linux v6.12
`native_calibrate_tsc`, `detect_art`, `__setup_APIC_LVTT` and
`calibrate_APIC_clock`. Stop at a concrete shared frequency contract or a missing
source dependency. No experiment, new runtime or generator selection.

### CLM-03-01-13 — Select a coupled virtual TSC/crystal/LAPIC frequency contract

**Source-backed interface choice and explicit design inference; independently
accepted by memory in the findings thread.** For the Intel-vendor virtual CPU candidate, select a fixed **1GHz TSC**
and **24MHz virtual crystal/LAPIC input**. CPUID.15H, subleaf0, returns EAX=3,
EBX=125, ECX=24000000, EDX=0. Raise maximum basic leaf to15H; do not advertise
leaf16 processor frequency or copy a physical Haswell CPUID image wholesale.
Any otherwise unsupported intervening feature leaf must return its specified
unsupported form consistently with the corresponding clear feature bits.
This chooses virtual counter rates, not translated instruction throughput or the
Apple core's physical rate. All vCPUs expose the same values.

Intel Volume2A, CPUID Table3-8, leaf15 defines TSC frequency as crystal frequency
multiplied by EBX/EAX, with ECX in Hz and EDX reserved zero. The chosen rational
gives exactly1GHz. The retained primary manual is253666-082US under
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-split-cmpxchg-01/intel-vol2a.txt`;
[primary Intel manual](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/812383/253666-sdm-vol-2a.pdf).
This is an explicit extension of the candidate's former maximum-D inventory;
claim05's absence-of-leaf finding remains true of the upstream source audited.

Linux v6.12
[`native_calibrate_tsc`](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/tsc.c)
requires Intel vendor, sufficient maximum leaf and a nonzero ratio; the selected
nonzero crystal avoids its model-specific frequency fallback. Crucially it also
sets the LAPIC period from that crystal. Therefore exposing the TSC ratio while
leaving the shim LAPIC at an unrelated inherited rate would be incorrect.
[`calibrate_APIC_clock` and `__setup_APIC_LVTT`](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/apic/apic.c)
use that period and the programmed divide configuration; the direct-calibration
path expects the local timer to remain running. The shim must consequently count
from24MHz divided by the guest-selected divisor, including while the vCPU is
halted or descheduled. This does not bypass x86 masking/pending rules.

Bind both counters to INV-03-03 claim29's single virtual elapsed-time expression.
For elapsed active nanoseconds N before per-vCPU architectural offsets, TSC is
N modulo2^64 and the crystal count is floor(N*24000000/1000000000).
The actual implementation uses the shared exact host timebase rational with
wide intermediates, rather than successively rounding through nanoseconds and
crystal ticks. LAPIC current-count, initial-count, divider and expiry calculation
use that same rational and their instruction-commit anchors. Round native wakeup
scheduling toward no earlier than the virtual deadline, then recheck the deadline
on wake; a callback never advances virtual time. Explicit whole-VM pause freezes
both through the existing epoch protocol; ordinary coordination, HLT and host
preemption do not. Host sleep retains claim29's active-time policy.

RDTSC/RDTSCP/IA32_TSC reads and IA32_TSC writes use the already selected common
TSC expression and per-vCPU write-offset owner. A TSC write does not change the
crystal, LAPIC countdown or peer offset. This scoped profile keeps TSC_ADJUST
and TSC-deadline unadvertised, as in the existing candidate, rather than acquiring
their MSRs by exposing leaf15. Linux `detect_art` additionally requires adjustment
support and other prerequisites, so leaf15 alone does not establish its ART
cross-timestamp facility. Required TSC instruction privilege/ordering and
TSC_AUX checks remain in the CPU semantics owner.

**Alternatives and cost:** retaining maximum-D and calibrating from PIT/PM remains
a compatible alternative under the earlier conditional machine contract, but
leaves frequency discovery dependent on sampled early execution. The selected
leaf supplies an exact virtual ratio and avoids that frequency uncertainty.
Reporting leaf16 or a host-core MHz value provides no needed counter semantics.
Using the native counter rate directly could reduce arithmetic, but would make
guest rates host-dependent; no measured speed advantage is claimed for either.
The fixed conversion can be specialized by the host generator without changing
its visible result. Legacy PIT/PM/RTC support remains for their own interfaces;
this choice does not remove them based on one Linux calibration path.

**Closure and falsifiers:** this settles the numeric frequency/discovery seam
conditionally on the Intel-vendor profile and shared time owner. A different
vendor, inconsistent leaf dispatch, independently advancing timer epoch, or
LAPIC rate that differs from the reported crystal reopens it. It does not close
all timer-register semantics, interrupt delivery, CPU profile or boot. Native
conformance remains later implementation validation; no calibration probe is
needed merely to choose these internally consistent virtual rates. Independent
findings review is the next action for this claim.


**Claim13 closure:** memory independently accepts the coupled numeric frequency/
discovery/LAPIC seam. Native conformance and full clock/timer/profile composition
remain distinct obligations; no new runtime gate is introduced.

## Question 7 synthesis — one code identity and lifetime authority

**Method:** machine-authored bounded45-minute source/design synthesis, September20.
Read charter question7, this record claim11 and typed source capture, INV-03-02
claim37's actual dispatcher/link/landing seams, and INV-03-04 claims08–19 with
its revised coordinator and retained-object inventory. Reinspect Intel Vol3A092
§§7.6/11.1.3, the selected immutable publication direction and the pinned FEX
transfer source already retained under `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-fex-entry-01`.
No code mutation, source-download campaign, native execution or new mock. Select
one coherent architectural construction and distinguish its adapter/semantics
premises from any remaining code-lifetime design choice. This is not Track03
completion or a generator selection.

### CLM-03-01-14 — Select immutable bounded objects, registered execution leases and serialized code authority

**Question7 architectural contract independently accepted by root; combined
architecture and implementation conformance remain separate.** Select the
following construction for code identity, publication, mutation and reclamation.
It refines claim11's alternatives to the existing single coordinator's mutex,
closed admission epochs and per-vCPU owned continuation records. Host compilation
is outside that authority; the resident execution owner admits entries and
performs the executable installation. Architecture closure here means the
choices and ordering argument are specified, not that an unwritten emitter,
metadata catalog or platform barrier has been proved conformant.

### Concrete object and identity choices

Use finite **bounded translation objects**, including multi-basic-block CFGs.
Each adapter declares a finite maximum E of external data-link cells and the
metadata needed to enumerate every external transfer phase. Internal CFG edges
stay within their object's lifetime; they do not create additional allocation
pins. REP may loop inside its object with explicit iteration continuations.
Guest CALL/RET retain architectural stack effects. Between native objects select
tail transfer through the guarded external-entry interface; permanent resident
helpers do not recursively enter generated execution. This avoids an unbounded
native return-address ancestry independently of any particular slot formula.

A bounded guest-return prediction cache is permitted: its entries contain guest
return identity plus object incarnation and an **external guarded entry** target,
not an unchecked interior native continuation. A hit uses the same entry guard
and transfer-phase census as a data link; a miss performs ordinary resident
lookup. Before reclamation, detach cache entries under closed admission and
include any target already loaded by a CPU in the in-flight census. Cache entries
therefore do not indefinitely pin historical objects or their reachable graph.
This retains a return-prediction optimization without importing FEX's unchecked
native call/return stack. An adapter may omit the cache, but that is a cost choice,
not a correctness requirement. Larger bounded CFGs likewise remain eligible;
no basic-block or two-exit restriction is selected to preserve old arithmetic.

An object identity contains VM lifetime, unique allocation incarnation, admitted
guest entry PC, immutable CPU-profile/adapter identity, decode context, executable
source-fragment identities and their physical source generations, and the
architecturally valid mapping/route dependencies that produced those fragments.
A hash can accelerate lookup but cannot replace comparison of these identities.
The decode/context key includes the relevant architectural mode/segment and
control assumptions. **Conservative specialization rule:** bake only the declared
profile/decode inputs; read other architectural operands at execution. Any
additional CPU value specialized by an adapter becomes an explicit key dependency
with a mutation hook before such code may be published. Control/segment/mode
changes invalidate the corresponding context identity before subsequent entry;
ordinary changes to runtime operands do not invalidate code merely because they
change registers. This distinguishes source identity from all mutable guest state.

Lookup keys also bind the vCPU context when an assumption is vCPU-specific;
cross-vCPU object reuse is permitted only for equal complete declared contexts.
Source tracking uses physical bytes/routes, so writes through a different GVA,
CR3, shim access, backend or A/D update cannot escape merely by changing the
virtual address. All-writer admission from question6 supplies the mutation
boundary. A new writer or writable alias must join that authority before use;
code registration cannot race an unregistered write.

WB source capture under claim11 copies a coherent finite byte/decode input.
A UC/device source fetch instead retains claim04-16's completed effects and
operation identity. Such input is not promoted to freely reusable byte-cache
eligibility: absent a separate stable-fetch contract, bind the generated result
to that admitted fetch/operation only, with a fresh fetch admission required for
new execution. Host decoder continuation requests extend only the same admitted
instruction, without replaying completed fetches. Cancellation of compiler work
does not erase architectural fetch effects.

### Publication and invalidation have one ordering point

Reserve the claim11 request/output slot before compilation. Host receives
immutable inputs and returns bounded bytes/metadata/relocation offsets into its
own retained delivery storage. The resident copies a complete, structurally
validated object into a private unentered slot through the selected register-
transfer installation path; no guest-derived address can target those bytes.
Generated native instructions and recovery metadata are immutable after admission.
Only runtime-owned data-link cells change during execution; no warmed instruction
backpatching is selected.

Under the **same coordinator mutex** used to close writer/entry admission,
revalidate request incarnation, source and context dependencies, and register the
new object's dependency membership while it remains unreachable. Installation
and native cache/entry maintenance occur with relevant executors quiescent under
that closed epoch. Publish the entry/data target only after complete code and
metadata visibility and required executor context synchronization. The resident
publisher's own code writes avoid assuming that a host pointer write alone
publishes executable instructions across domains. This uses the selected
publication mechanism and its platform premises, not a new permission-flip or
cross-domain self-modification protocol.

A writer under that authority first closes conflicting entry admission, obtains
memory-quiescent participant records, revokes matching future entries/links, then
commits its actual physical write effects and advances affected source/mapping
identities. Complete required invalidation bookkeeping before reopening observers.
If publication won the mutex first, the writer sees its registered object and
revokes it; if mutation won first, the publisher's revalidation fails. There is
no check-then-unregistered-publication interval. Changes during the potentially
long host compilation merely make its output stale; no mutex spans compilation.
A duplicate/late result fails the request identity check and cannot reuse old
storage until producer/delivery references drain. Namespace wrap requires drain,
not a guessed sufficiently large generation counter.

Mapping invalidation is distinct from writing PTE bytes. Required guest invalidation
acknowledgement and route/tag reuse wait until covered old accesses cannot
execute; code invalidation does not revoke an already admitted operation's
physical route halfway through its effect. Optional conservative invalidation
must respect the memory owner's selected architectural translation/cache rules.
A/D writes are ordinary tracked writers for this purpose; their own-dependency
fold cannot erase an earlier mismatch, as claim04-13 already requires.

### Entry, active execution and handover

Every external entry—including a warmed data link—targets claim02-37's resident
veneer. Its guard checks live object/context/source admission and acquires or
transfers the execution pin without a host compiler/dispatcher round trip.
This does not make the first veneer instruction safe by itself. Before that
instruction, **incoming cached/link targets and in-flight transfers are lifetime
references**. Reuse waits for their retirement under the existing all-participant
reclamation protocol. Invalidation changes eligibility immediately but leaves
retained entry bytes available to reject a stale target safely.

Use the existing fixed handover record and exact transfer-phase metadata to
account for old object, destination and operation origin (at most K=3). A stop
between loading a data-link target and acquiring its entry pin must identify the
pending target from the captured native context/transfer phase; it cannot fall
into an uncounted interval. Under closed admission, clear relevant incoming
cells/cache entries and census in-flight transfers before freeing their targets.
A link is an independently detachable eligibility edge, not a reason to pin its
entire reachable graph. Unknown/unmapped native phases are a conformance failure;
they do not authorize freeing an object or guessing a target reference.

**Superseded polling policy:** claim19 replaces the universal ordinary-instruction
checkpoint below with queued faults, bounded object/loop exits and precise cold
completion. The following paragraph retains the earlier construction and its
source evidence; it is not an additional current polling requirement.

Inside an admitted object, the earlier construction used a stop checkpoint at
**every guest-instruction commit boundary and every legal REP-iteration boundary**.
Do not infer it from native-PC maps or TCG `insn_start`. The checkpoint is an
actual conditional transfer with correct current-state recipes and native
continuation identity, as the reviewed TCG/FEX source constructions demonstrate
in their respective scopes. A mutation that paused instruction A can let A's
already admitted effects finish under its retained operation, but cannot let
stale following instruction B execute from that block. Permanent drain service
repairs/services A, then forces its next boundary to dispatch under current
identity. RETRY_ACCESS, ACCESS_COMPLETED and GUEST_FAULT retain their distinct
meaning; completed MMIO/device effects are never replayed for canonicalization.

A healthy long REP keeps its admitted decoded operation and pinned object across
internal iteration pauses; it does not require a fresh lookup or a historical
object per iteration. A true guest fault/event abandons native execution through
the semantic owner, and later architectural restart performs normal current
source admission. Preserve any native PC-relative anchor independently from the
guest-facing boundary PC. Intel's
[self/cross-modification and restart rules](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922487/253668-092-sdm-vol-3a.pdf)
(§§11.1.3 and7.6) support distinguishing synchronized new execution from an
invisible runtime pause; no universal claim about a physical Haswell's
unsynchronized next-instruction prefetch is made. Required synchronization cannot
be bypassed by resuming a completed old operation as if it remained in flight.

### Reclamation and progress argument

Retain claim04-12's fixed-capacity slot construction with configured finite V
vCPUs and P admitted compiler jobs, P at least one. Let B bound a complete object
including entry veneer, recovery/checkpoint metadata and at most E data-link cells.
The separate bounded per-vCPU return/lookup cache storage is included in R;
its entries are detachable under quiescence, not additional persistent object pins.
Use `C >= 3V + P + V + 1` slots. The execution/handover union occupies at most3V;
producer outputs at mostP; V successor reservations and one service spare remain
available before admitting new work. Tail transfers and permanent helpers remove
native call ancestry; REP count does not enlarge this retained union. Metadata,
frames, typed operation records and callback storage retain the explicit F/A/J/Q/R
budgets from claim04-12 rather than being hidden outside B.

This is a selected admission invariant, not a claim that a numeric B or stack
bound has been measured for a chosen full-ISA adapter. The adapter must declare
finite sizes and split oversized objects at guest boundaries; a supported single
instruction uses its bounded permanent semantic path if it does not fit an
ordinary body. E may differ between adapters without changing the argument.
The former 2C link-cell estimate becomes E*C, included in C*B. Routine valid instructions may not be rejected just because an
optimizer exceeded the block budget. Those sizing/ABI obligations belong to
adapter conformance; no unbounded object or recursive generated stack is an
alternative permitted by this choice.

At pressure, close admission, cancel stale unpublished work, and park/pause
participants. Reclaim only allocations outside the actual retained reference
union after incoming edges/caches and in-flight targets are retired. Preserve
pinned islands; never reset an entire arena while a paused fault/helper/REP
continuation still refers to it. Native PC, return, exception, spill/recovery map
and callback-origin references all count. Host backend callbacks carry operation
IDs rather than generated pointers; their RAM/result operations stay retained
until acknowledged completion/cancellation. A timeout is not an acknowledgement.

Drain service uses permanent reserved storage, does not wait on compilation or
on a participant made unable to service coordination by that wait, and grants
only one exclusive operation segment at a time. Compiler replies/event arrivals
remain serviceable during translation waits. Backpressure stops *new* cache work;
a retained REP resumes in its existing object. An architectural event releases
or retains that operation according to its semantic phase and can use the
reserved successor slot. Thus cache reclamation does not require a healthy REP's
remaining count to reach zero, a second coordinator acquisition or completion
of an unrelated backend I/O. Genuine unrecoverable resource/runtime failure uses
the declared contained-VM teardown, never stale storage reuse.

### Question7 disposition and boundaries

The identity comparison, complete publication, alias/all-writer invalidation,
warmed links, active instruction/REP continuation, stale replies, and every
native-reference reclamation class now have one selected owner and ordering.
The choices jointly prevent the concrete races: stale publication after mutation,
write-through canceled storage, entry into freed veneer bytes, old suffix execution
after a source write, and reclaim waiting forever on a healthy REP. The table's
question7 row can therefore move from a list of candidate mechanisms to this
**selected conditional architectural contract**, pending independent review.

The conditions are named external correctness contracts, not claims proved by
this synthesis: question1's complete architectural context/CPU semantics,
question3/5's actual generator phase/state/helper metadata, question6's all-writer
memory/barrier authority, and question8's legal guest event delivery. Their
implementation or full-profile completion is not asserted. If a chosen adapter
cannot supply the fixed transfer phases/checkpoints or requires recursive
native generated calls, it does not fit this selection without revising it;
that is a falsifier, not a hidden supported route. No mock or code patch is
needed merely to choose this contract, and no performance acceptance follows.
Root independent review of claim14 and row7 is the next action.


**Claim14 closure:** root independently accepts the question7 architectural
contract after the bounded-CFG/return-cache correction. This advances that design
choice beyond an inventory of mechanisms; combined architecture acceptance still
depends on the named CPU, memory, recovery and event contracts.


## Execution privilege, address authority and transport — proposed composition

**Method:** root, September20, one30-minute architecture composition over the
existing guarded-EL1 candidate, accepted request/lifetime contracts11/14,
INV03-02’s resident entry/result interfaces, and the accepted Track01 register-
transfer mechanism. No experiment, new platform assertion or generator selection.
Stop at a reviewable question3 choice with explicit cross-question dependencies.

### CLM-03-01-15 — Select guarded EL1 execution and resident-owned register transport

**Architectural contract independently accepted by memory after R15-1 correction.** Select host
compilation and captive native execution at EL1, with x86 privilege represented
only by guest architectural state and memory policy. Retain the48-bit Arm layout
in the execution candidate above: guest canonical halves map only resolved guest
backing; bounded private allocations occupy the lower x86-noncanonical interval.
Guest physical numbers never directly select IPA/private backing. Stage1 tables
and roots are resident-owned. Guest RAM is native NX; only admitted translated
objects and permanent runtime code are executable. This selects the candidate,
not a claim that an existing upstream emitter already respects it.

**Concrete operand exclusion.** After the instruction’s address-size/segment
semantics, a direct native guest access of positive length n requires both:

- unsigned E=A+n-1 does not overflow;
- either A and E are both at most0x00007fffffffffff, or A and E are both at
  least0xffff800000000000.

This is a sufficient fast-path predicate, not a replacement x86 exception rule.
A rejected predicate goes to the typed semantic access owner, which distinguishes
noncanonical/segment faults, legal wrapping or split behavior and permitted
partial effects. It must never issue the unchecked native access. Instructions
with several architectural subaccesses apply admission at their legal boundaries;
this predicate does not preflight a whole REP/gather/save instruction. Shadow
permissions independently encode the selected CPL/WP/NX/SMEP policy. Memory
question4 owns those semantics; question6 owns atomic widths and ordering.

Trusted state accesses instead use the resident vCPU/allocation base and bounded
ABI offsets. Guest data stored in a pointer-sized field is still guest data.
Helper interfaces distinguish guest addresses, resident handles and scalar
values; guest-memory helpers use the same checked access owner. Implicit stack,
descriptor, exception-delivery and paging accesses do not escape classification
merely because a C helper performs them. Indirect guest RIP/RET destinations
enter guest dispatch, never a native branch. Only resident-resolved block entries,
permanent helpers and retained native continuations may supply native targets.
Claim14 owns their identity and pre-entry/return lifetime.

**State authority through boundaries.** The running vCPU alone owns its live
guest state. Register allocation and deferred flags/vector state may remain
noncanonical while valid recovery recipes name their actual roots. Each helper
boundary declares inputs, outputs, clobbers and retry/completed/fault phases;
only affected live roots need preservation. The permanent exception landing
first captures native state on a resident stack, then uses the pinned code/phase
metadata before clobbering those roots. Unknown metadata/PC is contained failure,
not guessed guest recovery. Internal repair retains the operation; guest fault/
event delivery uses the canonical state required by its semantic owner.
This binds questions2/5’s state/result contract without pretending the unfinished
FEX exporter or full guest exception catalog is already correct. An adapter that
cannot produce the required roots or helper phases is incompatible and reopens
that adapter choice, rather than licensing state loss.

**Concrete communication baseline.** Select the inherited stopped-vCPU register
transfer for translation source requests and code replies; claim11 owns jobs and
claim14 owns installation. The host vCPU-owner thread exchanges bounded scalar
headers and byte chunks only at a permanent resident service stop. The resident
retains its guest roots before using transfer registers, chooses destination
allocation/offset, validates request/lifetime/chunk bounds, and copies admitted
reply bytes itself. The host cannot turn a reply field into a resident pointer
or installation address. Source chunks come from the request’s immutable captured
image; they are not repeated reads of concurrently changing guest source pages.
Cancellation/stale replies cannot resume an obsolete operation. Bounded chunk
framing/field assignments are construction details within this selected transport,
not a new shared-memory coherence assumption. Publication remains separately
serialized and synchronized by14 before any entry becomes reachable.

The creating/owning host thread alone performs run and vCPU state register/API
operations. A bounded control thread may call **hv_vcpus_exit** to wake that owner
from a blocked run; this is the explicit cross-thread exception, not permission
to mutate vCPU registers. SDK26.4 hv_vcpu.h documents both blocking run and the
forced-exit latch when a targeted vCPU is not currently running. Publish the
bounded pending request and lifetime/epoch before the kick; the owner rechecks
those records after every exit and before reentry. A stale or coalesced wake is
not loss of the durable request.
[Apple forced-exit API](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/hypervisor/hv_vcpus_exit(_:_:)).

A forced exit may stop arbitrary native code. It neither establishes an x86
instruction boundary nor permits overwriting transfer registers or reclaiming
its frame. The owner retains that native state and uses the existing resident
control/rendezvous path to reach its appropriate PAUSED/service disposition.
Only the permanent register-transfer service stop admits transfer-register reuse.
The permanent wait/poll path remains available while compilation is outstanding;
coordination does not depend on guest IF or the compiler completing. Failure of
a required native control operation contains the VM rather than fabricating an
acknowledgement.

While compilation is outstanding, the resident permanent wait/service path continues
coordination and event bookkeeping; it neither blocks the shared memory owner
on that compiler nor holds a live RAM grant. A response wake is a service prompt,
not an x86 interrupt or authority to resume guest code. Existing admitted device
RAM windows remain the separate question4 backend interface; they do not grant
compilers arbitrary access to private runtime state.

**Isolation argument and trust limit.** Guest-derived memory cannot reach private
VAs through the direct predicate or the checked physical resolver. Guest-derived
control cannot become a native target. Runtime references originate from owned
allocations/relocations and survive through their admitted lifetime. Under those
three interface invariants, guest numerical privilege cannot confer EL1 authority.
The compiler, resident helpers and publication code are trusted implementations
of these invariants; this does not claim to contain malicious native compiler
output inside the VM. Compromise/failure containment of the host worker remains
question12. Accepting a software architecture cannot establish correctness of an
unwritten emitter, but missing semantic interfaces remain real design gaps in
their owning questions, not automatically deferred tests.

**Alternatives and cost.** EL0 remains an alternative requiring a different
helper/state-access and transition design; EL0 permissions alone do not isolate
state that generated code must itself access. Guarded EL1 preserves the accepted
native execution mechanism and permits resident helpers without a privilege
transition per call. Register transfer avoids introducing a private shared queue
for this choice, but its compilation/publication bandwidth may be expensive.
No lower-cost claim is made for it or for the direct-access guards. A later
transport or EL change must re-establish affected authority, coherence and
continuation contracts. Codegen comparisons must include these actual costs.

**Closure:** independent memory review accepts question3’s privilege, address
authority, state ownership and communication contract after the wake-interface
correction. Named memory/recovery/lifetime/event/containment interfaces remain
required; incompatible later choices reopen the affected composition. Actual
adapter conformance, combined-architecture acceptance, codegen selection and
runtime behavior are not established by this architectural acceptance.


## Combined CPU-profile proposal — explicit virtual exposure

### CLM-03-01-16 — Intel-compatible x86-64/SMP profile and remaining compatibility-register seam

**Author:** memory, September20, bounded source/design composition; independent
root review accepts the amended scope after R16-1–3. This profile choice: this does not overwrite claims12–15
or declare an unimplemented instruction engine correct. The profile below is
selected independently of QEMU's TCG availability mask. Reused semantics/codegen
must satisfy it, rather than silently filtering it to whatever an upstream
realization happens to expose. No runtime, boot or compatibility experiment.

Primary contracts are the retained Intel CPUID table in253666-082US, control/
paging rules in253668-092US and MSR definitions in335592-092US, with the Linux
v6.12 Intel initialization source already retained for claim09. They define the
bit meanings and compatibility checks; selected cache geometry/topology/capacity
are explicitly virtual design choices, not new observations.
[Intel CPUID](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/812383/253666-sdm-vol-2a.pdf),
[Intel control/paging](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922487/253668-092-sdm-vol-3a.pdf),
[Intel MSRs](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922493/335592-092-sdm-vol-4.pdf),
[Linux Intel initialization](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/cpu/intel.c).

#### Selected identity, topology and instruction surface

Select `GenuineIntel`, family6/model0x3c/stepping3 as the guest compatibility
identity, with the hypervisor-present bit and a plainly virtual brand
`Natemu Virtual x86-64 CPU`. This retains the intended Intel-style control,
paging and syscall interpretation, **not** a promise of physical Haswell timing,
cache residency, machine-check hardware or every model-specific feature. Model-
specific Linux initialization remains a real compatibility obligation below.
It is not bypassed by calling the model virtual.

One socket, one thread per core, configurable N logical CPUs, initially N=2 for
SMP, with1<=N<=64 and N additionally bounded by actual platform capacity and the
accepted resource envelope before VM creation. No CPU hotplug is exposed.
APIC/x2APIC IDs are unique0..N-1 and identical across CPUID, MADT, LAPIC routing
and startup mailboxes. CPUID.1's logical-count field is N and HT is set iff N>1;
HT here reports a package containing multiple logical processors, not a second
thread on a core. Leaf0BH subleaf0 reports one thread/shift0/SMT type; subleaf1
reports N/ceil(log2(N))/core type, with the full x2APIC ID in EDX. A terminating
level has zero count/type while retaining its specified subleaf/ID fields.
Sparse non-power-of-two core IDs are permitted by that shift; no fictitious
extra CPUs appear in firmware.

| Discovery group | Selected enabled names / consequence |
| --- | --- |
| CPUID.1 EDX | FPU, DE, PSE, TSC, MSR, PAE, MTRR, CX8, APIC, SEP, PGE, CMOV, PAT, PSE36, CLFSH, MMX, FXSR, SSE, SSE2; HT conditional as above. APIC EDX9 follows authoritative IA32_APIC_BASE global-enable bit11: disabling that bit clears discovery, while LAPIC SVR software-disable alone does not. |
| CPUID.1 ECX | SSE3, PCLMULQDQ, SSSE3, FMA, CX16, SSE4.1, SSE4.2, MOVBE, POPCNT, AES, XSAVE, AVX, F16C, RDRAND, x2APIC and hypervisor-present. OSXSAVE is dynamic from committed CR4.OSXSAVE, not a constant capability. |
| CPUID.7.0 | FSGSBASE, BMI1, AVX2, SMEP and BMI2 in their architectural fields; no further structured-feature subleaf. |
| CPUID.80000001 | NX, RDTSCP, long mode, LAHF/SAHF in64-bit mode and ABM/LZCNT. Intel SYSCALL/SYSRET EDX11 is1 only while executing in64-bit mode and0 otherwise, including LMA=1 compatibility execution; it is not a static capability bit in this profile. |
| CPUID.6 | ARAT, matching claim13's running LAPIC countdown; no frequency scaling, HWP or thermal-control promises. |
| CPUID.80000007 | Invariant TSC bit only, under the explicit virtual active-time/pause contract; no new host power-state behavior. |
| CPUID.80000008 | Physical40/linear48 from accepted memory42, no extra feature promises. |

This preserves x87/MMX/SSE through SSE4, full AVX/YMM state and AVX2, inherited
FMA/half-precision/AES/carryless-multiply behavior, modern integer operations,
RDRAND and SMEP. It does not narrow the FP surface to make a scalar pilot pass.
Floating-point implementation remains inherited from the selected semantic
component, with the known NaN-policy and state-image corrections/limits in
INV03-02; no handwritten FP is selected. RDRAND remains a typed entropy service
with its architectural success/failure result, not permission for a native helper
to call a host library from inside the VM. Device entropy is a separate machine
interface and is not replaced by RDRAND.

Deliberate optional choices relative to the historical model intersection:
MCE/MCA are **not exposed**—this VM does not model physical machine-check banks
or pass Apple hardware error state to Linux. XSAVEOPT and ERMS are also not
advertised: ordinary XSAVE/XRSTOR and ordinary REP string semantics remain fully
supported, but this profile promises neither modified-state save optimization
nor enhanced-REP performance. These are explicit surface/performance choices,
not claims that an adapter failed to implement mandatory behavior. Software
requiring those optional feature bits must detect their absence; compatibility
is for the declared profile, not every precompiled physical-Haswell binary.
