The workload acceptance check must retain that distinction. No existing required
x87/SSE/AVX operation is removed by these choices.

Keep PCID/INVPCID, LA57,1-GiB pages, SMAP, PKU/PKS, CET, AVX-512, MPX, TSX/HLE,
VMX/SVM, SGX, MONITOR/MWAIT, RDSEED, TSC_ADJUST, TSC-deadline, PMU and all power/
thermal/frequency-control families unadvertised. This adds no nested virtualization,
persistent-memory, memory-encryption or speculative-control MSR contract. Debug
registers, TF/RF and the selected guest debug behavior remain required regardless
of the absence of optional debug/performance MSRs.

#### CPUID shape, caches and extended-state layout

Basic maximum15H retains claim13's `(3,125,24000000,0)` TSC/crystal ratio; leaf16
is not advertised. Extended maximum80000008H covers the brand, invariant-clock
and width leaves. Hypervisor leaf40000000H identifies `NatemuNatemu`, with no
additional hypervisor feature leaves. Unsupported holes within advertised ranges
return zero; out-of-range ordinary basic/extended requests follow Intel's specified
highest-basic-leaf behavior, not arbitrary zero. Leaf/subleaf termination follows
the individual contract (notably7,0BH,0DH); no KVM/paravirtual feature is implied
merely by setting the hypervisor-present bit.

Select a virtual64-byte cache line for CLFLUSH/discovery and the guest cached
unaligned-access guarantee. It is not Apple's line size. Leaf4 describes private
32KiB8-way L1 data and instruction caches, private256KiB8-way L2, and shared8MiB
16-way L3, all with64-byte lines and one physical partition; sets are64,64,512,
8192 respectively. Sharing count is1 for private caches and N for L3; package
core count is N. Encode the count/line/partition/way/set fields as value-minus-one
where Table3-8 specifies it; cache type/level and self-initializing bit are explicit,
fully-associative is clear. Select EDX=1: no inclusion or complex-indexing promise,
and no guarantee of WBINVD/INVD acting on non-originating threads' lower caches.
A zero bit0 would add that lower-cache guarantee and is not silently assumed. Leaf2 returns EAX0x0000ff01 and other registers0, pointing cache discovery to
leaf4 rather than publishing a contradictory physical descriptor list.
Leaf80000005 is zero; leaf80000006 returns ECX0x01006040 (256KiB L2,8-way,
64-byte line), with its other registers0. These are a virtual discovery geometry over
the accepted coherent-backing cache abstraction, not physical capacity/performance
measurements. Leaf0AH PMU is zero.

XSAVE enumeration is finite: supported XCR0 mask0x7 (x87/SSE/AVX), no supervisor
state or IA32_XSS. Leaf0DH subleaf0 has EAX7/EDX0/ECX832; EBX is832 with AVX enabled
and576 otherwise. Subleaf1 is zero because XSAVEOPT/XSAVEC/XGETBV1/XSAVES/XFD are
not exposed. Subleaf2 reports size256, standard offset576, user-state flags0;
other component subleaves are zero. XCR0 must retain bit0, and bit2 requires bit1;
XSETBV, CR4.OSXSAVE and instruction admission use claim12's actual rules.

The authoritative state includes all architectural integer/segment/control/debug
state plus complete x87/MMX, MXCSR, sixteen XMM and sixteen upper-YMM halves, their
valid/deferred representations and required status. Disabled XCR0 components do
not vanish from runtime ownership merely because an instruction cannot currently
use them. FXSAVE/FXRSTOR remain their512-byte formats; XSAVE/XRSTOR use standard
legacy512+header64+YMM-high256 layout, reserved-header validation and component
initialization rules. Exact partial-store/fault precedence stays with INV03-02's
instruction/state-image owner, including its accepted limited alignment choices;
this leaf table is not permission to fake whole-image atomicity or arbitrary
helper rollback.

#### State mutation, MSR and mode interfaces

Claims12/42 bind CR0/CR3/CR4/EFER and hidden PAE state, all four required paging
modes,40-bit masks, NX/WP/SMEP, A/D, PAT/MTRR and invalidation. Real mode remains
the owner's standing exclusion, not an accidental new #GP for legal protected-
mode startup transitions. CPUID/control state/entry keys derive from the same
committed tuple. FSGSBASE and FS/GS MSR writes share appropriate canonical-value
validation while preserving their different privilege and mode rules.

The architectural MSR surface uses the profile-owned whitelist/validation layer
from claim09. The consequential families are fixed, with state owned as follows:

| Admitted family | Owner and required coupling |
| --- | --- |
| TSC0x10, TSC_AUX0xc0000103 | Claim13's common time/per-vCPU offset/AUX state; AUX upper32 reserved. No TSC_ADJUST/deadline acceptance. |
| APIC_BASE0x1b and implemented x2APIC indices | Authoritative LAPIC mode/register owner, respecting holes/read-only/write-only/reserved fields and exact EOI/ICR effects; no invented second APIC copy. |
| SYSENTER0x174–176; EFER/STAR/LSTAR/CSTAR/FMASK0xc0000080–84; FS/GS/KERNEL_GS_BASE0xc0000100–102 | Validated syscall/segment/control state, exact Intel mode/canonicality/privilege rules; CSTAR remains defined but unused by the selected Intel compatibility SYSCALL behavior. |
| PAT0x277, MTRRCAP0xfe, DEF_TYPE0x2ff, eight variable pairs0x200–20f and fixed ranges0x250/258/259/268–26f | Memory42's width40/type/range/policy contract. MTRRCAP read-only reports VCNT8/FIX/WC; updates commit generation/invalidation before reuse. PAT INIT retention is distinct from cold reset. |
| Required compatibility identity/control registers | MISC_ENABLE, microcode identity and any model-required platform register must be given exact fields/reset/effects in the remaining seam below; a broad permissive upstream default is forbidden. |

Absent MSRs #GP(0); privilege and reserved/write-protection checks precede mutation.
MCE/MCA/PMU/VMX/power facilities are not exposed by accepting an upstream switch
case. Disabled instruction features follow their actual decode rules: many
feature-specific encodings #UD, but legacy-compatible hints/prefixes can retain
legacy behavior (for example HLE prefixes are not a reason to invent #UD on the
underlying otherwise legal instruction). Unknown CPUID, unknown MSR and unsupported
opcode are three different contracts. MCE disablement must agree with CR4.MCE
admission and absent MCG/bank registers, not leave a writable advertised facility.

BSP direct entry must publish one valid prepared architectural tuple and boot
manifest; AP startup uses the accepted native/system-emulated INIT/SIPI mailbox
semantics, not execution of unsupported real-mode code. Descriptor tables,
segments, stack, APIC ID and boot-visible memory map must agree with that tuple.
The prepared long-mode entry is not falsely called hardware reset, and ordinary
Linux startup32 remains supported. HLT, events, I/O permissions and system
instructions keep the selected semantic/event owners; hardware TSO and EL1
execution do not replace their guest privilege checks.

#### Compatibility rationale, exact stop and next step

The selected integer/FP/state/paging surface supports the intended modern Linux
x86-64/SMP and AVX2-capable workload classes without inheriting an end-to-end
QEMU machine. Linux can select ordinary XSAVE and REP paths when optimization
flags are absent; this is a compatibility rationale, not a stock-boot observation.
The retained Linux v6.12 CPUID15 calibration path is already bound to24MHz LAPIC
input/1GHz TSC. PAE/startup32, NX, SMEP, syscalls and complete vector state are
not omitted to match an incomplete fixture. Firmware and driver configuration
remain separately owned machine contracts.

**Genuine remaining profile seam:** the family6/model0x3c identity reaches Intel
initialization that accesses IA32_MISC_ENABLE, and microcode/platform identity
paths, beyond pure feature-leaf interpretation. Claim09's retained Linux source
already demonstrates the mandatory MISC_ENABLE read and CPUID-limit/fast-string
interaction; some other platform probes are safe but must still receive their
selected #GP or value consistently. This proposal does not invent their complete
bit/reset/write behavior without the primary register contract. Therefore it
selects a concrete CPU exposure/state shape but **does not yet close question1's
complete guest-visible MSR/identity contract**. That is an exact missing interface,
not a demand to implement every instruction before deciding the architecture.

Next bounded action: root reviews this profile choice, including the explicit
optional-feature differences and topology/state layout. If accepted, one bounded
primary-source pass should settle the finite MISC_ENABLE/microcode/platform
compatibility-register table and its Linux call sites for this chosen identity,
then reconcile the complete profile. Actual opcode/helper conformance, full FP
compatibility and guest event/fault delivery remain their named architectural
contracts; no source-level exposure list or unexecuted adapter proves them.
No native execution, feature-filter fallback or broad opcode inventory follows
from this submission.


**Pre-review invariant corrections:** root identified the omitted MTRR name in
the original CPUID.1 table and the Intel mode-dependent SYSCALL discovery flag;
both are corrected above. A third root return adds CPUID.1 APIC discovery's
dependency on IA32_APIC_BASE global enable (Intel092§13.4.3), distinct from SVR
software disable. Author rechecked CPUID Table3-8's cache minus-one
encodings, nearest-power-of-two ID-space rules,0BH level/count termination,
80000006 L2 encoding and0DH component size/offset rules. The chosen cache sizes
are64×1×8×64=32KiB,64×1×8×512=256KiB and64×1×16×8192=8MiB. N<=64 fits the
six-bit core-count-minus-one field; ceil(log2(N)) agrees with the rounded ID
space without inventing firmware CPUs. The optional XSAVE-optimization absence
makes subleaf0DH.1 EBX0 appropriate; the supported standard component ends at
576+256=832, while x87/SSE-only standard storage retains its512+64 prefix.
No measured cache or native instruction result is added by these static checks.


## Compatibility-register closure proposal

### CLM-03-01-17 — Finite virtual MISC_ENABLE and microcode/platform policy

**Author:** memory, September20; independent root review accepted. Claim16's
corrected profile is the input. This selects the remaining virtual compatibility
register behavior; it does not copy the full physical Haswell MSR inventory or
change the declared instruction features. Source inspection only, no runtime.

Primary MSR definitions are Intel335592-092US Table2-2 (platform ID, update
trigger/signature and generic miscellaneous controls) and Table2-20's
IA32_MISC_ENABLE row, pp.2-231–232. §2.13 explicitly makes Table2-20 applicable
to model3c alongside the listed overrides. That model row marks bits6:1 reserved;
copying generic thermal-control bit3 from Table2-2 would therefore be wrong.
Its XD-disable34 definition refers to Table2-3. CPUID visibility under bit22 is
also specified in253666-082US Table3-8.
[Intel MSR primary](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922493/335592-092-sdm-vol-4.pdf),
[Intel CPUID primary](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/812383/253666-sdm-vol-2a.pdf).

The existing Linux v6.12 `intel.c`/`common.c` copies remain in
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-msr-audit`. Three additional official v6.12 microcode
files and their identities are retained under
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-cpu-compat-01/sources.json`. Initial sandbox DNS and
web-header misses are recorded there; subsequent read-only fetch succeeded.
No source was executed.

#### IA32_MISC_ENABLE: exact virtual field policy

Select per-vCPU register0x1a0 with cold-reset value0x1801. It has exactly two
writable control fields, bit0 and bit22, plus fixed status bits. All writes retain
the ordinary WRMSR privilege/prefix/exception-origin checks and serialize the
applicable context before subsequent guest instruction admission.

| Field | Read/reset | Write and effect |
| --- | --- | --- |
|0, Fast-Strings Enable |1 initially | R/W. Retain its value; clearing forbids a selected fast-string execution optimization but leaves every ordinary REP instruction and its precise partial effects supported. Setting does not expose ERMS, which remains absent. No throughput is guaranteed by either value. |
|7, Performance Monitoring Available |0 | Read-only fixed0, matching absent PMU. Writes to this read-only status position are ignored, never enable counters. |
|11, BTS unavailable;12, PEBS unavailable |1/1 | Read-only fixed1/1. Writes to these status positions are ignored; no debug-store buffers or host performance facilities appear. |
|22, Limit CPUID Maxval |0 initially | R/W. When set, basic maximum becomes2 and ordinary basic queries above2 use the limited highest-basic-leaf response. When cleared, claim16's maximum15H and normal leaves return. Extended leaves retain their architectural namespace, and the explicitly virtual hypervisor namespace remains a separate convention. Commit CPUID dispatch generation with the bit. |
|34, XD disable |fixed0, not a writable facility in this virtual profile | Any attempt to set it gets #GP(0), preserving old state. NX remains available as selected in16 and EFER.NXE remains governed by12/42. Do not silently clear EFER.NXE or change reserved-PTE interpretation. |
|16 EIST,18 MONITOR FSM,23 xTPR,38 turbo; all other positions |0 | Not implemented in this virtual surface; attempting to set any of them or a reserved bit gets #GP(0), before state mutation. No broad writable mask copied from upstream or physical-model storage. |

Equivalently, write validation first rejects any set bit outside
`{0,7,11,12,22}`; it then commits only0/22 and reinstates fixed status0/1/1 at7/11/12.
That rule accepts Linux's read/modify/write of bit22 without pretending status
bits are writable controls. Ignoring writes to the explicit read-only status
positions is a selected virtual interface rule; it is not a claim that every
physical-model reserved/read-only bit is universally write-ignored.

**Explicit XD-control scope:** physical Haswell's table includes writable34 and
its CPUID.NX coupling. The virtual profile does **not** select that BIOS disable
facility. It selects NX always available and the documented EFER.NXE enable
interface, keeping the CPU feature set unchanged. This is an intentional reduced
MSR control surface, not a claim that physical bit34 has no effect. In particular,
primary text inspected here gives CPUID clearing but does not settle every
active-NXE transition; no guessed transition is inserted merely to close the
profile. A future request to expose34 must bind that behavior and its paging
invalidation explicitly. Modern Linux's selected normal initialization need
not disable NX through this register.

Cold reset initializes the two controls; INIT preserves this compatibility
register in the selected virtual lifecycle. A prepared BSP entry records actual
values instead of assuming it executed physical firmware. Changes are local to
the logical CPU's state; they do not rewrite other vCPUs' CPUID-limit state.

#### Signature, platform identity and update trigger

| MSR | Selected read/reset/write behavior |
| --- | --- |
|0x17 IA32_PLATFORM_ID | Read-only0: platform-ID field52:50 is0 and reserved fields are0. Any write #GP(0). This is a virtual identity, not Apple platform metadata. |
|0x8b IA32_BIOS_SIGN_ID | Per-vCPU64-bit latch, reset0. Low32 reserved and remain0. WRMSR with nonzero low32 #GP(0); valid writes store the upper32 preload only. Executing CPUID leaf1 refreshes upper32 to the fixed virtual revision0, keeping low32 zero. Other CPUID leaves do not impersonate this refresh. No write changes CPU semantics or loads host code. |
|0x79 IA32_BIOS_UPDT_TRIG | No guest microcode-update engine is selected; read/write #GP(0) with no guest-buffer dereference and no host operation. Do not fabricate success, load an Intel patch into the Apple host or bump a revision without changing semantics. |
|0xce MSR_PLATFORM_INFO;0x140 MSR_MISC_FEATURES_ENABLES | Absent: read/write #GP(0). No CPUID-faulting or power/frequency feature is inferred from the model number. These are distinct from supported architectural IA32_PLATFORM_ID0x17. |

Revision0 means no virtual microcode update is loaded. It does not claim a
physical Intel patch number or vulnerability-mitigation level. Security-feature
MSRs remain absent as16 states. Guest-triggered host firmware updates are outside
this interface entirely; neither a guest pointer nor an MSR payload grants such
authority. If supporting another guest that insists on updating virtual microcode
becomes a requirement, the policy must be reconsidered rather than silently
acknowledging an ineffective update.

#### Required versus safe Linux paths

Existing v6.12 `intel.c` performs the MISC_ENABLE read for fast-string policy and
uses `msr_clear_bit` on the CPUID-limit control during Intel initialization. The
selected register supplies those operations. Its safe probes of
MSR_MISC_FEATURES_ENABLES and MSR_PLATFORM_INFO do not justify exposing them:
the former failure returns before its follow-on writes; the latter failure does
not advertise CPUID faulting. This is the precise call-site distinction already
required by claim09, not a claim that every possible third-party driver ignores
unsupported model MSRs.
[Linux Intel initialization](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/cpu/intel.c).

The separate `intel_get_microcode_revision` helper writes0 to0x8b, executes
CPUID1, then reads its high32 bits. Thus microcode **identity reads still need a
valid interface** even though updates are disabled. The selected latch supplies
that sequence truthfully.
[Linux revision helper](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/include/asm/microcode.h).

Linux v6.12 `check_loader_disabled_bsp` returns on the hypervisor-present bit;
`dis_ucode_ldr` stays true, AP loading checks it, and late `microcode_init` exits
when it is true. This explains why the selected unmodified Linux path does not
attempt the rejected update trigger. It is not a promise that arbitrary privileged
guest code cannot explicitly issue that WRMSR.
[Linux loader admission](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/cpu/microcode/core.c).

The Intel loader's CPU-info path reads platform ID0x17 and the revision helper;
its actual update path writes0x79 then checks the resulting revision. Those are
separate from safe probes of0xce. The exposed ID read is supported, while the
update engine is deliberately not entered by the selected hypervisor-marked
Linux configuration.
[Linux Intel update path](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/cpu/microcode/intel.c).

#### Closure and review boundary

This resolves16's finite compatibility-register interface with exact field,
reset, write, discovery and side-effect behavior. The choice requires independent
review, particularly the explicit omission of XD-disable and guest update controls;
it is not unchanged physical-Haswell emulation. Together with12/13/16 and memory42,
it supplies the proposed virtual CPU exposure/state/control contract. Actual
helper masks, CPUID routing, reset integration, guest exception precision and FP/
state-image conformance remain their named owners' duties. No stock Linux boot,
full user-program compatibility, performance or combined architecture acceptance
is claimed by these source paths.

**Next bounded action:** root independently reviews17 and whether this explicit
virtual compatibility subset closes question1's profile contract. No new opcode
inventory, native probe or implementation follows automatically.


**Claim16 progression:** root accepted the corrected scoped exposure proposal;
claim17 above is the submitted compatibility-register follow-on. The earlier
“review then compatibility pass” direction is completed, not a request to repeat
that work. Independent review of17 is pending.


**Claim17 review disposition:** root independently accepted the explicit virtual
register subset and scoped question1 profile contract. The compatibility seam
is closed; the review does not establish physical-model equivalence, actual
helper conformance or guest boot. Continue the separate semantic, event-reset
and lifecycle contracts rather than repeating this finite register inventory.
## Combined construction — integration submission

**Method:** root, September20, bounded composition of the current accepted
contracts, with questions2/5 expressly incomplete. No new source claim, runtime
experiment or implementation. Independent review must test the interactions,
not count accepted component claims. This supersedes the earlier interaction
table as the current proposed argument; its adversarial cases remain falsifiers.

### CLM-03-01-18 — Compose the selected owners without importing an upstream runtime

**Historical component binding, superseded by claim20’s current recomposition.**
The original conditional integration was accepted in the translator review. Host TCG
generation targets guarded resident EL1 execution. QEMU supplies the adapted
frontend/semantic provenance and SSE/AVX numerical components; Bochs supplies
the selected full80 trig/scale/rounded-x87 numerical slice. Neither upstream
CPU loop owns guest execution. One resident per-vCPU owner carries architectural
state, working roots, semantic-operation progress and event disposition.
The selected CPU profile and direct-boot machine remain those in claims16–17
and INV03-03 claim69; the generator cannot silently narrow their requirements.

| Charter interaction | Concrete composition and failure test |
| --- | --- |
| CPU state, dispatch and privilege | Claim12 commits control-state changes together with derived shadow/context admission;14 keys specialized assumptions and guards every external link;15 checks guest address spans and native control targets. A retained old link cannot authorize entry after a mode/context change. Guest x86 privilege remains data, never native EL1 authority. |
| Recovery and temporary state | Claim15 captures native roots before the permanent landing clobbers them;02-37/43–50/53 distinguish repair, completed effects, architectural faults and numerical publication. Native service pauses do not retire instructions. Whole coverage remains conditional on the compound-progress mapping and debug ordering under question5; this row does not manufacture their missing proof. |
| All writers and aliases |04-26/40–42 and14 give CPU writes, helper writes, A/D effects and admitted backend commits the same source/shadow mutation authority. Host work uses snapshots/staging outside that authority; completion reacquires a bounded grant. A new writer or alias must join this protocol before it can operate. |
| Remapping and split accesses |04-41 holds coordinator ownership from the first PTE read through its access result and eligible publication. Instruction owners choose actual subaccess boundaries; they do not preflight an entire gather/REP merely because one scalar access uses preflight. Earlier A/D/device effects survive later faults, and each later access receives current admission. |
| Host/guest ordering and MMIO |04-40 supplies native TSO, width-specific atomicity, barriers and all-observer service;03-73 publishes admitted payload/status/ring effects before notification. Completed register transactions are distinct from the asynchronous disk/network operations they trigger. Retry cannot repeat a retained external effect, and a flush cannot be acknowledged from mere queue admission. |
| Translation and source mutation |11/14 capture immutable compilation inputs, retain source identity and serialize final registration/publication with writers. The host compiles outside the coordinator. Mutation before publication rejects the result; mutation after registration finds and invalidates it before required guest visibility. |
| Retirement and references |14 counts active execution, loaded links, returns, fault/helper continuations and publication references before reuse. Memory quiescence is not code-reclaimability: a paused helper can acknowledge a drain while retaining its object pin.02's semantic continuations therefore cannot be treated as dead merely because they are outside generated code. |
| Events, waits and locks |15's permanent wait/service path and03's accepted event contract process native management while compilation or guest interrupt masking persists.04-41 forbids holding coordinator ownership across disk/network waits, guest execution or reset drain. A backend needing guest progress must release its grant and use staged work; it cannot solve that wait cycle with a timeout masquerading as guest success. |
| Boot, topology and time |03-69 derives CPU IDs, MADT/mailbox routes and devices from one launch manifest;01-13 and03-61–62 bind TSC/crystal/timers to one active-time epoch. Ordinary coordination pauses do not freeze the clock. Whole-VM pause/sleep policy and timer receiver cuts are retained, rather than independently pausing only one timer source. |
| Failure and reuse |03-70/73 revoke an incarnation before teardown, retain outstanding storage/transport/callback ownership and apply04-29 durability. New VM/queue/code identities cannot reuse old authority while references survive. A stuck or failed worker may terminate its VM; neither cancellation nor cold reboot rolls back issued disk writes or claims a successful flush. |

**Trust and capability composition.** Claim15 trusts the compiler, resident
helpers and publication logic to enforce the VM's private-address/control-flow
invariants; it does not promise to sandbox malicious native generated code inside
that same EL1 runtime. Question12 separately contains the host worker to its
explicit per-VM grants. The trusted supervisor, OS enforcement, ICMP matcher and
DNS provider stay outside the fallible worker boundary, with73's distinct role
manifests. A compromised network stack gets ordinary authorized TCP/UDP network
access, not raw ICMP fanout, task rights, disk objects or guest RAM. These are
different boundaries and neither is used to prove the other.

**Platform and reuse premises remain visible.** The applicable host is the named
M4/macOS26 regime.04-39's aligned128 operational atomicity premise is broader
than its finite observations; it is not a private stage-2 attribute proof.
Hardware TSO and required HVF/service/policy behavior retain their named contracts.
Numerical-component conformance is a disclosed reuse premise, not a universal
error certificate. A failed premise reopens its dependent mechanisms; no silent
fallback changes the ISA, device protocol or isolation boundary. Missing M2 parity
narrows applicability and remains with its existing measurement owner.

**Implementation direction and costs.** Build the selected resident ownership,
checked access/recovery and immutable-publication interfaces around the existing
generator and numerical components; adapt the explicitly identified helpers and
staged devices. Preserve normal native memory hits and resident service calls;
no per-instruction host round trip or universal canonical spill is selected.
Costs include address guards, root preservation, compilation/register transport,
coordinator misses, conservative fences and numerical/device adapters. Their
frequency, latency and engineering effort are not measured here. FEX/Box64,
alternative transport/privilege choices and vmnet retain their recorded
conditional alternatives; the selected policy establishes no comparative winner.

**Historical stopping boundary for this original binding.** This supplied a reviewable interaction argument,
not final architecture acceptance. Questions2/5 remain open on their specific
semantic/composition work. Independent integration review accepts the stated conditional composition; the
charter's product/architecture/correctness/methodology breadth check remains required.
Final performance/economic judgement and authorization of planning belong to
synthesis with Tracks04/05 inputs; an architectural contract is not measured
end-to-end superiority or permission to implement.


### CLM-03-01-19 — Queued faults and bounded drain before mutation

**Architectural revision independently accepted by `/root/decision_audit`,
September20, after admitted-route and fairness corrections.** This changes
claim14's universal instruction-commit polling. It reuses INV03-04 rendezvous
revision2's memory-quiescent PAUSED frames and scoped DRAIN_SERVICE; it does not
reintroduce revision1's superseded canonical-only drain or claim that lazy native
state retention is a new discovery. It retains claim14's object
identity, guarded external entries, immutable code, registered references and
serialized mutation authority. It does not select a generator, weaken guest
fault semantics, or accept any emitter's complete recovery implementation.

**Why the old proof paid too much.** The old construction lets a paused A finish
after mutation and therefore needs a checkpoint before stale B. An alternative
is to leave the mutation uncommitted while unaffected CPUs finish bounded old-view
execution, and to retain the faulting CPU without continuing into B. Ordinary
peers cannot execute stale B after mutation because they are drained first; the
faulting CPU must take a cold completion/exit rather than resume arbitrary native
code after mutation. This trades cold recovery and bounded mutation latency for
removing the unconditional per-instruction pending-load/branch requirement.

#### State and authority

Keep the existing coordinator OPEN/CLOSING/EXCLUSIVE/RELEASING epochs and one
FIFO transaction queue. Split two facts already distinguished by04claim11:
**active access authority** and **retained references**. An already admitted
route also carries a **completion obligation**, distinct from both: its remaining
subaccess must complete against that admitted route before a conflicting remap
can commit/acknowledge or reuse its backing, unless the semantic owner proves
effect-free cancellation is permitted. An inactive vCPU may retain its
native frame, code/metadata pin and typed operation while holding no RAM grant.
It is not reclaimable merely because it is inactive.

Use the existing states rather than adding a second state machine. ACTIVE owns
old-epoch access authority; DRAINING remains active until actual quiescence.
PAUSED retains a native frame and pinned dependencies with no native memory effect
in flight. A queued fault is PAUSED with the existing typed pending descriptor,
not a completed guest instruction. PARKED keeps its stronger canonical/no-native-
reference meaning for reclamation. A forced HVF exit alone establishes neither.

A pending descriptor contains VM/vCPU/operation identity, current guest instruction
and immutable admitted decode identity, captured native roots/stack, source and
route references, completed effects, next subaccess, required atomicity class and
continuation disposition. Recovery metadata must reconstruct the instruction's
state without guessing that resident canonical slots are current. These are
existing02/04 recovery obligations exercised on the cold path, not new success
assumptions based on the body probe.

#### Closing and draining

1. A tracked write, shadow/service miss, fallback atomic or backend commit is
   prepared without its pending protected effect. Queue it, close new admission,
   and publish a durable stop request. No vCPU waits with the coordinator mutex
   held. Fault capture prevents later guest execution on that vCPU.
2. ACTIVE peers finish under the unchanged old view to a **bounded exit**.
   All external object entries, including warmed links, check closed admission.
   Every internal CFG cycle has a bounded stop opportunity; REP and helpers keep
   their legal partial-progress safepoints. Finite object byte size alone does
   not bound a loop. No unchecked recursion or blocking external I/O holds an
   active guest-RAM grant. Instructions that alter relevant CPU/control state
   take their existing semantic exit. Stop opportunities preserve native roots;
   normal execution does not force canonical publication.
3. A peer that faults while draining queues its descriptor too. It does not wait
   for its own fault to be serviced before acknowledging inactivity, provided
   its access phase is safely detachable. Native frame/code retention is separate
   from active-access accounting. Completed memory work receives the existing
   completion barrier before release acknowledgement; exclusive acquisition
   uses the matching established ordering contract.
4. An operation with outstanding indivisible effects is not detachable merely
   because it faulted. Complete its bounded already-admitted unit using retained
   route authority before acknowledgement, or use the existing checked atomic
   service before beginning that unit. A split lowering of an operation promised
   atomic must never publish a prefix and then wait for this queue. Non-atomic
   architectural prefixes may be retained and resumed according to their typed
   semantics. A helper holding a lock needed by drain must release it or finish
   its bounded unit first. These are admission/lowering rules; inability to meet
   them for an ordinary supported instruction is a design counterexample.
5. Only after all old active access grants have been relinquished may EXCLUSIVE
   begin. Queued admitted-route completion obligations remain registered; entry
   into EXCLUSIVE does not discharge them or authorize a conflicting mutation.
   Queued native frames do not prevent exclusive mutation, but their references
   prevent freeing/reusing the storage they name. Outstanding backend/native
   memory accesses do prevent EXCLUSIVE and must actually finish; a saved PC or
   a queued host callback is not a completion acknowledgement.

#### Commit and resume

Inside EXCLUSIVE, first discharge any retained admitted-route obligation that
conflicts with the head mutation through a bounded permanent maintenance grant,
using the old admitted route. Its completed partial effects remain recorded. A
permitted proven-effect-free cancellation can discharge the obligation instead;
merely observing a native fault cannot. These dependency completions precede a
conflicting FIFO mutation and its invalidation acknowledgement; they are not new
independently queued transactions. If an obligation cannot finish or cancel under
these rules, that conflicting mutation cannot commit. Only unadmitted next
subaccesses resolve afresh. Thus a frame may be memory-quiescent while still
preventing a specific remap, without keeping every peer active.

Then service the head request against the current admitted access
semantics, including the existing permission/A-D/route rules and preserved prefix.
Never reuse a raw old native address as authority after a route change. Complete
nested A/D/semantic work inside that transaction; do not queue a request and wait
on the same transaction. If a later queued request's premises changed, classify
it again under its instruction owner, preserving already committed effects and
architecturally retained access decisions. Revalidation is not retroactive erasure
of an earlier completed access or a new blanket x86 TLB policy.

A mutation may make a queued frame's following translated code ineligible.
The default after a coordinated queued operation is **cold completion of only
its remaining instruction effects, then current-source dispatch**. Use the saved
admitted decode/progress, not a fresh decode of a self-modified current instruction.
Do not replay the instruction from its beginning if it already has visible effects.
A native interior continuation is admissible only when its typed phase/route and
code identity prove it finishes the same operation and cannot enter stale following
instructions. Otherwise the existing resident semantic owner completes the suffix.
An internal shadow repair with unchanged relevant identities may use its qualified
retry continuation; guest fault delivery remains the separate architectural path.

Queued REP may retain its admitted decoded operation and already completed
iterations as04claim11 permits. It uses checked future accesses under the current
memory owner and legal internal safepoints; eventual completion or architectural
fault/event exits to current-source dispatch. Guest-facing boundary RIP never
replaces a native PC-relative anchor required for a retained continuation.

An ordinary asynchronous interrupt remains pending until its eligible bounded
boundary; synchronous traps, enabled single-step/watchpoint conditions and
instruction-specific debug/interrupt-shadow obligations retain their exact
architectural boundaries through specialized lowering, object termination or
cold service. They are not deferred merely because a normal object is longer.
For asynchronous delivery,
control transitions that unmask it keep their specific exit obligations. A queued
synchronous exception never licenses later guest instructions to retire first.
No error is converted to success to make the queue drain.

#### Adversarial composition and progress

| Case | Required outcome |
| --- | --- |
| Two CPUs fault on tracked stores during closure | Both retain descriptors and relinquish safely detachable access grants. Neither waits active for the other. FIFO commits one, then revalidates the second; no recursive rendezvous. |
| A writes its own following B | A's protected write remains pending while peers execute old code. After drain, commit A once; cold-complete A and dispatch the new B. Never return to the old A-to-B fallthrough. |
| Mapping mutation with a queued old pointer | Frame pins retain storage lifetime. Discharge an already admitted route before conflicting remap/ack/reuse; only an unadmitted next access can rebind. Never silently switch an admitted R access to S. Completed prefix remains completed. |
| Partial MMIO or compound instruction | Record exactly completed transactions and resume only its suffix. No speculative MMIO, replay, rollback fiction or whole-instruction preflight. A genuinely unfinished indivisible unit prevents acknowledgement. |
| Internal branch/REP/helper loop | Bound work between legal safepoints and inspect the durable stop request there. Arbitrarily many block-internal iterations cannot hide from closure. |
| Fault frame pins a retired object | Invalidation removes new eligibility; pin keeps old metadata/frame storage alive. Reclaim only after permanent completion/abandonment removes native references. |
| Continued requests arrive while exclusive | Service at most one queued head per transaction/revalidation round. After at most8 heads, grant a waiting ordinary vCPU a round-robin admission opportunity for one bounded interval (or until it faults/exits), before closing for another batch. Mutex unlock alone is insufficient; the interval must actually be admitted and allowed to progress under healthy scheduling. New requests queue meanwhile; required pending faults stay serviceable. No guest execution is needed to acknowledge quiescence. |

For finite V vCPUs, admit at most one active queued guest operation per vCPU;
reserve its descriptor/frame before native entry. Nested semantic work reuses
that descriptor. Existing finite backend/compile slots retain their own reserves.
Queued old/destination/operation references remain within claim14's declared
three-reference census; any lowering requiring another persistent reference must
extend the declared capacity proof before admission. Invalidation cannot allocate
an unbounded chain of cold copies: permanent semantic service consumes the pinned
instruction description. Emergency completion storage is reserved in advance.

Under healthy host scheduling and those bounded lowering premises, closure ends:
each running CPU either reaches a bounded exit or becomes a safely queued PAUSED fault;
none needs a queued transaction to finish before releasing a detachable grant.
EXCLUSIVE therefore need not wait for canonicalization or code reclamation.
The protocol gives no real-time host scheduling guarantee, no measured speedup,
and no full-ISA implementation proof. Failure of a detachable-phase or bounded
completion premise must be addressed in that lowering; VM termination remains
failure containment, not normal instruction semantics.

**Decision and applicability:** select this independently accepted queued
construction instead of universal per-instruction checkpoint polling. Retain
checks at external admissions and bounded internal cycles/required semantic exits.
The old guarded/body timing remains exploratory and incomplete, but a probe that
adds five unconditional checkpoints solely from superseded claim14 would price
the wrong design. Actual adapter cold completion, checkpoint spacing, contention
and event rates remain consequential comparison inputs. This revision applies to
the fixed captive architecture and existing M4 mechanism premises; it adds no
native experiment, cross-host result or candidate ranking.

### CLM-03-01-20 — Performance-led component choice and current recomposition

**Selection/completion withdrawn after the owner's extension-versus-hybrid
challenge, September21.** The prior independent acceptance established the
following hybrid's conditional construction and finite cost premise, but did
not compare it against extending FEX. Retained historical proposal: choose
FEX for claim56's ordinary native domain, with claim57's guarded native scalar
arithmetic where admitted. Use claim58's concrete QEMU frontend/TCG service
objects and resident checked helpers for the remaining exposed instructions,
startup32, compound effects and numerical slow paths. Retain the existing TCG
SQRT lowering when already in its service/state binding and as the measured
alternative. Its body advantage does not justify a FEX-to-TCG transition at every
SQRT: that choice must include materialization, admission and reload costs, which
can exceed the roughly1.4ns measured body saving. The native path remains available
without claiming it wins SQRT. This is an implementation direction inside the
fixed captive architecture.

**Observed comparative premise:** independently reviewed and replicated guarded
primary/replica each pass204 admission and294 long output checks. Both lengths
in both runs show separated costs for FEX's tested branches, add/sub, mul/div
and restricted unsigned DIV versus Box64/TCG; TCG wins SQRT. Four-median ranges
in nominal ns/body: FEX branches0.624–0.745 versus Box1.010–1.752 andTCG1.793–2.252;
FEX add/sub1.239–1.354 versusBox2.243–2.473/TCG6.221–6.290; FEX mul/div3.143–3.299
versusBox4.037–4.260/TCG7.732–7.997. TCG SQRT1.546–1.609 versusFEX2.948–3.087 and
Box2.916–3.097. These are particular dependency shapes and matched sticky-inexact
status, including native control/input/result guards and staged rejection. The
finite evidence is accepted in the existing findings thread; it is not a full
translated workload or a claim that every instruction is faster.

**Inferred preference and alternatives:** these ordinary shapes give a measured
reason to pay for FEX's recovery adaptation. Claim56 establishes bounded source
construction without mandatory dense canonical spills; claim58 supplies concrete
system/compound execution without narrowing the guest ISA to the native subset.
Box64 has no measured advantage in these selected ordinary shapes that would
justify preferring its adaptation. TCG remains the service engine and fallback
ordinary generator if native eligibility/recovery costs erase the gain. Its
system coverage is a reuse fact, not evidence of ordinary speed. This chooses
where to invest implementation effort; it does not assume a workload mix.

The cost condition is explicit: sum native-shape frequency times its measured
saving must exceed extra recovery moves/spills, service entry/exit conversions,
miss/compile costs and the engineering/footprint burden of two producers. The
minimum separated branch saving in this finite matrix is roughly0.26ns/body
against Box64 and1.04ns/body againstTCG; a mandatory cost of that scale can erase
the corresponding shape's gain. That is a sensitivity bound, not a workload
estimate or interchangeable-instruction average. Unsupported native shapes earn
no saving. Dense canonical state publication at every boundary previously
reversed the finite ordering; this design does not require that policy, but
actual frequent service can recreate its cost. Low-reuse and service-heavy
workloads can favorTCG. Tracks04/05 own actual eligibility, boundary frequency,
compilation and workload measurements; synthesis owns whether expected value
justifies planning. No numerical compile ranking follows from unmatched builds.

**x87 choice:** use claim59's bounded inherited binary64 numerical paths with
lazy instruction-specific C1 and full-status fallback outside their admitted
domain. This honors the owner's cost-over-accuracy priority without a blanket
full80 requirement or an assumed status waiver. The earlier numerical probe
shows why cheaper paths merit use: the selected cancellation fixture loses the
unit residual at exponents53–63 under binary64; full80 retains it. Its primitive
cost difference and libc-versus-extended sine comparison motivate this policy,
but do not price claim59's guards, descriptor or service bridge. Numerical
accuracy-dependent workloads and frequent status observers can use the strict
provider; no application tolerance or whole-adapter speedup is claimed. Preserve
that numerical limitation in synthesis and eventual product disclosure.

**Current binding of claim18:** this paragraph supersedes its historical
TCG-ordinary/Bochs-full80 selection premises. Its resident authority, CPU profile,
boot, memory, ordering, device, clock and containment owners remain unchanged.
Claims56–58 bind actual native roots, staged FP state and checked service to
that owner; claim19 binds queued recovery/drain and lifetime; claim59 adds one
copied-value numerical-status record with no RAM/code pin. Host compilation is
outside active RAM grants/EXCLUSIVE, completion handlers are reserved before
admission, and both producers share the existing job/reference caps. A service
handoff publishes the current state, releases the old native return reference,
and reloads every modified architectural field before current-source dispatch.
No arbitrary native continuation survives a conflicting remap. Admitted MMIO
and partial effects finish only their remaining suffix before conflicting reuse.
All ten interaction rows of claim18 apply under these replacement bindings.

Retained Intel-specific MOV-SS/ICEBP, ENTER prefix and protected14-byte FOP
unknowns remain disclosed compatibility details. They do not distinguish the
selected native envelope from its inherited checked service and do not justify
Haswell acquisition as a completion gate. No Intel-silicon equivalence is claimed.
A concrete target-kernel/application dependence reopens its owning semantic
choice. M4/macOS applicability, aligned128 operational premise, private policy
contracts and M2 measurement limits retain their earlier scope.

**Completion judgment:** independent review accepts questions2/5 and the updated
combined architecture under these comparative, source and interaction premises. Retain actual adapter conformance and
workload economics for implementation validation and synthesis, respectively.
This is not authorization to implement or a claim of end-to-end superiority.


### Track03 completion and synthesis handoff — September21

**Historical closure, subsequently withdrawn for the missing extension-versus-
hybrid comparison:** the independent final claim20 review accepted selection and all four
breadth perspectives after the SQRT bridge-cost correction. Earlier direction
review accepted the service/lifetime/drain composition; independent reviews own
claims56–59 and the fresh primary/replica evidence. The twelve-question inventory
and claim20 identify every selected owner and replacement binding. No additional
architecture-invalidating issue or unowned question remains in that review.

- **Product:** retain the selected virtual CPU, direct-boot Linux/SMP machine,
  required devices and startup32. Native eligibility does not narrow guest ISA.
  x87 binary64 values can lose cancellation residuals retained by full80; lazy
  C1 preserves the selected status-provider contract, not Intel transcendental
  equivalence. Q-00-02 owns application compatibility/tolerance decisions.
- **Architecture/correctness:** current-root maps, staged effects and permanent
  suffix handlers preserve queued fault precision, remap obligations and bounded
  references. Actual emitter/service/loader/device/containment conformance remains
  implementation validation; a failed named platform or construction premise
  reopens the dependent choice. Exact Intel edge details and M2 applicability
  remain disclosed within their existing owners.
- **Methodology/performance:** accepted finite matched observations support the
  conditional direction. Q-04-01/05-01 own workload frequency, guard/recovery/
  service/compilation costs and qualified full-system baselines; Q-05-02 owns M2
  expansion. No NOP-subtracted score, arbitrary workload weighting or universal
  winner is inferred. Frequent service or C1 observation and low code reuse
  can erase the measured advantages.
- **Economic decision:** Q-06-03/06 own dual-compiler maintenance, lifecycle and
  adaptation budgets; Q-00-01/03/04 own workload, baseline and success inputs.
  Synthesis selects stop, targeted research or authorization of planning using
  those records. Track03 completion itself authorizes neither planning nor code.

are all charged together; no unused allowance creates another assignment.
